diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/design.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/design.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..05bd1f87
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/design.md
@@ -0,0 +1,138 @@
+## Context
+
+The pipeline inspection architecture requires extractors to persist a bounded, self-contained evidence set at extraction time — no live CI fetch is allowed after extraction. Currently the ADO extractor plans all task logs (failed and successful) as raw HTTP capture items under `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt`, derives normalized `logs/{parent_id}/{task_id}.log` and `logs/{parent_id}.log`, and can remove raw provider log text after normalization when `keep_provider_raw_logs=false`. This reduces retained duplication but does not solve the more important problem: extraction still downloads and temporarily accounts for unrelated successful logs before relevance is known. Large ADO task logs can exhaust the 400 MiB per-inspection cap or make LLM retrieval noisy.
+
+Jenkins extraction already uses a more targeted approach for log capture. The new reader/MCP work also gives unified consumers log inventory, log metadata, log-by-ref streaming, normalized test reports, artifact entries with attribution, Jenkins job-layer markers, and ADO runtime/changeset provider extensions. This change should use those surfaces instead of introducing provider-specific reader paths.
+
+The consumer-facing layout (`execution_timeline.json`, `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `steps/*.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, `artifacts.json`, `tests.json`, `logs/*.log`) is provider-neutral and must remain identical across Jenkins and ADO.
+
+## Goals / Non-Goals
+
+**Goals:**
+
+- Protect required unified JSON files from being blocked by large log retention
+- Capture LLM-useful failure evidence bounded by configurable per-log and per-inspection budgets
+- Maintain identical consumer-facing normalized layout across Jenkins and ADO
+- Provide deterministic, structure-preserving log truncation for large selected logs
+- Enable guided evidence discovery through the existing `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, `get_log_by_ref`, `get_test_report`, artifact-entry, and execution-timeline surfaces, plus new failure brief/index surfaces
+- Keep the implementation backward-compatible with existing inspections
+
+**Non-Goals:**
+
+- Adding live CI fetch to handlers or finalizers
+- Making provider raw logs part of the primary LLM retrieval contract
+- Storing all successful task logs for archival completeness
+- Changing the meaning of `Step.result` or `Step.log_ref` semantics
+- Provider-specific branching in downstream LLM handlers
+- Replacing the current unified log endpoints that already read `logs/...` by exact reference
+
+## Decisions
+
+### 1. Selective capture over bulk download
+
+**Decision**: Replace "download all ADO task logs" with explicit selection based on timeline record signals (failed, canceled, succeededWithIssues, errorCount > 0, error issues, sub-timeline failures).
+
+**Rationale**: The inspection purpose is failure analysis, not archival. Unrelated successful task logs add no analytical value but consume 60-90% of cap in typical large ADO builds.
+
+**Alternatives considered**:
+- *Cap-based proportional shrinking*: Download all, shrink proportionally. Rejected — still downloads everything, and proportional shrinking loses strong signals.
+- *Download-then-delete*: Download all, then delete successful. Rejected — wastes bandwidth and temporary storage for logs that are never useful.
+
+### 2. Relevance-aware truncation with prioritized windows
+
+**Decision**: For selected logs above a configurable threshold (default 64 MiB), retain head + error-marker windows + tail with explicit omission markers. Windows are prioritized: timeline issue messages > `##[error]` > generic markers.
+
+**Rationale**: Fixed head+tail misses root causes in the middle of logs (dependency errors, compiler failures, retry exhaustion). Relevance-aware windows retain the diagnostically useful sections.
+
+**Alternatives considered**:
+- *Fixed head+tail only*: Simple but loses mid-log root causes.
+- *LLM-based summarization at extraction*: Too slow, non-deterministic, and adds LLM dependency to the critical extraction path.
+- *Line-count based*: Byte-based is more predictable for cap accounting.
+
+### 3. Selected normalized writes replace raw-log-first capture
+
+**Decision**: Replace the current raw-log-first ADO flow with selected normalized capture. Selected ADO logs are retained at `logs/{job_id}/{task_id}.log` and referenced by `Step.log_ref`. The capture path must not depend on retaining `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt` before `manifest.json`; local scratch files are allowed during extraction but are not part of HDLF retention.
+
+**Rationale**: Deleting raw provider logs after normalization reduces final snapshot size, but it still downloads every task log and keeps the raw log path in the critical extraction flow. The normalized path is the only one consumers use, and selection must happen before large unrelated logs consume extraction budget.
+
+**Alternatives considered**:
+- *Keep raw, derive normalized, then delete raw*: This matches the current transitional baseline, but it still downloads and temporarily handles every task log.
+- *Raw-only, on-demand normalize*: Violates the "no post-extraction processing" contract.
+
+### 4. Bounded evidence bundles for stage/job logs
+
+**Decision**: `logs/{job_id}.log` becomes a sectioned narrative: one header per step (with result + selection reason), inlined evidence for selected steps, explicit "omitted" markers for unselected steps.
+
+**Rationale**: LLMs need a top-down failure story. Blind concatenation of all task logs produces unreadable multi-hundred-MiB files. A sectioned bundle lets the LLM read the narrative, then drill into exact step logs via `Step.log_ref`.
+
+### 5. Structured manifest metadata
+
+**Decision**: Extend `DownloadResult` and manifest items with log metadata fields: `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, `retained_windows`, `capture_state`. The fields remain optional when parsing older manifests, but new normalized log entries must populate the applicable fields.
+
+**Rationale**: Reader evidence surfaces (`list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, `get_failure_evidence_index`) need cheap metadata lookup for selection/truncation details. Path inference remains the compatibility fallback for older manifests, but new manifests should not require readers to guess why a log was retained.
+
+### 6. Layered reader/MCP surfaces
+
+**Decision**: Treat the existing normalized traversal methods as the base contract: `get_execution_timeline`, `get_test_report`, `list_artifacts`/artifact entries, `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, and `get_log_by_ref`. Add `get_failure_brief` and `get_failure_evidence_index` as guided aggregate views over those files. Any future exact/fallback convenience tools must delegate to `Step.log_ref`, `list_logs`, and `get_log_by_ref` rather than inventing provider-specific paths.
+
+**Rationale**: The new log inventory/by-ref APIs already prevent inaccessible normalized logs. The remaining gap is not raw access; it is ordering. LLMs should read structured run, timeline, test, issue, artifact, and log metadata first, then open exact log refs only where the metadata points.
+
+### 7. Excerpt builder as a pure, provider-agnostic module
+
+**Decision**: The truncation engine (`log_excerpt.py`) takes a local file path, markers, and byte budgets — it knows nothing about HDLF, ADO, or network. Only the capture module orchestrates download, scratch, and HDLF write.
+
+**Rationale**: Testability, reusability across providers, and clear separation of concerns. Jenkins could use the same excerpt builder if Jenkins logs grow large in the future.
+
+## LLM Reading Flow
+
+The efficient reader flow is top-down and provider-neutral:
+
+1. Read `get_pipeline_run` / `pipeline_run.json` for result, trigger, branch, parameters, ADO captured variables ref, ADO changesets, Jenkins runtime-environment availability, and provider extensions.
+2. Read `get_execution_timeline` for failed stages, failed or suspicious steps, `Step.result`, `Step.log_ref`, Jenkins `provider_extensions.job_layer`, artifact attribution, jobs, parallel groups, and stage failure messages.
+3. Read `get_test_report` for failed, errored, skipped, or aborted test evidence before opening logs. Jenkins reports include `provider_extensions.aborted_outcome_supported=false`; ADO can represent aborted/error outcomes from the Test Results API.
+4. Read `list_artifacts` or artifact entries only when artifacts affect failure diagnosis. Check status and attribution confidence before opening artifact bytes.
+5. Read `list_logs` and `get_log_metadata` to discover which normalized logs exist, which planned logs were skipped, and which logs are full versus excerpted/truncated.
+6. Open logs by exact `Step.log_ref` or `list_logs[*].log_ref` through `get_log_by_ref`. Do not construct provider-raw paths and do not request live CI data.
+7. Use the job/stage bundle `logs/{stage_or_job_id}.log` for the narrative, then drill into `logs/{stage_or_job_id}/{step_id}.log` for exact step evidence when available.
+
+Failure brief and evidence index are aggregate accelerators over this same data. They must not be a separate source of truth and must not hide any normalized log that `list_logs` can reach.
+
+## Risks / Trade-offs
+
+- **[Risk] Error markers miss subtle root causes** → Mitigation: High default full-threshold (64 MiB) means most failure logs are stored complete; only truly massive logs use windowed excerpts. Timeline issue messages are matched first for strongest signal.
+
+- **[Risk] Adaptive compaction underestimates required JSON size** → Mitigation: Reserve bytes for required unified files before log capture begins. Required files are written first in extraction ordering.
+
+- **[Risk] Old inspections lack manifest metadata** → Mitigation: Reader methods fall back to path parsing and existing manifest fields. New metadata is mandatory for new normalized log entries, but older snapshots remain readable.
+
+- **[Risk] MCP response limits destroy excerpt structure** → Mitigation: Structure-preserving MCP truncation that keeps header, strongest windows, and tail rather than blind tail-only return.
+
+- **[Trade-off] No archival of unrelated successful logs** → Accepted: The inspection purpose is failure analysis, and the architecture does not rely on live CI fetch after extraction. Successful logs are retained only when selected as bounded context during extraction; otherwise they are intentionally outside the inspection evidence set.
+
+- **[Trade-off] Local scratch files during extraction** → Accepted: Temporary disk use during extraction is architecturally permitted; only HDLF retention and cap accounting matter for the snapshot contract.
+
+## Migration Plan
+
+1. **Phase 1 — Models and readers (partially present in the worktree)**
+   - Add optional manifest metadata fields to `DownloadResult` and `ManifestItem`
+   - Keep the existing `hdlf_server.models.LogInventoryEntry` and `LogMetadata` for log inventory/by-ref responses unless a shared-model move is required by another OpenSpec
+   - Add only the missing failure brief/index models; do not duplicate the existing log inventory models
+   - Reader methods for `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, and `stream_log_by_ref` already exist and must be preserved with fallback inference
+
+2. **Phase 2 — HTTP and MCP surfaces (partially present in the worktree)**
+   - Preserve existing HTTP/MCP endpoints for log inventory, log metadata, log-by-ref reads, normalized test reports, and artifact entries
+   - Add only the missing failure brief/evidence-index endpoints and tools
+   - Update tool docstrings to recommend the top-down reading flow above
+
+3. **Phase 3 — ADO extraction refactor**
+   - Add log selection, excerpt builder, capture, and bundle modules
+   - Integrate into ADO extractor; stop bulk log download
+   - Jenkins manifest metadata enrichment
+
+4. **Rollback**: Each phase is independently deployable. Phase 3 can be reverted to restore bulk download without affecting Phase 1/2 readers (they fall back gracefully).
+
+## Resolved Policy Points
+
+- The full-log threshold and evidence budgets are configurable through `DataExtractorSettings` with conservative defaults.
+- Context logs are attempted as Tier 2 evidence and omitted first under cap pressure; they are not a prerequisite for inspection completion.
+- `capture_state` stays in manifest/reader evidence metadata. It is not added to `Step`, preserving `Step` as provider-neutral execution metadata.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/proposal.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/proposal.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..cc9798eb
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/proposal.md
@@ -0,0 +1,41 @@
+## Why
+
+Azure DevOps task logs can fill almost the entire 400 MiB inspection cap before required unified files (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `jobs.json`) are written. The current ADO extractor still plans raw task-log downloads under `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt`, derives normalized logs under `logs/`, and then can delete the raw provider log text when `keep_provider_raw_logs=false`. That cleanup reduces retained duplication, but the extraction path still downloads every task log before it knows which evidence is useful. Large inspections can still spend bandwidth, temporary space, and cap accounting on unrelated successful logs, while LLM handlers need compact failure evidence in the normalized layout.
+
+The architecture requires the inspection snapshot to be self-contained — no live CI fetch after extraction. We need bounded, LLM-useful failure evidence that protects required unified files while preserving the same consumer-facing layout across Jenkins and ADO.
+
+## What Changes
+
+- **Replace "download all ADO task logs" with selective evidence capture**: retain failed, canceled, abandoned, `succeededWithIssues`, `errorCount > 0`, timeline error-issue, failed sub-timeline, job-failure-without-failed-task, and bounded context logs.
+- **Add relevance-aware truncation for large selected logs**: retain head, error-marker windows, and tail instead of fixed head+tail clipping.
+- **Replace raw-log-first ADO capture with normalized selected capture**: selected ADO log bodies are written to normalized `logs/...` paths without using `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt` as the primary pre-manifest staging location.
+- **Generate bounded stage/job evidence bundles**: `logs/{job_id}.log` becomes a sectioned narrative rather than blind concatenation.
+- **Add structured manifest metadata for log entries**: `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, `retained_windows`.
+- **Build on the new reader/MCP/HTTP log surfaces**: `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, and `get_log_by_ref` already provide exact normalized log traversal; this change adds richer metadata plus failure brief/evidence-index flows on top.
+- **Emit Jenkins manifest metadata** for normalized logs to maintain provider parity.
+- **Preserve the new normalized context fields**: ADO `runtime_environment` availability, captured variables ref, source commit changesets, Jenkins job-layer markers, split Jenkins JUnit suites, and artifact attribution remain part of the first-pass reading context before logs are opened.
+
+## Capabilities
+
+### New Capabilities
+
+- `ado-log-selection`: ADO timeline-based log candidate classification with selection reasons and evidence budgets.
+- `log-excerpt-builder`: Provider-agnostic relevance-aware truncation engine (head, error windows, tail, omission markers).
+- `ado-log-capture`: ADO-specific log capture pipeline writing selected logs directly to normalized paths with adaptive compaction.
+- `evidence-bundle-builder`: Bounded stage/job evidence bundle generation from selected step evidence.
+- `failure-evidence-reader`: Provider-neutral reader and MCP surfaces for failure brief and evidence index, extending the existing log inventory, log metadata, and log-by-ref streaming surfaces.
+
+### Modified Capabilities
+
+- `ingestion-api`: Manifest model gains optional structured log metadata fields; ADO extraction ordering changes from bulk download to selective capture.
+
+## Impact
+
+- **Data extractor (ADO)**: Major refactor of log download and normalization flow; new modules for selection, capture, excerpt, and bundle building.
+- **Data extractor (Jenkins)**: Minor — emit new manifest metadata fields for existing normalized logs.
+- **Manifest/result models**: Extended with optional log metadata fields (backward-compatible).
+- **Reader (`fl_shared` / `hdlf_server`)**: Existing normalized log inventory/by-ref methods are retained and enriched; new failure evidence models are added where they do not already exist.
+- **MCP server**: Existing `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, `get_log_by_ref`, and `get_test_report` tools become the foundation for guided evidence flow.
+- **HDLF server**: Existing unified log endpoints are retained; failure brief/evidence-index endpoints and enriched manifest metadata are added.
+- **Configuration**: New `DataExtractorSettings` fields for ADO evidence budgets and thresholds.
+- **Downstream consumers**: No breaking changes — same normalized layout, same `Step.log_ref` semantics.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-capture/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-capture/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..219f7216
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-capture/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Selected normalized path writes
+
+The ADO log capture module SHALL write selected logs to normalized `logs/{parent_id}/{step_id}.log` paths and use those paths as the retained evidence contract. It SHALL replace the current raw-log-first flow that writes `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt`, derives normalized logs, and then optionally deletes the raw provider log text.
+
+#### Scenario: Selected step log written to normalized path
+
+- **WHEN** a log candidate with `kind="step_log"` is captured
+- **THEN** the system SHALL write it to `logs/{parent_id}/{step_id}.log`
+
+#### Scenario: No provider raw log dependency
+
+- **WHEN** ADO extraction captures selected logs
+- **THEN** normalized log availability SHALL NOT depend on a retained file under `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/`
+- **AND** provider-raw log bodies SHALL NOT be retained in HDLF as a duplicate of normalized log evidence unless a debug-only configuration explicitly enables that retention
+
+### Requirement: Full or excerpted capture based on threshold
+
+The capture module SHALL store selected logs in full when they are below the full-threshold and as excerpts when above. When the provider does not expose content length before download, the module SHALL stream to local scratch, determine size during capture, and then choose full or excerpted normalized output before writing to HDLF.
+
+#### Scenario: Small selected log stored complete
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log's content-length is at or below `ado_full_log_threshold`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL write it complete with manifest `status="captured"`
+
+#### Scenario: Large selected log stored as excerpt
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log's content-length exceeds `ado_full_log_threshold`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL write a relevance-aware excerpt with manifest `status="truncated"`
+
+#### Scenario: Unknown-size selected log below threshold
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log has no content-length and the streamed scratch file size is at or below `ado_full_log_threshold`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL write the scratch content complete to the normalized log path with manifest `status="captured"`
+
+#### Scenario: Unknown-size selected log above threshold
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log has no content-length and the streamed scratch file size exceeds `ado_full_log_threshold`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL generate a relevance-aware excerpt from the scratch file and write it with manifest `status="truncated"`
+
+### Requirement: Adaptive compaction under cap pressure
+
+The capture module SHALL reduce storage when projected captured bytes approach the inspection cap, following a defined priority order.
+
+#### Scenario: Context logs omitted first
+
+- **WHEN** projected bytes exceed the inspection cap after required JSON reservation
+- **THEN** context candidates (priority 5) SHALL be omitted before reducing primary evidence budgets
+
+#### Scenario: Excerpt caps reduced before skipping primary logs
+
+- **WHEN** omitting context logs is insufficient
+- **THEN** the system SHALL lower `excerpt_cap` toward `min_excerpt_cap` for primary candidates before skipping them
+
+#### Scenario: Remaining cap smaller than generated excerpt
+
+- **WHEN** a generated excerpt would exceed the remaining inspection cap
+- **THEN** the system SHALL regenerate the excerpt using a smaller cap down to `min_excerpt_cap` before marking the primary candidate skipped
+
+### Requirement: Local scratch file lifecycle
+
+The capture module MAY use local scratch files for streaming and excerpt generation. Scratch files SHALL be cleaned in `finally` blocks and SHALL NOT appear in HDLF.
+
+#### Scenario: Scratch file cleaned after capture
+
+- **WHEN** a log is downloaded to local scratch for excerpt generation
+- **THEN** the scratch file SHALL be deleted after the normalized log is written to HDLF, regardless of success or failure
+
+### Requirement: Manifest metadata for captured logs
+
+Each captured log SHALL produce a `DownloadResult` with structured metadata fields: `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, `retained_windows`, and `capture_state`.
+
+#### Scenario: Captured log manifest entry
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log is successfully written
+- **THEN** the `DownloadResult` SHALL include `kind`, `stage_id`, `selection_reason`, and `capture_state` fields
+
+### Requirement: Step log_ref consistency
+
+After log capture, `Step.log_ref` values SHALL point only to normalized logs that actually exist in HDLF. Steps whose logs were not selected SHALL have `log_ref = null`.
+
+#### Scenario: Unselected step has null log_ref
+
+- **WHEN** a step's task log was not selected for capture
+- **THEN** `Step.log_ref` SHALL be `null`
+
+#### Scenario: Selected step has valid log_ref
+
+- **WHEN** a step's task log was selected and successfully captured
+- **THEN** `Step.log_ref` SHALL point to the existing normalized log path
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-selection/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-selection/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..84cdc7e4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ado-log-selection/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,97 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: ADO log candidate classification
+
+The system SHALL classify ADO timeline records into log candidates based on record result and structured issues. Each candidate SHALL carry a `selection_reason`, evidence budget (`excerpt_cap`), full-threshold, minimum excerpt cap, and priority.
+
+#### Scenario: Failed task is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `result == "failed"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_failed"` and priority 0
+
+#### Scenario: Canceled task is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `result == "canceled"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_canceled"` and priority 0
+
+#### Scenario: Abandoned task is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `result == "abandoned"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_abandoned"` and priority 0
+
+#### Scenario: SucceededWithIssues task is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `result == "succeededWithIssues"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_succeeded_with_issues"` and priority 2
+
+#### Scenario: Task with error count is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `errorCount > 0`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_error_count"` and priority 1
+
+#### Scenario: Task with error issue is selected
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has at least one issue with `type == "error"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="task_error_issue"` and priority 1
+
+#### Scenario: Failed sub-timeline task is selected
+
+- **WHEN** a task record in a failed deployment or matrix sub-timeline has a failure signal
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="failed_sub_timeline_task"` and priority 3
+
+#### Scenario: Job failed without failed task and has log
+
+- **WHEN** a job record has `result == "failed"`, has a log reference, and no child task has a failure signal
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="job_failed_without_failed_task"` and priority 4
+
+#### Scenario: Job failed without failed task and without log
+
+- **WHEN** a job record has `result == "failed"`, has no log reference, and no child task has a failure signal
+- **THEN** the system SHALL NOT produce a log candidate for the job record
+- **AND** the evidence bundle builder SHALL still be able to generate a metadata-only job bundle from the job and child task summaries
+
+### Requirement: Context log selection
+
+The system SHALL select context logs only after primary candidates are identified. Context candidates SHALL have lower priority than all primary candidates.
+
+#### Scenario: Predecessor task selected as context
+
+- **WHEN** a primary failed task has a direct predecessor sibling task with a log
+- **THEN** the system MAY produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="context_predecessor"` and priority 5
+
+#### Scenario: Setup task selected as context
+
+- **WHEN** the job containing a primary failure has a checkout or dependency-install task
+- **THEN** the system MAY produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="context_setup"` and priority 5
+
+#### Scenario: Test report task selected as context
+
+- **WHEN** the job containing a primary test failure has a test-report-publish task
+- **THEN** the system MAY produce a log candidate with `selection_reason="context_test_report"` and priority 5
+
+### Requirement: Deduplication by log ID
+
+The system SHALL deduplicate selected candidates by ADO `log_id`. When the same log is selected for multiple reasons, the highest-priority (lowest numeric value) reason SHALL be kept.
+
+#### Scenario: Same log selected twice
+
+- **WHEN** a task log qualifies as both `task_failed` and `task_error_issue`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce exactly one candidate with `selection_reason="task_failed"` (priority 0)
+
+### Requirement: Successful unrelated tasks not selected
+
+The system SHALL NOT select task logs for records that have `result == "succeeded"` and no error signals, unless they qualify as context logs.
+
+#### Scenario: Successful task without errors
+
+- **WHEN** an ADO timeline task record has `result == "succeeded"` and `errorCount == 0` and no error issues
+- **THEN** the system SHALL NOT produce a log candidate for that record
+
+### Requirement: Issue markers attached to candidates
+
+The system SHALL attach ADO timeline issue messages to each candidate record. These markers SHALL be used by the excerpt builder for relevance-aware windowing.
+
+#### Scenario: Candidate with timeline issues
+
+- **WHEN** a selected task record has timeline issues with messages
+- **THEN** the candidate SHALL carry those messages in its `issue_markers` field
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/evidence-bundle-builder/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/evidence-bundle-builder/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..9c9b1b86
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/evidence-bundle-builder/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,53 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Sectioned evidence bundle format
+
+The stage/job evidence bundle at `logs/{stage_or_job_id}.log` SHALL be a sectioned narrative with one header per step in timeline order, containing inlined evidence for selected steps and explicit omission markers for unselected steps. For ADO the owner is normally a Job record; for Jenkins the owner is a Stage record.
+
+#### Scenario: Bundle contains all step headers
+
+- **WHEN** a stage/job evidence bundle is generated
+- **THEN** it SHALL include a section header for every step under that stage or job, regardless of selection status
+
+#### Scenario: Selected failed step inlined
+
+- **WHEN** a step was selected with a failure reason and its captured body is available
+- **THEN** the bundle SHALL inline the step's evidence (full or snippet) under its section header
+
+#### Scenario: Unselected step shows omission
+
+- **WHEN** a step was not selected for log capture
+- **THEN** the bundle section header SHALL indicate `log omitted: not selected`
+
+### Requirement: Bundle section header format
+
+Each step section header SHALL include step_id, display name, result, log_ref (when available), and selection_reason (when available).
+
+#### Scenario: Section header content
+
+- **WHEN** a step section is emitted in the bundle
+- **THEN** the header SHALL include `step_id=<id>`, `name=<display_name>`, `result=<RESULT>`, `log_ref=<path-or-null>`, and `selection_reason=<reason-or-none>`
+- **AND** the header SHALL follow a stable section format such as: `===== Step: <name> | step_id=<id> | result=<RESULT> | log_ref=<path-or-null> | selection_reason=<reason-or-none> | <status> =====`
+
+### Requirement: Bundle budget enforcement
+
+The evidence bundle SHALL respect `ado_job_bundle_cap` (default 16 MiB). Under adaptive compaction, it SHALL shrink to `ado_job_bundle_min_excerpt` (default 2 MiB).
+
+#### Scenario: Bundle within budget
+
+- **WHEN** total inlined evidence fits within `ado_job_bundle_cap`
+- **THEN** all selected step evidence SHALL be inlined in full
+
+#### Scenario: Bundle exceeds budget
+
+- **WHEN** total inlined evidence would exceed the bundle cap
+- **THEN** the system SHALL inline compact snippets for large failed step logs and point to exact `Step.log_ref` for full evidence
+
+### Requirement: Priority-based section shrinking
+
+When the bundle must shrink, failed and suspicious step sections SHALL be preserved before successful context sections.
+
+#### Scenario: Context sections trimmed first
+
+- **WHEN** the bundle must be reduced to fit the budget
+- **THEN** context step sections (selection_reason starting with "context_") SHALL be trimmed or omitted before failed step sections
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/failure-evidence-reader/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/failure-evidence-reader/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..3e301021
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/failure-evidence-reader/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,147 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Failure brief
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_failure_brief` reader method, MCP tool, and HTTP endpoint that returns a deterministic summary of the pipeline failure assembled from normalized files.
+
+#### Scenario: Failure brief content
+
+- **WHEN** `get_failure_brief` is called for an inspection
+- **THEN** it SHALL return `pipeline_result`, `pipeline_state`, `failed_stage_count`, `failed_step_count`, `primary_failure_stage_ids`, `primary_failure_step_ids`, `issue_messages`, `failed_tests_count`, and `recommended_read_order`
+
+#### Scenario: Successful pipeline brief
+
+- **WHEN** `get_failure_brief` is called for an inspection with no failures
+- **THEN** `failed_stage_count` and `failed_step_count` SHALL be 0 and `recommended_read_order` SHALL be minimal
+
+### Requirement: Failure evidence index
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_failure_evidence_index` reader method, MCP tool, and HTTP endpoint that returns failed/suspicious stages with their steps, logs, truncation status, related tests, and related artifacts.
+
+#### Scenario: Evidence index for failed stage
+
+- **WHEN** `get_failure_evidence_index` is called and a stage has `result == "FAILURE"`
+- **THEN** the returned index SHALL include that stage with its steps, `stage_log_ref`, `stage_log_summary`, step-level `log_ref`, `log_status`, `selection_reason`, and `truncation_strategy`
+
+#### Scenario: Evidence index surfaces truncation
+
+- **WHEN** a step log was stored as an excerpt
+- **THEN** the evidence index entry for that step SHALL show `log_status="truncated"` and the `truncation_strategy` used
+
+### Requirement: Log inventory
+
+The system SHALL provide a `list_logs` reader method, MCP tool, and HTTP endpoint that returns every normalized log under `logs/` with metadata.
+
+#### Scenario: All normalized logs listed
+
+- **WHEN** `list_logs` is called
+- **THEN** it SHALL return an entry for every normalized log under `logs/` present in the manifest or discovered by scanning, including `log_ref`, `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `status`, `source`, `original_size`, and `captured_size`
+- **AND** entries for skipped or non-captured planned logs SHALL remain visible through their `status` rather than being silently dropped
+
+#### Scenario: Path-derived metadata
+
+- **WHEN** a manifest log entry lacks enriched metadata fields
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL derive `kind`, `stage_id`, and `step_id` from `logs/<stage_id>.log` or `logs/<stage_id>/<step_id>.log`
+
+#### Scenario: Fallback for old inspections
+
+- **WHEN** `list_logs` is called on an inspection without enriched manifest metadata
+- **THEN** the system SHALL fall back to path parsing and existing manifest fields to construct inventory entries
+
+### Requirement: Log metadata
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_log_metadata` reader method, MCP tool, and HTTP endpoint that returns detailed metadata for a single log reference.
+
+#### Scenario: Metadata for existing log
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_metadata` is called with a valid `log_ref` that exists
+- **THEN** it SHALL return `log_ref`, `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `status`, `source`, `original_size`, and `captured_size`
+- **AND** it SHALL include `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, and `retained_windows` when those fields are present in the manifest
+
+#### Scenario: Metadata for known missing log
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_metadata` is called with a `log_ref` that appears in the manifest or log inventory with a skipped/non-captured status
+- **THEN** it SHALL return metadata for that `log_ref` with its skipped/non-captured `status`
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT perform any live CI lookup
+
+#### Scenario: Metadata for unknown log reference
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_metadata` is called with a syntactically valid `log_ref` that is not present in the manifest, log inventory, or any `Step.log_ref`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL return a not-found response rather than treating the path as a known missing log
+
+### Requirement: Log-by-ref reading
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_log_by_ref` reader method, MCP tool, and HTTP endpoint that reads a normalized log by its exact reference path.
+
+#### Scenario: Valid log_ref read
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_by_ref` is called with a valid `log_ref` starting with `logs/`
+- **THEN** it SHALL return the stored log content
+
+#### Scenario: Supported normalized log_ref shapes
+
+- **WHEN** a log reference is accepted by metadata or streaming methods
+- **THEN** it SHALL have the shape `logs/<stage_or_job_id>.log` or `logs/<stage_or_job_id>/<step_id>.log`
+
+#### Scenario: Unknown log_ref read
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_by_ref` is called with a syntactically valid `log_ref` that is not present in the log inventory and does not exist in HDLF
+- **THEN** it SHALL return a not-found response without attempting live CI lookup
+
+#### Scenario: Path traversal rejected
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_by_ref` is called with a `log_ref` containing `..`, backslashes, NUL bytes, or not starting with `logs/`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL reject the request with a validation error
+
+#### Scenario: Encoded path traversal rejected
+
+- **WHEN** `get_log_by_ref` is called with a percent-encoded value that decodes to an invalid path or provider-raw path
+- **THEN** the system SHALL reject the request with a validation error
+
+#### Scenario: Structure-preserving MCP truncation
+
+- **WHEN** the stored excerpt exceeds the MCP response budget
+- **THEN** the MCP tool SHALL preserve the `[LOG EXCERPT]` header, strongest `[WINDOW]` sections, and `[TAIL]` rather than returning only the tail
+
+### Requirement: Exact step log reading
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_step_log_exact` tool that reads only the exact step-level log without fallback.
+
+#### Scenario: Exact log exists
+
+- **WHEN** `get_step_log_exact` is called and the step log exists
+- **THEN** it SHALL return the step log content
+
+#### Scenario: Exact log missing
+
+- **WHEN** `get_step_log_exact` is called and the step log does not exist
+- **THEN** it SHALL return an explicit "not found" response without falling back to the stage bundle
+
+### Requirement: Best-available log reading
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_best_available_log_for_step` tool that tries the exact step log first, then falls back to the stage/job bundle with an explicit note about which source was returned.
+
+#### Scenario: Exact log available
+
+- **WHEN** `get_best_available_log_for_step` is called and the exact step log exists
+- **THEN** it SHALL return the step log content with a note indicating the source is the exact step log
+
+#### Scenario: Fallback to stage bundle
+
+- **WHEN** `get_best_available_log_for_step` is called and the exact step log does not exist but the stage bundle does
+- **THEN** it SHALL return the stage bundle content with an explicit note indicating fallback to stage bundle
+
+### Requirement: HTTP API endpoints
+
+The system SHALL expose evidence discovery through HTTP endpoints at `hdlf_server`:
+- `GET /api/v1/inspection/{id}/unified/failure-brief`
+- `GET /api/v1/inspection/{id}/unified/failure-evidence-index`
+- `GET /api/v1/inspection/{id}/unified/logs`
+- `GET /api/v1/inspection/{id}/unified/log-metadata?log_ref=logs/...`
+- `GET /api/v1/inspection/{id}/unified/log-by-ref?log_ref=logs/...`
+
+#### Scenario: HTTP endpoint returns typed evidence models
+
+- **WHEN** any evidence HTTP endpoint is called
+- **THEN** log inventory and log metadata responses SHALL use the existing typed log models exposed by `hdlf_server.models`
+- **AND** failure brief and evidence-index responses SHALL use typed Pydantic models rather than unstructured dictionaries
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md

+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,54 @@
+## MODIFIED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Manifest item model
+
+The manifest item model SHALL support optional structured log metadata fields in addition to existing fields. All new fields SHALL be optional for backward compatibility with existing inspections.
+
+#### Scenario: New manifest fields for log entries
+
+- **WHEN** a normalized log entry is written during extraction
+- **THEN** the manifest item SHALL include `kind` (stage_log | step_log), `stage_id`, optional `step_id`, `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, `retained_windows`, and `capture_state`
+
+#### Scenario: Old manifest items still parse
+
+- **WHEN** a manifest from an older inspection lacks the new fields
+- **THEN** the system SHALL parse it without error using default `None` values for missing fields
+
+#### Scenario: Reader fallback before manifest enrichment
+
+- **WHEN** a new or old manifest lacks structured log metadata for a normalized `logs/...` item
+- **THEN** reader methods SHALL still expose that log through path-derived `kind`, `stage_id`, and `step_id`
+- **AND** enriched manifest metadata SHALL be treated as an optimization for selection/truncation details, not as a prerequisite for log accessibility
+
+### Requirement: ADO extraction ordering
+
+ADO extraction SHALL follow a defined ordering that protects required unified files from log cap pressure.
+
+#### Scenario: Required independent unified files written before selected log capture
+
+- **WHEN** ADO extraction runs
+- **THEN** required unified JSON files that do not depend on selected log existence (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, `artifacts.json`, `tests.json`) SHALL be written or have reserved space before selected log capture begins
+
+#### Scenario: Log-dependent unified files written after capture
+
+- **WHEN** ADO selected log capture completes
+- **THEN** `steps/*.json` and `execution_timeline.json` SHALL be generated with `Step.log_ref` values derived only from normalized logs that were actually written
+
+#### Scenario: Manifest written only after log refs validated
+
+- **WHEN** ADO extraction completes
+- **THEN** `manifest.json` SHALL only be written after all `Step.log_ref` values point to existing normalized logs
+
+### Requirement: Jenkins manifest metadata enrichment
+
+Jenkins normalized log entries SHALL emit structured manifest metadata matching the same schema as ADO, maintaining provider parity for reader surfaces.
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins stage log manifest entry
+
+- **WHEN** Jenkins extraction writes `logs/{stage_id}.log`
+- **THEN** the manifest entry SHALL include `kind="stage_log"`, `stage_id`, and `capture_state`
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins step log manifest entry
+
+- **WHEN** Jenkins extraction writes `logs/{stage_id}/{step_id}.log`
+- **THEN** the manifest entry SHALL include `kind="step_log"`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `selection_reason`, and `capture_state`
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/log-excerpt-builder/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/log-excerpt-builder/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..7e11d190
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/log-excerpt-builder/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,70 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Relevance-aware log truncation
+
+The system SHALL truncate logs above the configured full-threshold by retaining head, prioritized error-marker windows, and tail sections with explicit omission markers between retained regions.
+
+#### Scenario: Log below full threshold stored complete
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log's size is at or below the full-threshold (default 64 MiB)
+- **THEN** the system SHALL store the log in full without modification
+
+#### Scenario: Log above full threshold excerpted
+
+- **WHEN** a selected log's size exceeds the full-threshold
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce an excerpt containing head, error windows, and tail within the configured `excerpt_cap`
+
+### Requirement: Deterministic excerpt structure
+
+The excerpt builder SHALL produce deterministic output for the same input file and configuration. The output SHALL include a machine-readable header, retained sections, and omission markers.
+
+#### Scenario: Excerpt header format
+
+- **WHEN** an excerpt is generated
+- **THEN** the output SHALL begin with a `[LOG EXCERPT]` header containing `provider`, `record_id`, `record_name`, `selection_reason`, `strategy`, `original_size`, and `captured_size` fields
+
+#### Scenario: Omission markers between regions
+
+- **WHEN** content is omitted between retained regions
+- **THEN** the system SHALL emit an `[OMITTED <size>]` marker indicating the approximate bytes removed
+
+### Requirement: Error marker priority
+
+The system SHALL match markers in priority order: timeline issue messages first, then ADO-specific markers (`##[error]`, `##[warning]`), then generic markers (`error:`, `failed`, `exception`, `traceback`, `exit code`, `non-zero`).
+
+#### Scenario: Timeline issue outranks generic marker under budget pressure
+
+- **WHEN** the retained-window budget cannot fit all matched windows
+- **THEN** windows matching timeline issue messages SHALL be kept before windows matching generic markers
+
+#### Scenario: No markers found falls back to head plus tail
+
+- **WHEN** no error markers are found in a log above the full threshold
+- **THEN** the system SHALL produce an excerpt with head and tail only (no error windows)
+
+### Requirement: Window merging
+
+The system SHALL merge overlapping or adjacent retained windows before applying the final budget.
+
+#### Scenario: Overlapping windows merged
+
+- **WHEN** two matched marker windows overlap
+- **THEN** they SHALL be merged into a single contiguous window
+
+### Requirement: Line-boundary preference
+
+The system SHALL prefer line-boundary cuts when trimming retained regions.
+
+#### Scenario: Cut lands mid-line
+
+- **WHEN** a byte-boundary cut would split a line
+- **THEN** the system SHALL expand to the nearest line boundary or include a marker indicating the line was clipped
+
+### Requirement: Configurable budget split
+
+The excerpt builder SHALL accept configurable byte budgets for head, tail, and error windows. The error-window budget SHALL be shared across all matched windows.
+
+#### Scenario: Budget split for 8 MiB excerpt
+
+- **WHEN** a failed task log is excerpted with an 8 MiB cap
+- **THEN** the default split SHALL allocate approximately 1 MiB head, 2 MiB tail, and 5 MiB shared error windows
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/normalized-evidence-context/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/normalized-evidence-context/spec.md

index 00000000..9fb915ac
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/specs/normalized-evidence-context/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,43 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Structured context before log reads
+
+The evidence reader flow SHALL use normalized structured files before opening large logs so LLM consumers can spend log budget only where the timeline, tests, issues, artifacts, and provider extensions point.
+
+#### Scenario: Pipeline run context includes provider extensions
+
+- **WHEN** an LLM begins an inspection read
+- **THEN** it SHALL be able to inspect `pipeline_run.json` for provider-neutral run fields and provider extensions such as runtime-environment availability
+- **AND** Azure DevOps runs SHALL expose captured build-variable metadata through a provider-raw reference when available without persisting secret values
+- **AND** Azure DevOps runs SHALL expose captured source commit changesets when available
+
+#### Scenario: Execution timeline explains provider shape
+
+- **WHEN** an LLM reads `execution_timeline.json`
+- **THEN** Jenkins timelines SHALL explicitly indicate that the job layer is unavailable by provider design
+- **AND** Azure DevOps timelines SHALL expose job details from timeline Job records
+- **AND** both providers SHALL keep `Step.log_ref` as the only normalized pointer to exact step/task log evidence
+
+#### Scenario: Tests are normalized before log evidence
+
+- **WHEN** an inspection contains test data
+- **THEN** `get_test_report` SHALL expose normalized test runs and cases before the LLM opens logs
+- **AND** Jenkins reports SHALL indicate that aborted outcomes are not representable when the JUnit source only contains observed test cases
+- **AND** Azure DevOps reports SHALL preserve ADO outcomes such as failed, aborted, and errored where the Test Results API supplies them
+
+#### Scenario: Artifacts are discoverable with attribution
+
+- **WHEN** artifact evidence is needed
+- **THEN** artifact entries SHALL expose storage status and normalized attribution before artifact bytes are requested
+- **AND** Jenkins attribution SHALL be treated as confidence-bearing heuristic metadata
+- **AND** Azure DevOps API-source attribution SHALL be represented as high confidence when the source can be resolved to a producing job/task
+
+### Requirement: No provider-raw log dependency in reader flow
+
+The reader and MCP flow SHALL NOT require provider-raw log paths for normal failure analysis.
+
+#### Scenario: Normalized logs are the retrieval contract
+
+- **WHEN** an LLM needs log evidence
+- **THEN** it SHALL use `Step.log_ref`, `list_logs`, `get_log_metadata`, and `get_log_by_ref` over normalized `logs/...` paths
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT construct `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/*.txt` or Jenkins raw stage paths as the primary retrieval contract
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/tasks.md b/openspec/changes/ado-jenkins-log-retention-truncation/tasks.md

@@ -0,0 +1,98 @@
+## 1. Models and Configuration
+
+- [x] 1.1 Add ADO log evidence settings to `DataExtractorSettings` (`ado_full_log_threshold`, `ado_failed_log_excerpt_cap`, `ado_infra_log_excerpt_cap`, `ado_context_log_excerpt_cap`, `ado_job_bundle_cap`, `ado_failed_log_min_excerpt`, `ado_infra_log_min_excerpt`, `ado_job_bundle_min_excerpt`, `ado_window_before_bytes`, `ado_window_after_bytes`)
+- [x] 1.2 Extend `DownloadResult` with optional log metadata fields: `kind`, `stage_id`, `step_id`, `selection_reason`, `truncation_strategy`, `retained_windows`, `capture_state`
+- [x] 1.3 Update `hdlf_server/models.py` `ManifestItem` with matching optional fields and `extra="allow"`
+- [x] 1.4 Add only the missing aggregate evidence models (`EvidenceStep`, `EvidenceStage`, `FailureEvidenceIndex`, `FailureBrief`); keep the existing `hdlf_server.models.LogInventoryEntry` and `LogMetadata` unless a broader shared-model migration is requested
+- [x] 1.5 Add `SelectionReason` literal type and `AdoLogCandidate` model in `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/log_selection.py`
+
+## 2. Log Excerpt Builder
+
+- [x] 2.1 Create `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/log_excerpt.py` with `RetainedWindow`, `LogExcerptResult` models and `build_log_excerpt()` function
+- [x] 2.2 Implement marker scanning: timeline issue messages → ADO markers (`##[error]`, `##[warning]`) → generic markers (`error:`, `failed`, `exception`, `traceback`, `exit code`, `non-zero`)
+- [x] 2.3 Implement window merging and priority-based budget allocation
+- [x] 2.4 Implement line-boundary preference and omission marker emission
+- [x] 2.5 Add unit tests: marker-in-middle retained, no-marker fallback to head+tail, overlapping windows merged, priority ranking under budget pressure, deterministic output
+
+## 3. ADO Log Selection
+
+- [x] 3.1 Implement `build_ado_log_candidates()` in `log_selection.py` classifying timeline records by failure signals
+- [x] 3.2 Implement sub-timeline task selection for failed deployment/matrix records
+- [x] 3.3 Implement context log selection (predecessor, setup, test-report tasks)
+- [x] 3.4 Implement deduplication by `log_id` keeping highest-priority reason
+- [x] 3.5 Add unit tests: each selection reason classified correctly, successful tasks excluded, dedup by log_id, priority ordering, issue markers attached
+
+## 4. ADO Log Capture
+
+- [x] 4.1 Create `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/log_capture.py` with `capture_selected_ado_logs()` and `AdoLogCaptureResult` model
+- [x] 4.2 Implement full capture path (below threshold → write complete to HDLF with `status="captured"`)
+- [x] 4.3 Implement excerpt capture path (above threshold → local scratch → excerpt → write to HDLF with `status="truncated"`)
+- [x] 4.4 Implement adaptive compaction: context omission → excerpt cap reduction → bundle shrinking
+- [x] 4.5 Implement scratch file cleanup in `finally` blocks
+- [x] 4.6 Add unit tests: small log stored full, large log excerpted, adaptive compaction drops context first, scratch cleanup on failure

diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/design.md b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/design.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..e9e36c88
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/design.md
@@ -0,0 +1,72 @@
+## Context
+
+Azure DevOps pipelines use a hierarchical timeline model: **Stage → Job → Task**. For most jobs, Task records live in the primary timeline alongside their parent Job records, linked via `parent_id`. However, **deployment jobs and matrix expansions** create a separate sub-timeline — the primary timeline only contains the Job-level summary result, while individual Task records live in a different timeline referenced by the Job record's `details.id` field.
+
+The capture layer already:
+1. Detects Job records with `details` field and `result ∈ {failed, canceled, abandoned}` (`_sub_timeline_items()` in `capture_plan.py`)
+2. Fetches the sub-timeline via `client.get_sub_timeline(timeline_id)`
+3. Stores it at `provider_raw/azure_devops/sub_timelines/{record_id}.json`
+
+The normalization layer (`build_azure_devops_unified_items()`) produces `steps/<stage_id>.json` by calling `normalize_steps(stage_id, timeline)` — which only looks at the primary timeline's records. Sub-timeline files are never read back, so failed deployment jobs and matrix legs produce **zero steps** in the unified output.
+
+Jenkins handles the equivalent problem fully: `normalize_steps()` in `jenkins/steps.py` reads `stages/<name>/steps.json` (which contains per-stage Blue Ocean step data) and writes unified `steps/<stage_id>.json`. We need the same completeness for ADO.
+
+## Goals / Non-Goals
+
+**Goals:**
+- Produce `steps/<job_id>.json` entries for Job records whose Task-level detail lives in a sub-timeline
+- Reuse the same `Step` model and field-mapping helpers (`_map_record_result`, `_map_record_state`, `_iso_utc`) as the primary `normalize_steps()` 
+- Handle missing/corrupt sub-timeline data gracefully (warning + skip, no crash)
+- Work within the existing data-flow: sub-timeline content is already available in-memory during `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` because the capture plan writes it in the same execution pass
+
+**Non-Goals:**
+- Fetching sub-timelines for **succeeded** jobs (capture only fetches for failed results — this is intentional to avoid explosion for large matrices)
+- Supporting recursive sub-timelines (sub-timeline Tasks that themselves have a `details` field — not observed in practice)
+- Changing the sub-timeline capture policy (what to fetch and when) — that's already done and correct
+- Log retrieval from sub-timeline tasks (separate concern; `log_ref` will be `None` until log capture is extended)
+
+## Decisions
+
+### 1. Pass sub-timeline data through `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` rather than reading from storage
+
+**Decision:** Add a `sub_timelines: dict[str, RawAdoTimeline]` parameter to `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` where keys are Job record IDs and values are the parsed sub-timeline objects.
+
+**Rationale:** The sub-timelines are already in memory during the capture plan execution (they're returned from `_sub_timeline_items()` as `AzureDevOpsGeneratedJsonItem` objects with `.data` containing the raw dict). Passing them into the normalization function avoids a read-back from storage and keeps the normalization layer as a pure function of its inputs (no I/O). This matches how the primary `timeline` is already passed directly.
+
+**Alternative considered:** Having the normalizer read from HDLF/blob storage. Rejected because it introduces async I/O into an otherwise synchronous normalization layer and couples normalization to the storage backend.
+
+### 2. Separate helper function `normalize_sub_timeline_steps()` rather than modifying `normalize_steps()`
+
+**Decision:** Create `normalize_sub_timeline_steps(job_record_id, sub_timeline, parent_result, available_log_refs)` as a peer function to `normalize_steps()`.
+
+**Rationale:** The existing `normalize_steps()` operates on a `(stage_id, timeline)` pair and assumes the Tasks are direct children via `parent_id == stage_id`. Sub-timelines have a different internal structure — the Task records' `parent_id` points to an internal Phase/Job record within the sub-timeline, not to the original Job record in the primary timeline. A dedicated function handles this structural difference cleanly without adding conditional branches to the existing code.
+
+### 3. Filter to Task-type records within the sub-timeline, ignoring internal hierarchy
+
+**Decision:** Within a sub-timeline, collect all records with `type == "Task"` regardless of their `parent_id` value. Sort by `order`.
+
+**Rationale:** Sub-timelines have their own internal hierarchy (Phase → Job → Task), but from the unified schema's perspective, all Tasks in a sub-timeline belong to the parent Job record. The internal Phase/Job containers are structural scaffolding used by ADO's execution engine — they don't carry information the unified schema needs that isn't already on the parent Job record.
+
+### 4. Merge sub-timeline steps with any existing primary-timeline steps for the same Job
+
+**Decision:** If `normalize_steps()` already found Task children for a Job (from the primary timeline), sub-timeline Tasks are **appended** after them, maintaining the `order` field for sorting within each source.
+
+**Rationale:** In rare cases, a Job may have both direct Task children in the primary timeline (initialization tasks) and a sub-timeline (deployment tasks). Both should be visible. The primary-timeline tasks typically have lower `order` values (they run first), so appending sub-timeline tasks preserves chronological order.
+
+### 5. Conditional failure detection applies to sub-timeline tasks
+
+**Decision:** Apply the same `is_conditional_failure` logic to sub-timeline Tasks: a Task that failed while its parent Job (in the primary timeline) succeeded indicates a `condition: always()` execution that doesn't block the pipeline.
+
+**Rationale:** This is the existing contract of the `is_conditional_failure` field. Sub-timeline tasks are subject to the same ADO behaviors (continueOnError, conditional execution).
+
+**Note:** For sub-timeline steps, the parent result is the Job record's result in the primary timeline (already known), not the sub-timeline's internal Phase/Job result.
+
+## Risks / Trade-offs
+
+**[Sub-timeline internal hierarchy is complex]** → Mitigation: Flattening to all Task records with `type == "Task"` is conservative — it captures all execution steps. If a sub-timeline ever contains non-Task records that carry meaningful step-like information, those are lost. This is acceptable because the unified `Step` model maps 1:1 to ADO Task records specifically.
+
+**[No log refs for sub-timeline tasks initially]** → Mitigation: `log_ref` will be `None` for sub-timeline steps. The log capture extension is a separate follow-up concern. The unified reader and MCP tools already handle `log_ref: None` gracefully.
+
+**[Memory usage for large matrix expansions]** → Mitigation: Sub-timelines are only fetched for failed jobs. A matrix with 100 legs but only 2 failures produces only 2 sub-timelines in memory. The capture policy (already implemented) is the primary throttle.
+
+**[dict[str, RawAdoTimeline] parameter makes the function signature wider]** → Mitigation: Default to empty dict (`sub_timelines: dict[str, RawAdoTimeline] | None = None`) so existing callers are unaffected. Only the capture plan orchestrator needs to pass it.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/proposal.md b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/proposal.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..0b3538d8
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/proposal.md
@@ -0,0 +1,27 @@
+## Why
+
+Azure DevOps sub-timelines are fetched and stored as raw provider data at `provider_raw/azure_devops/sub_timelines/{record_id}.json`, but are never merged into the unified step-level representation (`steps/<stage_id>.json`). The spec (Bottleneck 9 in the ADO extractor spec) explicitly requires: _"For unified `steps/<stage_id>.json`, merge sub-timeline tasks into the step list"_, and task 7.8 is marked done — but the actual normalization code was never written. This means deployment jobs and matrix expansions that have sub-timelines show up as opaque job-level results with no step granularity, defeating the purpose of capturing them and creating a coverage gap relative to Jenkins (which already fully normalizes its equivalent `stages/<name>/steps.json`).
+
+## What Changes
+
+- **Add sub-timeline merge pass to `normalize_steps`**: After the main step normalization reads Task records from the primary timeline, a second pass reads stored sub-timeline files for failed Job-type records and merges their Task records into the unified `steps/<job_id>.json` output.
+- **Extend `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` signature**: Pass sub-timeline data (or a mapping of record_id → sub-timeline content) to the normalization layer so it can access sub-timeline Task records without separate I/O.
+- **New helper function `normalize_sub_timeline_steps()`**: Dedicated function that takes a raw sub-timeline, filters to Task-type records, and maps each to a `Step` using the same field-mapping logic as `normalize_steps()` (reusing `_map_record_result`, `_map_record_state`, etc.).
+- **Tests for sub-timeline step normalization**: Unit tests verifying Task-to-Step mapping, merge behavior with existing steps, graceful handling of missing/corrupt sub-timeline data, and matrix expansion (separate step files per leg).
+
+## Capabilities
+
+### New Capabilities
+
+- `ado-sub-timeline-step-normalization`: Normalizing ADO sub-timeline Task records into unified `steps/<stage_id>.json` — the missing bridge between raw sub-timeline capture and the unified pipeline schema.
+
+### Modified Capabilities
+
+_(none — this fills a gap in the existing ADO extractor implementation without changing any spec-level requirements)_
+
+## Impact
+
+- **Code**: `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/normalizer.py` (new helper + modified `normalize_steps` or callers), `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/capture_plan.py` (pass sub-timeline data through to normalization)
+- **Models**: No model changes — `fl_shared/models/unified_pipeline.py::Step` already has all needed fields (`id`, `display_name`, `result`, `state`, `duration_ms`, `start_time`, `log_ref`, `job_id`, `is_conditional_failure`, `provider_extensions`)
+- **Reader/MCP layer**: No changes needed — steps written to `steps/<stage_id>.json` are already consumed by the unified reader and MCP tools
+- **Tests**: `tests/data_extractor/azure_devops/test_ado_extractor.py` needs new assertions and possibly new test cases
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/specs/ado-sub-timeline-step-normalization/spec.md b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/specs/ado-sub-timeline-step-normalization/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..8a9d64cb
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/specs/ado-sub-timeline-step-normalization/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,159 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Sub-timeline Task records are normalized to unified Steps
+
+The ADO normalizer SHALL produce `Step` objects for Task-type records found within sub-timelines stored at `provider_raw/azure_devops/sub_timelines/{record_id}.json`. Each Task record in a sub-timeline SHALL be mapped to a `Step` using the same field mapping as primary-timeline Tasks:
+- `id` ← record.id
+- `display_name` ← record.name
+- `display_description` ← record.task.name (if present)
+- `result` ← `_map_record_result(record)` (applies Bottleneck 1 cancellation handling)
+- `state` ← `_map_record_state(record)`
+- `duration_ms` ← computed from `start_time` and `finish_time`
+- `start_time` ← `_iso_utc(record.start_time)`
+- `log_ref` ← None (sub-timeline log capture is out of scope)
+- `job_id` ← the parent Job record's ID from the primary timeline
+- `is_conditional_failure` ← True if task.result == "failed" AND parent Job result ∈ {"succeeded", "succeededWithIssues"}
+- `provider_extensions` ← empty dict
+
+#### Scenario: Failed deployment job with sub-timeline produces steps
+
+- **WHEN** a Job record in the primary timeline has `result: "failed"` AND `details.id` is non-null AND a sub-timeline file exists for that record
+- **THEN** the normalizer SHALL produce a `steps/<job_id>.json` file containing Step objects for every Task-type record in the sub-timeline, sorted by `order`
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline with multiple Task records
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline contains 5 Task records with `type: "Task"` and varying `order` values
+- **THEN** the output `steps/<job_id>.json` SHALL contain exactly 5 Step objects sorted ascending by their `order` field
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline Task timing is computed correctly
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline Task has `startTime: "2024-03-15T10:00:00Z"` and `finishTime: "2024-03-15T10:00:05.500Z"`
+- **THEN** the corresponding Step SHALL have `duration_ms: 5500` and `start_time: "2024-03-15T10:00:00.000Z"`
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Sub-timeline filtering selects only Task-type records
+
+The normalizer SHALL filter sub-timeline records to include ONLY those with `type == "Task"`. Records of type "Phase", "Job", or "Checkpoint" within the sub-timeline SHALL be excluded from the unified step output.
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline contains Phase and Job containers
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline contains records of types ["Phase", "Job", "Task", "Task", "Task", "Checkpoint"]
+- **THEN** only the 3 Task records SHALL appear in the unified step output
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline contains only non-Task records
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline contains only Phase and Job records (no Tasks)
+- **THEN** the output `steps/<job_id>.json` SHALL contain an empty array
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Sub-timeline steps are merged with primary-timeline steps
+
+When a Job record has both direct Task children in the primary timeline AND a sub-timeline, the normalizer SHALL produce a single `steps/<job_id>.json` containing steps from BOTH sources. Primary-timeline steps SHALL appear first, followed by sub-timeline steps, each group sorted by `order`.
+
+#### Scenario: Job has both primary and sub-timeline tasks
+
+- **WHEN** a Job record has 2 Task children in the primary timeline (order 1, 2) AND its sub-timeline contains 3 Task records (order 1, 2, 3)
+- **THEN** `steps/<job_id>.json` SHALL contain 5 Steps: the 2 primary-timeline steps first, then the 3 sub-timeline steps
+
+#### Scenario: Job has only sub-timeline tasks (no primary children)
+
+- **WHEN** a Job record has no Task children in the primary timeline but has a sub-timeline with 4 Task records
+- **THEN** `steps/<job_id>.json` SHALL contain exactly 4 Steps from the sub-timeline
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Sub-timeline data is passed through the normalization function
+
+The `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` function SHALL accept an optional `sub_timelines` parameter of type `dict[str, RawAdoTimeline]` (keyed by the parent Job record ID). When provided, sub-timeline normalization SHALL be performed for each entry. When not provided or empty, behavior SHALL be identical to the current implementation (no sub-timeline steps produced).
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timelines parameter is provided
+
+- **WHEN** `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` is called with `sub_timelines={"job-uuid-1": <RawAdoTimeline>}`
+- **THEN** the normalizer SHALL process the sub-timeline for job-uuid-1 and include the resulting steps in the output items
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timelines parameter is omitted (backward compatibility)
+
+- **WHEN** `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` is called without the `sub_timelines` parameter
+- **THEN** behavior SHALL be identical to the current implementation — no sub-timeline steps are produced, no errors are raised
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Conditional failure detection applies to sub-timeline steps
+
+The normalizer SHALL apply the `is_conditional_failure` heuristic to sub-timeline Task records: a Task with `result == "failed"` whose parent Job record (in the primary timeline) has `result ∈ {"succeeded", "succeededWithIssues"}` SHALL have `is_conditional_failure: true`.
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline task fails but parent job succeeded
+
+- **WHEN** a Job record in the primary timeline has `result: "succeeded"` AND its sub-timeline contains a Task with `result: "failed"`
+- **THEN** the corresponding Step SHALL have `is_conditional_failure: true`
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline task fails and parent job also failed
+
+- **WHEN** a Job record in the primary timeline has `result: "failed"` AND its sub-timeline contains a Task with `result: "failed"`
+- **THEN** the corresponding Step SHALL have `is_conditional_failure: false`
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Missing or corrupt sub-timeline data is handled gracefully
+
+The normalizer SHALL NOT crash when sub-timeline data is missing, empty, or contains unexpected structure. Instead, it SHALL log a warning and skip producing steps for that Job, allowing other normalization to complete.
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline has no records
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline's `records` array is empty
+- **THEN** no steps SHALL be produced for that Job (empty list in output), no error SHALL be raised
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline record has null start/finish times
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline Task record has `startTime: null` and `finishTime: null`
+- **THEN** the Step SHALL have `duration_ms: null` and `start_time: null`, and normalization SHALL continue without error
+
+#### Scenario: Sub-timeline record has null result and inProgress state
+
+- **WHEN** a sub-timeline Task has `result: null` and `state: "inProgress"` (Bottleneck 1: cancellation mid-execution)
+- **THEN** the Step SHALL have `result: ABORTED` and `state: RUNNING` (same as primary timeline Bottleneck 1 handling)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Matrix expansion produces separate step files per leg
+
+For matrix expansion Jobs, each matrix leg is a separate Job record with its own `details` sub-timeline. The normalizer SHALL produce a separate `steps/<leg_job_id>.json` for each leg that has a sub-timeline, not merge all legs into a single file.
+
+#### Scenario: Two matrix legs failed with sub-timelines
+
+- **WHEN** two Job records "Build_Linux" and "Build_Windows" each have `details` with different sub-timeline IDs, and both are present in the `sub_timelines` dict
+- **THEN** the output SHALL contain separate `steps/<linux_job_id>.json` and `steps/<windows_job_id>.json` items, each with their respective sub-timeline's Task records
+
+#### Scenario: Matrix with 10 legs but only 1 failure
+
+- **WHEN** a matrix expansion has 10 Job records but only 1 has `result: "failed"` and a sub-timeline entry in the `sub_timelines` dict
+- **THEN** only 1 `steps/<failed_leg_id>.json` SHALL be produced from sub-timeline data (other legs may still have primary-timeline steps if any exist)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: New helper function is exposed for testability
+
+A dedicated `normalize_sub_timeline_steps()` function SHALL be created with the following signature:
+
+```python
+def normalize_sub_timeline_steps(
+    job_record_id: str,
+    sub_timeline: RawAdoTimeline,
+    parent_succeeded: bool,
+    available_log_refs: set[str] | None = None,
+) -> list[Step]:
+```
+
+This function SHALL be the single entry point for converting a sub-timeline into Steps, callable independently for unit testing.
+
+#### Scenario: Function returns empty list for timeline with no Tasks
+
+- **WHEN** `normalize_sub_timeline_steps("job-1", timeline_with_no_tasks, parent_succeeded=False)` is called
+- **THEN** it SHALL return `[]`
+
+#### Scenario: Function produces correctly mapped Steps
+
+- **WHEN** `normalize_sub_timeline_steps("job-1", timeline_with_3_tasks, parent_succeeded=False)` is called
+- **THEN** it SHALL return a list of 3 Step objects with `job_id == "job-1"` and fields mapped from the Task records
diff --git a/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/tasks.md b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/tasks.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..91a0a81b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/ado-sub-timeline-normalization/tasks.md
@@ -0,0 +1,46 @@
+## 1. Core Normalization Function
+
+- [x] 1.1 Create `normalize_sub_timeline_steps()` in `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/normalizer.py` — accepts `(job_record_id: str, sub_timeline: RawAdoTimeline, parent_succeeded: bool, available_log_refs: set[str] | None = None)` and returns `list[Step]`
+- [x] 1.2 Implement Task-type filtering: select only records with `type == "Task"` from the sub-timeline's `records` array, ignoring Phase/Job/Checkpoint containers
+- [x] 1.3 Implement sorting by `order` field (ascending, nulls last)
+- [x] 1.4 Map each Task record to a `Step` using existing helpers (`_map_record_result`, `_map_record_state`, `_iso_utc`) — same field mapping as primary `normalize_steps()`
+- [x] 1.5 Implement `is_conditional_failure` detection: task.result == "failed" AND `parent_succeeded` is True
+- [x] 1.6 Handle edge cases: empty records array → return `[]`; null start/finish times → `duration_ms: None`; null result with inProgress state → ABORTED (Bottleneck 1)
+
+## 2. Integration into Unified Item Builder
+
+- [x] 2.1 Add `sub_timelines: dict[str, RawAdoTimeline] | None = None` parameter to `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` signature (backward-compatible default)
+- [x] 2.2 After the existing `normalize_steps()` loop over stages, iterate over `sub_timelines` entries: for each `(job_record_id, sub_timeline)`, call `normalize_sub_timeline_steps()`
+- [x] 2.3 Merge logic: if `steps_by_stage[job_record_id]` already exists (primary-timeline tasks), append sub-timeline steps; otherwise create new entry
+- [x] 2.4 Emit `AzureDevOpsGeneratedJsonItem(key=f"steps/{job_record_id}.json", ...)` for merged results
+- [x] 2.5 Determine `parent_succeeded` for each job by looking up the Job record in `timeline.records` by `id == job_record_id` and checking its result
+
+## 3. Wire Sub-Timeline Data Through Capture Plan
+
+- [x] 3.1 In the capture plan orchestrator (the function that calls both `_sub_timeline_items()` and `build_azure_devops_unified_items()`), extract `RawAdoTimeline` objects from the sub-timeline items and build the `dict[str, RawAdoTimeline]` mapping
+- [x] 3.2 Parse the stored `.data` dict back into `RawAdoTimeline` for each sub-timeline item (it's stored as `model_dump(by_alias=True)` — use `RawAdoTimeline.model_validate()`)
+- [x] 3.3 Pass the `sub_timelines` dict to `build_azure_devops_unified_items()`
+
+## 4. Unit Tests — Core Function
+
+- [x] 4.1 Test: sub-timeline with 3 Task records produces 3 Steps sorted by order, with correct field mapping (`id`, `display_name`, `result`, `state`, `duration_ms`, `start_time`, `job_id`)
+- [x] 4.2 Test: sub-timeline with mixed record types (Phase, Job, Task, Checkpoint) produces Steps only for Task records
+- [x] 4.3 Test: sub-timeline with empty records array returns `[]`
+- [x] 4.4 Test: Task with null start/finish times produces Step with `duration_ms: None`, `start_time: None`
+- [x] 4.5 Test: Task with null result and `state: "inProgress"` produces Step with `result: ABORTED` (Bottleneck 1)
+- [x] 4.6 Test: conditional failure — Task failed but `parent_succeeded=True` → `is_conditional_failure: True`
+- [x] 4.7 Test: Task failed and `parent_succeeded=False` → `is_conditional_failure: False`
+
+## 5. Unit Tests — Integration and Merge Behavior
+
+- [x] 5.1 Test: `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` with `sub_timelines` produces `steps/<job_id>.json` items in output
+- [x] 5.2 Test: Job with both primary-timeline Tasks and sub-timeline Tasks produces merged steps (primary first, sub-timeline appended)
+- [x] 5.3 Test: Job with only sub-timeline Tasks (no primary children) produces steps from sub-timeline only
+- [x] 5.4 Test: Two matrix leg Jobs each with their own sub-timeline produce separate `steps/<leg_id>.json` files
+- [x] 5.5 Test: `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` called without `sub_timelines` parameter behaves identically to current (backward compatibility)
+- [x] 5.6 Test: `has_step_logs` on the parent stage is updated correctly when sub-timeline steps have log refs (currently always None, so `has_step_logs` should remain unchanged)
+
+## 6. Export and Documentation
+
+- [x] 6.1 Add `normalize_sub_timeline_steps` to the module-level aliases (alongside existing `normalize_azure_devops_steps`)
+- [x] 6.2 Update `docs/azure-devops-extraction-files.md` to note that `steps/<id>.json` may include steps sourced from sub-timelines for failed deployment jobs
