index 00000000..8c23885f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/design.md
@@ -0,0 +1,222 @@
+# Design Decisions: Unified CI-Agnostic Schema
+
+Architectural decisions for the unified pipeline data format. Each decision is ratified — implementation details live in the linked spec files.
+
+---
+
+## D1: Unified schema lives as top-level JSON files alongside metadata.json
+
+**Decision:** Each inspection folder gains these top-level files:
+
+```
+<uuid>/
+├── metadata.json
+├── execution_timeline.json    (required aggregate full-run view)
+├── pipeline_run.json
+├── stages.json
+├── steps/<stage_id>.json
+├── jobs.json                 (Azure DevOps job details; [] for Jenkins)
+├── parallel_groups.json      ([] when no parallel execution)
+├── artifacts.json
+├── logs/
+│   ├── console.log
+│   ├── <stage_id>.log
+│   └── <stage_id>/<step_id>.log
+├── provider_raw/<ci_system>/
+└── manifest.json
+```
+
+**Why over alternatives:**
+- Embedding in metadata.json: metadata.json is already large; separate files allow lazy loading
+- Nested per-stage directories: harder for flat listing queries; stage_id-keyed files are simpler
+- `execution_timeline.json` as an aggregate: gives readers and MCP tools one full-run entry point while preserving the separate files as the canonical normalized components
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [provider-raw-storage/spec.md](specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md), [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md)
+
+---
+
+## D2: Common status vocabulary with explicit mapping per provider
+
+**Decision:** A single `PipelineStatus` enum: `SUCCESS`, `FAILURE`, `UNSTABLE`, `ABORTED`, `SKIPPED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `QUEUED`, `UNKNOWN`.
+
+Each extractor maps its native vocabulary at write-time. `UNSTABLE` for GitHub Actions uses `result_confidence="inferred"` since GHA has no native equivalent.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) — "Unified Status enum" and "UNSTABLE status confidence and inference rules" requirements.
+
+---
+
+## D3: Provider-specific data in `provider_raw/<ci_system>/` subdirectory
+
+**Decision:** Raw API responses stored unchanged in provider-specific subdirectories. The unified reader NEVER accesses `provider_raw/`. A dedicated HDLF Server endpoint serves raw files for debugging.
+
+**Why over embedding:** Clean separation means the shared reader never parses provider-specific formats.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [provider-raw-storage/spec.md](specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md)
+
+---
+
+## D4: Shared reader as a protocol with single unified implementation
+
+**Decision:** `InspectionReaderProtocol` defines the read interface, including `get_execution_timeline()` for the required full-run aggregate. `UnifiedInspectionReader` reads unified JSON files. `LegacyJenkinsReader` handles pre-migration inspections. A factory function (`create_reader`) selects the right implementation by probing for `pipeline_run.json`.
+
+**Why not per-provider readers:** The extractor (write-side) is per-provider; the reader (read-side) is universal. Consumers never branch on CI system.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [shared-inspection-reader/spec.md](specs/shared-inspection-reader/spec.md)
+
+---
+
+## D5: Flat stage array with parent_id + depends_on for hierarchy
+
+**Decision:** `stages.json` is a flat array of `Stage` objects. `parent_stage_id` expresses containment hierarchy (parallel branches under a stage). `depends_on: list[str]` expresses the execution dependency graph (multi-parent DAG for GHA, sequential for Jenkins).
+
+**Why over nested tree:** Jenkins PARALLEL nodes, Azure DevOps phases/jobs, and GitHub Actions jobs all have different nesting semantics. A flat list with pointers is the least-common-denominator.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) — Stage model requirement.
+
+---
+
+## D6: Stage and step IDs are string, provider-assigned, scoped to the pipeline run
+
+**Decision:** IDs are strings (GitHub uses UUIDs, Azure uses integers, Jenkins uses numeric strings). Unique within a pipeline run but not globally.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md)
+
+---
+
+## D7: Timestamps normalized to ISO 8601 UTC; logs normalized to 1-indexed
+
+**Decision:** All timestamp fields use ISO 8601 in UTC (suffix `Z`). Provider-local timezones stripped at write-time. All stored logs use 1-indexed line numbering (ADO normalized from 0-indexed, GHA timestamp prefixes stripped).
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) — "Timestamps normalized to UTC" and "Log line numbering normalized to 1-indexed" requirements.
+
+---
+
+## D8: Artifacts carry attribution with confidence levels
+
+**Decision:** Each artifact has `producing_stage_id` (confident), `producing_step_id` (task-level), `candidate_stage_ids` (ambiguous), and `attribution_confidence` (high/medium/low). ADO uses the native `source` API field (high confidence). Jenkins uses a 3-tier heuristic cascade (archive_step_node → naming_heuristic → mtime_correlation).
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) — Artifact model requirement.
+
+---
+
+## D9: MCP tools operate in dual-mode during transition
+
+**Decision:** MCP tools detect the inspection format (probe for `pipeline_run.json`) and return the appropriate response shape:
+- **Unified inspections** (new format): Return unified models (`Stage`, `Step`, `Artifact`) with the new field set (`id`, `has_step_logs`, `depends_on`, `failure_category`, etc.)
+- **Legacy inspections** (old format): Return legacy models (`StageSummary`, `StepInfo`, `ArtifactEntry`) with existing fields (`dir`, `has_console_log`, `has_steps_json`, etc.)
+
+Handlers key stages by `id` (unified) or `dir` (legacy) depending on which fields are present. The MCP tool `get_stage_log` accepts both `stage_id` (new) and `stage_dir` (legacy) parameters — exactly one must be provided.
+
+The MCP server also exposes `get_execution_timeline` for unified inspections as the canonical full-run aggregate. This tool is additive; existing stage, step, log, and artifact tools remain available during transition.
+
+**Why not break old API:** Deployed fault handlers are coded against the current response shapes. Breaking them requires coordinated redeployment of all handlers. Dual-mode lets handlers migrate incrementally while new handlers use unified models from day one.
+
+**When to remove legacy mode:** After all deployed handlers have migrated (tracked via handler version annotations), the legacy path can be removed.
+
+---
+
+## D10: `metadata.json` schema evolves with backward-compatible additions
+
+**Decision:** The `metadata.json` schema gains new fields while retaining all existing ones:
+- **New fields:** `ci_system` (Literal["jenkins", "azure_devops", "github_actions"]), `pipeline_url` (generic), `run_identifier`
+- **Retained fields:** `jenkins_url` remains populated for Jenkins inspections (backward compat)
+- **ADO/GHA inspections:** `jenkins_url` is `null`; `pipeline_url` is the canonical URL
+
+The write-side model (`MissionMetadata` in `fl_shared/`) is extended. The read-side model (`InspectionMetadata` in `hdlf_server/`) adds the same fields as optional. The `LegacyJenkinsReader` synthesizes `ci_system="jenkins"` and `pipeline_url` from `jenkins_url` for old inspections.
+
+**Why not rename `jenkins_url`:** It would break every consumer that reads metadata. The additive approach costs one redundant field per inspection.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) — "Unified metadata model" requirement.
+
+---
+
+## D11: GHA URLs rejected at ingestion until extractor exists
+
+**Decision:** The ingestion API URL validation does NOT accept GitHub Actions URLs in this change. The `VALIDATORS` list gains `AzureDevOpsUrlValidator` only. GHA URLs receive HTTP 422 "Unsupported CI system" until the GHA extractor spec is written and implemented.
+
+**Why not accept-and-fail:** Accepting a URL implies the system can process it. A 202 followed by a workflow failure is confusing to API consumers and generates noise in monitoring. Clean rejection is honest.
+
+**When to enable:** The GHA extractor change adds `GitHubActionsUrlValidator` to the validators list and the ingestion API spec is updated at that time.
+
+---
+
+## D12: Temporal activity dispatch happens inside existing activities (no new activities)
+
+**Decision:** `extract_metadata_activity` and `extract_data_activity` gain an internal dispatch branch based on `request.source_system`:
+```python
+if request.source_system == "jenkins":
+    ...  # existing Jenkins path
+elif request.source_system == "azure_devops":
+    ...  # new ADO path
+else:
+    raise NonRetryableError(f"Unsupported source system: {request.source_system}")
+```
+
+The Jenkins normalizer (post-processing) runs at the end of `extract_data_activity` for Jenkins — after downloads, before manifest write. This is NOT a new activity.
+
+**Why not new activities:** Adding activities changes the workflow's determinism (existing running workflows would fail on replay). Activity-internal dispatch is safe for already-running workflows because old workflows always have `source_system="jenkins"`.
+
+**Authoritative spec:** [azure-devops-extractor/spec.md](specs/azure-devops-extractor/spec.md) — "Extractor dispatch by CI system" requirement.
+
+---
+
+## D13: Per-step logs stored at `logs/<stage_id>/<step_id>.log`
+
+**Decision:** When step-level logs are available (ADO task logs, Jenkins Blue Ocean step logs), they are stored at:
+```
+logs/
+├── console.log                 (full build log)
+├── <stage_id>.log              (full stage log — always present for stages with logs)
+└── <stage_id>/
+    ├── <step_id_1>.log         (per-step logs — present when has_step_logs=true)
+    └── <step_id_2>.log
+```
+
+The Step model's `log_ref` field contains the relative path (e.g., `logs/42/7.log`). The reader's `stream_log(uuid, stage_id, step_id)` resolves to this path. If `step_id` is None, it serves `logs/<stage_id>.log`.
+
+**Why nested directories:** Flat naming (`logs/42_7.log`) risks collisions when stage IDs contain underscores. Nesting is unambiguous and mirrors the hierarchy.
+
+**Relationship to per-step-log-fetching change:** The per-step-log-fetching spec captures Jenkins step logs into a raw `step_logs/{node_id}_{step_id}.log` flat layout (provider-raw capture format). The Jenkins normalizer (task 6.4) transforms these into the unified `logs/<stage_id>/<step_id>.log` layout. Both specs are correct — they describe different stages of the pipeline: raw capture → normalized output. The raw files end up in `provider_raw/jenkins/step_logs/` while the normalized files appear at the unified path.
+
+---
+
+## D14: `ci_system` column added to Inspection DB table
+
+**Decision:** The `inspection` table gains a `ci_system VARCHAR(20)` column (nullable for old rows). Populated at ingestion time from the URL validator result. Indexed for efficient filtering.
+
+**Why not derive at runtime:** The handler orchestrator evaluates applicability frequently. Re-parsing the URL every time is wasteful. A column also enables direct SQL analytics queries.
+
+**Migration:** Alembic migration backfills existing rows with `"jenkins"` (all current inspections are Jenkins).
+
+---
+
+## D15: `CiSystem` canonical form is snake_case
+
+**Decision:** The canonical `ci_system` values are: `"jenkins"`, `"azure_devops"`, `"github_actions"` (matching `fl_shared/cr_models.py`). The display-name style `"Jenkins"`, `"Azure DevOps"`, `"GitHub Actions"` (in `hdlf_server/models.py`) is deprecated and replaced with a display helper function.
+
+**Why snake_case:** It's already used in the CRD schema (`FaultHandler.ci_systems`), Temporal search attributes, and the proposed unified models. Display-name style is only in one model.
+
+---
+
+## D16: Manifest v2 is written only after unified output validation
+
+**Decision:** `manifest.json` remains the completion marker and is written only after required unified outputs validate. Required unified files are `execution_timeline.json`, `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `artifacts.json`, `jobs.json`, and `parallel_groups.json`. Step files are optional per stage, but any `Step.log_ref` must point to an existing normalized `logs/...` file before the manifest is written.
+
+Manifest entries use `source="captured"` for provider API downloads and provider-raw copies under `provider_raw/<ci_system>/...`. They use `source="derived"` for unified JSON files and normalized logs under `logs/...`. Every stored manifest entry, captured or derived, includes `captured_size`; skipped entries may include `original_size` and do not claim stored bytes.
+
+**Why:** The manifest must describe a complete, readable snapshot. Writing it before validation would let handlers observe a completed inspection whose unified files or normalized logs are missing or malformed.
+
+---
+
+## Risks / Trade-offs
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|------|-----------|
+| **Migration complexity** | `LegacyJenkinsReader` falls back to parsing old files. No data migration needed. |
+| **Schema evolution** | `provider_extensions: dict[str, Any]` escape hatch; documented known keys per CI system. |
+| **Duplicate storage** | Raw files useful for forensics. Overhead is ~5-20 KB metadata per inspection. |
+| **UNSTABLE semantic mismatch** | GHA gets `result_confidence="inferred"`. Jenkins/ADO are authoritative. |
+| **Breaking change for HDLF consumers** | Dual-write period; existing files stay in place AND appear in `provider_raw/jenkins/`. |
+| **MCP handler migration** | Dual-mode MCP tools (D9) serve both legacy and unified shapes. Handlers migrate incrementally. |
+| **Temporal replay safety** | No new activities (D12). CI dispatch inside existing activities preserves determinism for running workflows. |
+| **metadata.json backward compat** | Additive fields only (D10). `jenkins_url` stays; `ci_system` + `pipeline_url` added alongside. |
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/execution-timeline-plan.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/execution-timeline-plan.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..809f71a4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/execution-timeline-plan.md
@@ -0,0 +1,301 @@
+# Execution Timeline Plan
+
+## Goal
+
+Add a provider-neutral execution timeline file for Jenkins and Azure DevOps that aggregates the existing unified inspection files into one reader- and MCP-friendly payload.
+
+The timeline must follow the unified schema instead of introducing a second model vocabulary. It should reuse `PipelineRun`, `Stage`, `Step`, `JobDetail`, `ParallelGroup`, and `Artifact` as defined by the unified pipeline schema.
+
+## File Name And Location
+
+Write the derived file at the inspection root:
+
+```text
+<uuid>/execution_timeline.json
+```
+
+Do not write this file under `provider_raw/`. The `provider_raw/` directory is reserved for provider-native API responses. In particular, Azure DevOps already has `provider_raw/azure_devops/timeline.json`, which is the native ADO timeline response.
+
+`execution_timeline.json` is a derived unified file, equivalent to reading:
+
+```text
+pipeline_run.json
+stages.json
+steps/<stage_id>.json
+jobs.json
+parallel_groups.json
+artifacts.json
+```
+
+For new unified inspections, this file is required. The reader may still assemble
+the same model from separate files as a compatibility fallback for older
+inspections, but new extractor output should be treated as incomplete if
+`execution_timeline.json` is missing.
+
+## Proposed Schema
+
+Add an `ExecutionTimeline` Pydantic model in `fl_shared/models/unified_pipeline.py`:
+
+```python
+class ExecutionTimeline(BaseModel):
+    """Complete derived execution view for one inspection.
+
+    Provenance: written to ``<uuid>/execution_timeline.json`` by provider
+    normalizers, or assembled by ``UnifiedInspectionReader`` from the separate
+    unified files when the aggregate file is absent.
+
+    Attributes:
+        schema_version: Timeline aggregate schema version.
+        pipeline_run: Top-level run metadata from ``pipeline_run.json``.
+        stages: Flat stage/job/phase/parallel list from ``stages.json``.
+        steps_by_stage: Step arrays keyed by the stage id used in
+            ``steps/<stage_id>.json``.
+        jobs: Job details from ``jobs.json``. Empty for Jenkins.
+        parallel_groups: Concurrent execution groups from
+            ``parallel_groups.json``.
+        artifacts: Artifact list with attribution from ``artifacts.json``.
+        provider_extensions: Provider-specific aggregate metadata. Core
+            consumers should not require this field.
+    """
+
+    schema_version: str = "1.0"
+    pipeline_run: PipelineRun
+    stages: list[Stage]
+    steps_by_stage: dict[str, list[Step]] = Field(default_factory=dict)
+    jobs: list[JobDetail] = Field(default_factory=list)
+    parallel_groups: list[ParallelGroup] = Field(default_factory=list)
+    artifacts: list[Artifact] = Field(default_factory=list)
+    provider_extensions: dict[str, Any] = Field(default_factory=dict)
+```
+
+## Mapping Rules
+
+### Azure DevOps
+
+Azure DevOps should continue using the existing unified mapping rules:
+
+| ADO timeline record | Unified output |
+| --- | --- |
+| `type: "Stage"` | `Stage(type="stage")` in `stages.json` |
+| `type: "Phase"` | `Stage(type="phase")` in `stages.json` |
+| `type: "Job"` | `Stage(type="job")` in `stages.json`, with `parent_stage_id` referencing the containing stage or phase |
+| `type: "Task"` | `Step` in `steps/<stage_or_job_id>.json` |
+
+For `execution_timeline.json`:
+
+- `pipeline_run` comes from normalized ADO build metadata.
+- `stages` contains the same entries as `stages.json`.
+- `steps_by_stage` uses the same keys as the generated `steps/<stage_id>.json` files.
+- ADO task steps retain `job_id` pointing to the containing job-stage id.
+- `jobs` contains `JobDetail` entries derived from ADO `Job` records.
+- `parallel_groups` contains matrix or multi-job groups detected from ADO timeline records.
+- `artifacts` contains normalized artifacts with API-source attribution when available.
+
+### Jenkins
+
+Jenkins should aggregate the output of the Jenkins normalizer:
+
+| Jenkins source | Unified output |
+| --- | --- |
+| Blue Ocean `STAGE` node | `Stage(type="stage")` |
+| Blue Ocean `PARALLEL` node | `Stage(type="parallel")`, with `parent_stage_id` pointing to the containing stage |
+| Blue Ocean stage steps | `Step` in `steps/<stage_id>.json` |
+| WFAPI stages | Fallback `Stage(type="stage")` data when Blue Ocean nodes are unavailable |
+
+For `execution_timeline.json`:
+
+- `pipeline_run` comes from `pipeline_run.json`.
+- `stages` contains the same entries as `stages.json`.
+- `steps_by_stage` uses the same keys as `steps/<stage_id>.json`.
+- Jenkins steps keep `job_id = None`.
+- `jobs` is an empty list.
+- `parallel_groups` contains Jenkins parallel branch groups when detected.
+- `artifacts` contains normalized Jenkins artifacts with attribution confidence.
+
+## Implementation Steps
+
+### 1. Update The OpenSpec Contract
+
+Update `specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md` with a new requirement for the required aggregate file:
+
+- `execution_timeline.json` is a top-level unified derived file.
+- New unified inspections must write `execution_timeline.json` before `manifest.json` is written.
+- It must not be read from or written under `provider_raw/`.
+- Its fields must reuse the existing unified models.
+- Harmonized readers and MCP tools expose it as the canonical full-run timeline view.
+- Readers may assemble it from separate files only as a backward-compatible fallback for older inspections.
+
+Update `design.md` to include `execution_timeline.json` in the inspection folder layout and in the list of required manifest-gated unified files.
+
+### 2. Add The Shared Model
+
+Add `ExecutionTimeline` to `fl_shared/models/unified_pipeline.py` and export it where the other unified models are exported.
+
+Keep the model small and compositional. Do not duplicate fields from `Stage` or `Step` into new timeline-specific node objects unless there is a separate consumer requirement for a fully flattened graph.
+
+### 3. Add A Shared Builder
+
+Add a shared helper in `fl_shared`, for example:
+
+```text
+fl_shared/execution_timeline.py
+```
+
+This keeps dependency direction aligned with the existing architecture:
+
+- `fl_control_plane` write-side normalizers may import from `fl_shared`.
+- `hdlf_server` readers may import from `fl_shared`.
+- `hdlf_server` must not import write-side helpers from `fl_control_plane`.
+
+Recommended API:
+
+```python
+def build_execution_timeline(
+    pipeline_run: PipelineRun,
+    stages: list[Stage],
+    steps_by_stage: dict[str, list[Step]],
+    jobs: list[JobDetail],
+    parallel_groups: list[ParallelGroup],
+    artifacts: list[Artifact],
+    provider_extensions: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
+) -> ExecutionTimeline:
+    ...
+```
+
+This keeps Jenkins and ADO aggregation behavior identical.
+
+### 4. Write The File For Azure DevOps
+
+In `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/capture_plan.py`, extend `build_azure_devops_unified_items()` after constructing the existing unified outputs.
+
+The implementation should:
+
+1. Build `pipeline_run`, `stages`, `jobs`, `parallel_groups`, and `artifacts` once as model objects.
+2. Build `steps_by_stage` while generating `steps/<stage_id>.json`.
+3. Add one generated item:
+
+```text
+execution_timeline.json
+```
+
+The manifest source should be `derived`.
+
+### 5. Write The File For Jenkins
+
+In `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/jenkins/normalizer.py`, write `execution_timeline.json` near the end of `normalize_jenkins_inspection()` after the existing normalizers have produced their model objects.
+
+The implementation should avoid reparsing provider raw where possible:
+
+1. Reuse `pipeline_run` from `normalize_pipeline_run()` or read back `pipeline_run.json` if the function currently only writes.
+2. Reuse `stages` returned from `normalize_stages()`.
+3. Reuse `steps_map` returned from `normalize_steps()` and updated by `normalize_logs()`.
+4. Reuse `parallel_groups` and `artifacts` if those normalizers are adjusted to return model lists; otherwise read the generated files back as a short-term bridge.
+5. Write `execution_timeline.json` with `jobs=[]`.
+
+Prefer returning model objects from normalizer functions over reading files back, but keep the first implementation scoped.
+
+### 6. Add Reader Support
+
+Extend `InspectionReaderProtocol` with:
+
+```python
+async def get_execution_timeline(self, inspection_uuid: str) -> ExecutionTimeline:
+    ...
+```
+
+Implement in `UnifiedInspectionReader`:
+
+1. Try to read and validate `execution_timeline.json`.
+2. If it is absent, assemble it from:
+   - `get_pipeline_run()`
+   - `list_stages()`
+   - `get_stage_steps()` for each stage
+   - `get_jobs()`
+   - `get_parallel_groups()`
+   - `list_artifacts()`
+
+The fallback preserves compatibility with inspections written before this file existed. For new inspections, validation should fail before manifest write if the physical `execution_timeline.json` file is missing.
+
+The fallback should use the shared builder from `fl_shared/execution_timeline.py`, not a control-plane module.
+
+Implement equivalent assembly in `LegacyJenkinsReader` if the protocol is used against legacy inspections. It can return `jobs=[]` and use the existing legacy-to-unified stage and step conversion.
+
+### 7. Add HDLF Server Endpoint
+
+Add a unified endpoint in `hdlf_server/router.py`:
+
+```text
+GET /api/v1/inspection/{inspection_id}/unified/execution-timeline
+```
+
+Response model:
+
+```python
+ExecutionTimeline
+```
+
+The endpoint should use `create_reader()` and call `reader.get_execution_timeline()`.
+
+It must not read `provider_raw/` directly.
+
+### 8. Add MCP Tool
+
+Extend `fl_mcp_servers/pipeline_data_mcp.py`:
+
+1. Add `_PipelineDataReader.get_execution_timeline()`.
+2. Add an MCP tool:
+
+```python
+@mcp.tool()
+async def get_execution_timeline(ctx: Context) -> ExecutionTimeline:
+    """Return the full unified pipeline execution timeline for the current inspection."""
+```
+
+This gives agents a single call for run metadata, stages, steps, jobs, parallel groups, and artifacts.
+
+This tool is additive. Existing MCP tools should keep their current dual-mode response shapes during the transition.
+
+### 9. Update Manifest And Contract Validation
+
+Include `execution_timeline.json` in generated manifests as a `derived` item.
+
+Require `execution_timeline.json` in `unified_contract.py` for new extractions. The manifest should not be written until this file validates.
+
+Update the design decision that lists required manifest-gated unified files so the architecture document and contract validation agree.
+
+### 10. Tests
+
+Add tests for the write side:
+
+- ADO extraction writes `execution_timeline.json`.
+- ADO aggregate includes `stage`, `phase`, `job`, and task steps keyed by the generated step file stage id.
+- Jenkins normalization writes `execution_timeline.json`.
+- Jenkins aggregate includes `stage` and `parallel`, `jobs=[]`, and steps with `job_id=None`.
+
+Add tests for the read side:
+
+- `UnifiedInspectionReader.get_execution_timeline()` reads the aggregate when present.
+- `UnifiedInspectionReader.get_execution_timeline()` assembles from separate files when absent.
+- Missing optional files produce empty lists, matching current reader behavior.
+- Malformed aggregate raises `MalformedInspectionDataError`.
+
+Add server and MCP tests:
+
+- HDLF endpoint returns `ExecutionTimeline`.
+- MCP `get_execution_timeline` returns the aggregate for unified inspections.
+- Legacy Jenkins behavior is either supported or explicitly covered by existing dual-mode fallback.
+
+## Rollout Strategy
+
+1. Add model, reader fallback, and tests first. This is backward-compatible because the reader can assemble the aggregate from existing files.
+2. Add writer support for ADO and Jenkins.
+3. Require `execution_timeline.json` in new-output validation before `manifest.json` is written.
+4. Add HDLF endpoint.
+5. Add MCP tool.
+
+## Non-Goals
+
+- Do not replace `stages.json`, `steps/<stage_id>.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, or `artifacts.json`.
+- Do not move ADO native `provider_raw/azure_devops/timeline.json`.
+- Do not make CI-agnostic readers parse `provider_raw/`.
+- Do not introduce a new flattened timeline-node schema unless a consumer needs it.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/issues-harmonization-implementation.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/issues-harmonization-implementation.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..79e7da02
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/issues-harmonization-implementation.md
@@ -0,0 +1,252 @@
+# Implementation Plan: Harmonize Provider Issue Files
+
+## Goal
+
+Make Azure DevOps and Jenkins expose failure issues through the same HDLF
+contract while keeping provider provenance clear.
+
+Canonical provider issue paths:
+
+```text
+provider_raw/azure_devops/issues/{record_id}.json
+provider_raw/jenkins/issues/{stage_id}.json
+```
+
+Consumer-facing normalized issue messages belong in:
+
+```text
+stages.json -> Stage.failure_messages
+```
+
+Consumers should not read `provider_raw/` for normal analysis. Provider raw issue
+files are for debugging, provenance, and deep provider-specific inspection.
+
+## Current State
+
+Azure DevOps already derives issue files from native timeline records:
+
+- Source: `RawTimelineRecord.issues[]`
+- Writer: `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/capture_plan.py`
+- Extractor helper: `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/normalizer.py::extract_issues`
+- Current shape: plain JSON array with `type`, `message`, `category`, and
+  `source_record`
+- Normalization: ADO `normalize_stages()` already copies error issue messages
+  into `Stage.failure_messages`
+
+Jenkins derives issue files from stage console logs:
+
+- Source: `stages/{stage_dir}/console.log`
+- Writer: `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/jenkins/issues.py::derive_issues`
+- Current shape: plain JSON array with `message` and `line`
+- Normalization gap: Jenkins issue files are written after `normalize_stages()`,
+  but the derived issue messages are not copied back into `Stage.failure_messages`
+  before the final `stages.json` write. Only WFAPI fallback stage errors populate
+  Jenkins `failure_messages` today.
+
+## Target Contract
+
+Use a plain JSON array for both providers. This matches the already documented
+and implemented ADO layout and avoids changing existing ADO files to an envelope.
+
+Each item SHALL have this provider-neutral issue shape:
+
+```json
+{
+  "type": "error",
+  "message": "Process returned exit code 1",
+  "category": null,
+  "source_record": "Build",
+  "source_line": 42
+}
+```
+
+Field rules:
+
+- `type`: `"error"` or `"warning"`. Jenkins log parsing should emit `"error"`
+  for the existing error patterns.
+- `message`: human-readable issue text, deduplicated within a file.
+- `category`: provider category when available. ADO may use values such as
+  `"General"`, `"Code"`, or `"Test"`; Jenkins should use `null` unless a later
+  parser has a defensible category signal.
+- `source_record`: provider record or stage/step display name that produced the
+  issue. ADO should keep the timeline child record name. Jenkins should use the
+  stage display name, or the step display name when parsing can identify a step
+  segment.
+- `source_line`: 1-based source log line when known. Jenkins should populate it
+  from console log parsing. ADO should use `null` because timeline issues do not
+  carry log line numbers.
+
+## Code Changes
+
+### 1. Add a shared issue model
+
+Create `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/issues.py`:
+
+```python
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from typing import Literal
+
+from pydantic import BaseModel
+
+
+class ProviderIssue(BaseModel):
+    """Provider-neutral issue item written under provider_raw/<ci_system>/issues/."""
+
+    type: Literal["error", "warning"]
+    message: str
+    category: str | None = None
+    source_record: str | None = None
+    source_line: int | None = None
+```
+
+Return `ProviderIssue` objects internally and write `model_dump(mode="json")`
+arrays at serialization boundaries.
+
+### 2. Harmonize Azure DevOps issue output
+
+Update `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/azure_devops/normalizer.py::extract_issues`:
+
+- Return `list[ProviderIssue]` or `list[dict[str, Any]]` produced from
+  `ProviderIssue`.
+- Preserve existing fields.
+- Add `source_line=None`.
+- Keep deduplication by message.
+
+Expected ADO output item:
+
+```json
+{
+  "type": "error",
+  "message": "Build failed",
+  "category": "General",
+  "source_record": "Compile",
+  "source_line": null
+}
+```
+
+### 3. Harmonize Jenkins issue output
+
+Update `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/jenkins/issues.py`:
+
+- Change `_extract_issues_from_log()` to emit the common issue shape.
+- Replace `line` with `source_line`.
+- Add `type="error"`, `category=None`, and `source_record`.
+- Pass the stage display name into the parser from `derive_issues()`.
+
+Expected Jenkins output item:
+
+```json
+{
+  "type": "error",
+  "message": "Process returned exit code 1",
+  "category": null,
+  "source_record": "Build",
+  "source_line": 42
+}
+```
+
+Keep absence semantics unchanged: if no recognized error pattern is found, do
+not write `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/{stage_id}.json`.
+
+### 4. Normalize Jenkins issues into stages.json
+
+Add a Jenkins helper, for example in `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/jenkins/issues.py`:
+
+```python
+async def enrich_stage_failure_messages_from_issues(
+    folder: str,
+    hdlf_client: HdlfClient,
+    stages: list[Stage],
+) -> None:
+    ...
+```
+
+Behavior:
+
+- For each failed or unstable stage, read
+  `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/{stage.id}.json` when present.
+- Validate issue items with `ProviderIssue`.
+- Set `stage.failure_messages` to deduplicated `message` values where
+  `type == "error"`.
+- Preserve existing WFAPI fallback messages when no issue file exists.
+- If both WFAPI and issue-file messages exist, append issue messages after any
+  existing WFAPI message and deduplicate.
+
+Update `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/jenkins/normalizer.py` ordering:
+
+```python
+await derive_issues(folder, hdlf_client, stages)
+await enrich_stage_failure_messages_from_issues(folder, hdlf_client, stages)
+await normalize_logs(folder, hdlf_client, stages, steps_map, config, inspection_bytes)
+await _write_json(hdlf_client, f"{folder}/stages.json", [stage.model_dump(mode="json") for stage in stages])
+```
+
+This works with the current flow because `stages.json` is written once by
+`normalize_stages()` and then written again at the end of
+`normalize_jenkins_inspection()` after log normalization updates `has_step_logs`.
+
+### 5. Keep unified readers provider-raw-free
+
+Do not change `hdlf_server/unified_reader.py` to read issue files. The normalized
+contract for consumers is `Stage.failure_messages` in `stages.json`.
+
+Provider raw issue files remain available through the provider-raw endpoint only.
+
+## Documentation Changes
+
+Update these files after implementation:
+
+- `docs/azure-devops-extraction-files.md`
+  - Add `source_line` to the issue item description.
+  - State that the file is a plain JSON array using the common issue item schema.
+- `docs/jenkins-extraction-files.md`
+  - Add `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/{stage_id}.json`.
+  - Document that Jenkins issues are log-derived, heuristic, and have
+    `category=null` unless a later parser can classify them.
+- `openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md`
+  - Replace the envelope example with the plain array example, or explicitly
+    mark the envelope as rejected.
+  - Keep the provenance difference table.
+- `openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md`
+  - Keep the requirement that Jenkins `Stage.failure_messages` is populated
+    from derived issue files.
+
+## Tests
+
+Add or update tests in `tests/data_extractor/test_jenkins_normalizer.py`:
+
+- `_extract_issues_from_log()` returns `type`, `message`, `category`,
+  `source_record`, and `source_line`.
+- Duplicate Jenkins messages are deduplicated.
+- `derive_issues()` writes `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/{stage_id}.json` with
+  the common shape.
+- `normalize_jenkins_inspection()` writes `stages.json` with
+  `failure_messages` populated from derived issue files.
+- WFAPI fallback messages are preserved when no issue file exists.
+
+Add or update ADO tests in `tests/data_extractor/azure_devops/test_ado_extractor.py`:
+
+- ADO issue files include `source_line: null`.
+- Existing `failure_messages` normalization still deduplicates error messages.
+
+## Acceptance Criteria
+
+- ADO and Jenkins issue files are both plain JSON arrays of the same issue item
+  schema.
+- Jenkins issue files no longer use the provider-specific `line` key.
+- ADO issue files include `source_line` with `null` values.
+- Jenkins `stages.json` includes log-derived error messages in
+  `failure_messages` for failed or unstable stages when issue parsing finds
+  errors.
+- Unified readers and MCP consumers can continue to use `stages.json` without
+  reading `provider_raw/`.
+- Provider raw endpoints can still expose the issue files for deep inspection.
+
+## Non-Goals
+
+- Do not make Jenkins issue extraction authoritative. It remains best-effort log
+  parsing.
+- Do not add Jenkins categories unless backed by a concrete parser signal.
+- Do not change provider raw paths.
+- Do not make `UnifiedInspectionReader` parse provider raw issue files.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/proposal.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/proposal.md

+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/proposal.md
@@ -0,0 +1,70 @@
+# Unified CI-Agnostic Schema — Proposal
+
+## Problem
+
+HDLF inspection folders are structured around Jenkins-native concepts. As we add Azure DevOps and GitHub Actions support, every consumer (HDLF Server, Handler Orchestrator, MCP tools) would need per-provider branching. This change normalizes pipeline data at write-time so all downstream consumers operate against a single format.
+
+## Scope
+
+| In scope | Out of scope |
+|----------|-------------|
+| CI-agnostic data models (PipelineRun, Stage, Step, Artifact, ParallelGroup, JobDetail) | Building the GHA extractor implementation (separate work item) |
+| Provider-raw storage contract | Real-time / streaming pipeline data |
+| Shared reader protocol + implementations | Changing Temporal workflow structure (no new activities) |
+| ADO extractor specification | Redesigning the ingestion API beyond URL validation |
+| GHA extractor specification | Enabling GHA ingestion before the extractor implementation exists |
+| Ingestion API URL validation for Jenkins + Azure DevOps | GitHub Actions URL validation (deferred until GHA extractor) |
+| CI dispatch logic inside existing Temporal activities | Unifying log content formats (logs remain opaque text) |
+| Unified metadata.json schema (additive, backward-compat) | |
+| DB schema: `ci_system` column on Inspection table | |
+| MCP tools dual-mode (serve both unified and legacy shapes) | |
+| ADO credential retrieval (Vault integration) | |
+| Jenkins normalizer (post-processing for new-format output) | |
+| CiSystem canonical form standardization (snake_case) | |
+
+## Spec Files
+
+| Spec | What it defines | Key models/contracts |
+|------|----------------|---------------------|
+| [unified-pipeline-schema](specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md) | All unified Pydantic models, status enums, normalization rules, provider_extensions contract | PipelineRun, Stage, Step, Artifact, ParallelGroup, ParallelLeg, JobDetail, PipelineStatus |
+| [shared-inspection-reader](specs/shared-inspection-reader/spec.md) | Reader protocol, implementations, factory | InspectionReaderProtocol, UnifiedInspectionReader, LegacyJenkinsReader, create_reader() |
+| [provider-raw-storage](specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md) | Raw file storage layout per CI system, HDLF endpoints, derived issues | provider_raw/ structure, dedicated endpoints, issues schema |
+| [azure-devops-extractor](specs/azure-devops-extractor/spec.md) | ADO client, capture plan, failure detection, Pydantic raw models | AzureDevOpsClient, RawTimelineRecord, RawAdoBuild, RawAdoArtifact, has_failures() |
+| [github-actions-extractor](specs/github-actions-extractor/spec.md) | Future GHA client, metadata extraction, capture plan, log ZIP handling, artifacts, attempts, unified output | GitHubActionsClient, RawGhaWorkflowRun, RawGhaWorkflowJob, RawGhaArtifact, provider_raw/github_actions/ |
+| [ingestion-api](specs/ingestion-api/spec.md) | URL validation expansion | Jenkins/ADO/GHA URL patterns, source_system detection |
+
+## Supporting Documents
+
+| Document | Purpose | Status |
+|----------|---------|--------|
+| [design.md](design.md) | Architectural decisions (D1–D15) with rationale | **Current** — links to specs for details |
+| [unification-gaps.md](unification-gaps.md) | Tracked schema gaps and their resolutions | **Current** — 13/13 resolved |
+| [jenkins-inspection-analysis.md](specs/analysis/jenkins-inspection-analysis.md) | Deep-dive on real Jenkins inspection data (primary research) | **Reference** — data used to inform spec decisions |
+| [gh-actions-extraction-issues.md](specs/analysis/gh-actions-extraction-issues.md) | GitHub Actions extraction pitfalls and community issues | **Research** — incorporated into github-actions-extractor spec |
+| [tasks.md](tasks.md) | Implementation task breakdown (19 sections) | **Current** — derived from specs |
+
+## Impact
+
+| Component | Change |
+|-----------|--------|
+| `fl_shared/` | New unified models, reader protocol, status mapping, metadata model extension |
+| `hdlf_server/reader.py` | Rewritten to use UnifiedInspectionReader; old logic → LegacyJenkinsReader |
+| `hdlf_server/routes.py` | New provider-raw endpoints; existing routes use create_reader() |
+| `hdlf_server/models.py` | CiSystem canonical form migration (display names → snake_case) |
+| `fl_control_plane/data_extractor/` | Jenkins normalizer (post-processing); ADO extraction pipeline; generic auth |
+| `fl_control_plane/metadata_extractor/` | metadata.json extended with `ci_system`, `pipeline_url`, `run_identifier` |
+| `fl_control_plane/credentials.py` | New `get_azure_devops_credentials()` (Vault-based PAT retrieval) |
+| `fl_control_plane/ingestion_api/` | ADO URL validator; source_system from validator (not string match); ci_system in DB |
+| `fl_control_plane/temporal/` | Activity dispatch by source_system inside existing activities (no new activities) |
+| `fl_control_plane/database.py` | New `ci_system` column on Inspection table + Alembic migration |
+| `fl_mcp_servers/pipeline_data_mcp.py` | Dual-mode tools (unified for new inspections, legacy for old) |
+| Handler Orchestrator | Minor: reads ci_system from DB column instead of re-parsing URL |
+
+## Breaking Changes
+
+- HDLF inspection folder layout gains new top-level files (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, etc.)
+- Existing Jenkins files (`pipeline_tree.json`, `wfapi_describe.json`) move to `provider_raw/jenkins/`
+- `CiSystem` type values change from display names ("Jenkins") to snake_case ("jenkins")
+- MCP tool responses gain new fields for unified inspections (additive, not removing old fields)
+- `DownloaderConfig.jenkins_auth_header` renamed to `auth_header`
+- **Mitigation:** Dual-write during migration + `LegacyJenkinsReader` for old inspections + MCP dual-mode
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/analysis/gh-actions-extraction-issues.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/analysis/gh-actions-extraction-issues.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..acd96918
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/analysis/gh-actions-extraction-issues.md
@@ -0,0 +1,501 @@
+# Common Issues with GitHub Actions Pipeline Data Extraction for Offline Analysis
+
+## Overview
+
+This document catalogs known issues and challenges when extracting GitHub Actions pipeline data for offline analysis. It covers both issues identified from our codebase design work and common problems reported by the community.
+
+### Resolution Status
+
+Issues 1–13 were identified during unified schema design. Most are resolved by schema decisions or documented as known constraints. Issues 14–31 are community-sourced and serve as input for the future GitHub Actions extractor spec.
+
+| Status | Meaning |
+|--------|---------|
+| ✅ RESOLVED | Addressed by unified schema decisions or spec requirements |
+| ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED | Requires handling in the future GHA extractor spec |
+| 📋 DOCUMENTED | Acknowledged constraint; no action beyond documentation |
+
+---
+
+## 1. Status Vocabulary Mismatch — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+GitHub Actions uses lowercase status strings (`"success"`, `"failure"`, `"skipped"`) with no concept of Jenkins's `UNSTABLE`. Mapping to a unified enum requires lossy inference:
+
+| GitHub Actions | Unified Enum | Notes |
+|---------------|-------------|-------|
+| `success` | `SUCCESS` | Direct mapping |
+| `failure` | `FAILURE` or `UNSTABLE` | `UNSTABLE` only if test results exist alongside failure |
+| `skipped` | `SKIPPED` | Direct mapping |
+| `cancelled` | `ABORTED` | Direct mapping |
+| `in_progress` | `IN_PROGRESS` | Direct mapping |
+| `queued` | `QUEUED` | Direct mapping |
+| (unknown) | `UNKNOWN` | Fallback |
+
+**Risk**: The `failure` → `UNSTABLE` inference is lossy and context-dependent.
+
+**Resolution:** Unified schema uses `result_confidence="inferred"` when mapping GHA failure → UNSTABLE. Only set when test result analysis shows mixed pass/fail. See unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md "UNSTABLE status confidence and inference rules".
+
+---
+
+## 2. Hierarchy Semantics Differ Fundamentally — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+- **Jenkins**: Tree of PARALLEL/STAGE nodes with `firstParent` edges
+- **GitHub Actions**: Arbitrary DAG via `needs` — cannot assume parent-child or sequential relationships
+- **Azure DevOps**: Phases/jobs with phases
+
+GitHub Actions `needs` can express any DAG (diamond dependencies, fan-out/fan-in), not just trees. This means:
+- A job can have multiple parents
+- Execution order is not implied by definition order
+- The graph may have disconnected components (jobs with no `needs`)
+
+**Solution**: Flat stage array with `parent_stage_id` and `order` fields.
+
+**Resolution:** Unified Stage model has `parent_stage_id` for hierarchy + `depends_on: list[str]` for the multi-parent DAG. GHA `needs:` maps directly to `depends_on`.
+
+---
+
+## 3. Timestamp Format Chaos — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+| Source | Format | Example |
+|--------|--------|---------|
+| Jenkins Blue Ocean | ISO 8601 with offset | `2026-06-05T08:00:45.363+0000` |
+| Jenkins WFAPI | Epoch milliseconds | `1780646444802` |
+| GitHub Actions | ISO 8601 UTC | `2026-06-05T08:00:45.000Z` |
+
+**Risks**:
+- Mixing formats silently corrupts duration calculations
+- Precision varies (seconds vs milliseconds) depending on endpoint
+- Must normalize ALL to UTC ISO 8601 at extraction time
+
+**Resolution:** Spec requires all timestamps normalized to ISO 8601 UTC (suffix `Z`) at write-time. GHA already uses UTC so no conversion needed — just format standardization.
+
+---
+
+## 4. Artifact Attribution Ambiguity — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+CI APIs provide artifact lists but don't cleanly associate them with the producing stage. Options:
+
+| Approach | Reliability | Cost |
+|----------|------------|------|
+| Parse upload action metadata | Unreliable | Low |
+| Naming convention correlation | Fragile | Low |
+| Per-step log parsing for archive patterns | Reliable | High (N API calls) |
+
+**Unified schema solution**: `producing_stage_id` (confident) + `candidate_stage_ids` (ambiguous).
+
+**Resolution:** Artifact model now has `producing_stage_id`, `producing_step_id`, `attribution_method`, and `attribution_confidence`. GHA can use job-level association from API (since v4) for medium confidence.
+
+---
+
+## 5. Rate Limiting & Pagination — ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED
+
+- **Authenticated limit**: 5,000 requests/hour
+- **Large workflows** (100+ jobs): Require pagination across multiple endpoints
+- **No built-in retry-on-429** in current client implementation
+- **Enterprise instances**: May have different rate limits
+
+**Requirements**:
+- Exponential backoff for 429 responses
+- Pagination handling for job/step lists
+- Token bucket or leaky bucket rate limiter
+
+---
+
+## 6. Null/Optional Field Variance — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+Many fields that seem required are actually optional:
+- Step-level `start_time` / `end_time` — absent for skipped steps
+- `log_ref` — null for steps that produced no output
+- `conclusion` — null while job is `in_progress`
+- Parallelism metadata — requires graph traversal of `needs`
+
+**Rule**: Consumers must never assume any field is populated.
+
+**Resolution:** All unified model fields that depend on CI API data are `| None`. Reader protocol returns empty lists (not errors) for absent files.
+
+---
+
+## 7. Artifact Expiry — ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED
+
+- **Default retention**: 90 days (configurable per-repo)
+- `download_url` values may 404 after expiry
+- **Mitigation**: Capture `size_bytes` and content hash at extraction time
+- The URL is best-effort; actual content must be downloaded during extraction window
+
+---
+
+## 8. Step/Job ID Format Differences — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+| CI System | ID Format |
+|-----------|-----------|
+| GitHub Actions | UUIDs or integers depending on endpoint |
+| Jenkins | Numeric strings |
+| Azure DevOps | Integers |
+
+**Rule**: Store all IDs as strings. No numeric comparisons or assumptions.
+
+**Resolution:** Design decision D6 — all IDs are `str` in unified models.
+
+---
+
+## 9. Authentication Token Scoping — ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED
+
+- Wrong token scope silently returns 403 (not 401)
+- `GITHUB_WDF_TOKEN` vs `GITHUB_TOOL_TOKEN` distinction
+- Enterprise instances need different base URLs
+- **No scope validation at startup** — extraction can fail deep into a run
+
+**Requirement**: Validate token scopes before starting extraction.
+
+---
+
+## 10. Workflow Definition Drift — ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED
+
+The YAML that defined a historical run may have changed since:
+- Runner OS versions change
+- Action versions are updated (`@v3` → `@v4`)
+- Environment variables / secrets rotate
+- Matrix dimensions added/removed
+
+**Mitigation**: Capture raw workflow YAML in `provider_extensions` for reproducibility.
+
+---
+
+## 11. Console Log Truncation — ⚠️ SPEC NEEDED
+
+- GitHub Actions workflow run logs have a **~40MB combined size limit**
+- Individual step logs can be truncated for long-running processes
+- Log download API returns a ZIP of per-job logs, not a single stream
+- **No streaming access** to historical logs (only download)
+
+---
+
+## 12. Rerun Semantics — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+- GitHub Actions supports re-running failed jobs or entire workflows
+- Re-runs create **new attempt numbers** under the same `run_id`
+- The API distinguishes `run_number` (increments per trigger) from `run_attempt` (increments per re-run)
+- **Risk**: Extracting only the latest attempt loses failure history; extracting all attempts multiplies data volume
+
+**Resolution:** Unified Stage model has `attempt`, `total_attempts`, `has_prior_failure`. GHA extractor fills from `run_attempt`. `run_identifier` uses `"{run_number}/{run_attempt}"` format.
+
+---
+
+## 13. Matrix Job Explosion — ✅ RESOLVED
+
+- Matrix strategies can generate hundreds of jobs from a single definition
+- Each matrix combination is a separate job with its own logs/artifacts
+- **Naming**: Jobs are named `<job_name> (<matrix_value_1>, <matrix_value_2>)`
+- **Extraction challenge**: Must parse matrix dimensions from job name or use the `matrix` field in API response
+
+**Resolution:** ParallelGroup model with `strategy="matrix"` and ParallelLeg with `matrix_values: dict[str, str]`. GHA matrix jobs map naturally.
+
+---
+
+## Community-Reported Issues (14–31) — ⚠️ ALL SPEC NEEDED
+
+The following issues have been identified from GitHub Community Discussions, open-source CI analytics tools (CIAnalyzer, workflow-telemetry-action, otel-export-trace-action), GitHub's REST API description repository, and official documentation.
+
+> **These are implementation considerations for the future GitHub Actions extractor spec.** They do not affect the unified schema design but must be addressed when building the GHA extraction pipeline.
+
+---
+
+### 14. Artifact URLs Only Available After Entire Workflow Completes
+
+**Source**: [GitHub Community Discussion #51403](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/51403)
+
+A common misconception is that artifact download URLs become available once individual jobs finish. In reality, **artifact URLs are only accessible after the entire workflow run completes**.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Cannot stream artifact data as jobs finish — must wait for full workflow completion
+- Forces architectural workarounds: splitting into separate workflows triggered by `workflow_run` event
+- Makes real-time analytics pipelines impossible without webhook-based architectures
+
+**Workaround**: `upload-artifact@v4` now includes `artifact-url` in step outputs, partially mitigating this for newer implementations.
+
+---
+
+### 15. Artifact Download URL Expires in 1 Minute
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation
+
+The `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/artifacts/{artifact_id}/zip` endpoint returns a **302 redirect** with a presigned URL that expires after **1 minute**.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Cannot cache or store download URLs for later batch processing
+- Must download immediately after URL retrieval
+- Retry logic must re-fetch the redirect URL, not retry the expired URL
+- High-latency networks may fail to complete large downloads within the window
+
+---
+
+### 16. Check Suite Assignment Randomness
+
+**Source**: [GitHub Community Discussion #24616](https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/24616)
+
+When multiple workflows trigger on the same commit SHA, GitHub **randomly assigns** check runs to existing check suites. The `check_suite_id` parameter in the API is **silently ignored**.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Annotations from one workflow (e.g., `pull_request` event) end up under a different workflow's tab (e.g., `push` event)
+- Test result annotations become "practically useless" for multi-workflow repositories
+- GitHub Advanced Security scans interfere with custom workflow checks
+- Cannot reliably associate check runs with the workflow run that produced them
+
+**Workaround**: Use separate GitHub App tokens instead of `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or extract check suite IDs from workflow run objects via `checkSuiteUrl`.
+
+---
+
+### 17. Log Archive Incompleteness for Re-runs
+
+**Source**: GitHub documentation on workflow run logs
+
+When downloading the log archive for a **partially re-run** workflow, the archive **only includes logs for the re-run jobs**. To obtain complete logs, you must separately download archives from each prior attempt.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- A single download does not give you the full picture
+- Must track `run_attempt` and download logs for each attempt separately
+- Joining logs across attempts requires understanding which jobs ran in which attempt
+- Makes "download all logs for this run" a multi-request operation
+
+---
+
+### 18. Run ID Integer Overflow (int32 vs int64)
+
+**Source**: [github/rest-api-description#5733](https://github.com/github/rest-api-description/issues/5733)
+
+The OpenAPI specification for `run-id` lacks a `format: "int64"` annotation. Code generators (especially Go SDK) default to `int32`, which **truncates large run IDs** and causes silent 404 errors.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Auto-generated SDKs may fail on repositories with high run counts
+- The truncated ID produces a valid-looking but wrong API request → 404, not a parse error
+- Developers debugging this see correct `curl` responses but broken SDK calls
+- Requires manual type overrides in generated client code
+
+---
+
+### 19. GITHUB_TOKEN Rate Limit (1,000 req/hr/repo)
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API rate limits documentation
+
+Workflows using the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` are limited to **1,000 requests per hour per repository** (vs 5,000 for PATs). Enterprise Cloud raises this to 15,000.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Self-referential analytics workflows (workflow that queries its own repo's API) hit limits quickly
+- A single matrix workflow with 256 jobs attempting step-level queries can exhaust the budget
+- Secondary rate limit: max **100 concurrent requests** across all endpoints
+- Content-generating requests: max **80 per minute**
+
+**Implication for extraction tools**: External extraction tools using PATs get 5× more headroom than in-workflow analytics.
+
+---
+
+### 20. Step Timing Fields are Optional (nullable)
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation for workflow jobs
+
+The `steps[].started_at` and `steps[].completed_at` fields are typed as `string | null`. They are:
+- **null** for steps in `queued` status
+- **null** for skipped steps (conclusion may still be set)
+- Potentially **null** for steps that errored before starting
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Cannot compute step duration without null checks
+- Aggregating step-level timing across runs requires filtering out nulls
+- A "total job time from steps" calculation will undercount if any step lacks timing
+
+Additionally, the `conclusion` field's **enum values for steps are undocumented** — the docs only list them for jobs.
+
+---
+
+### 21. Workflow Run Listing: 1,000 Result Cap
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation for workflow runs
+
+The "List workflow runs" endpoint returns **at most 1,000 results** per search filter combination (actor, branch, check_suite_id, created, event, head_sha, status).
+
+**Consequences**:
+- High-frequency repositories (>1,000 runs/day on a branch) lose historical data
+- Cannot paginate past 1,000 without changing filter parameters
+- Must use `created` date ranges to window through history
+- Time-series analytics must implement incremental collection (never go back more than 1,000 runs without date windowing)
+
+---
+
+### 22. Workflow Timing Endpoint Deprecation
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation
+
+The `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/{run_id}/timing` endpoint is **"in the process of closing down"** (deprecation notice in docs).
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Tools relying on this endpoint for billable-minutes data will break
+- No announced replacement for aggregate run timing at the API level
+- Must compute timing from individual job `started_at`/`completed_at` instead
+- Billing analytics require alternative approaches
+
+---
+
+### 23. Log ZIP Structure is Undocumented
+
+**Source**: Empirical observation (not documented by GitHub)
+
+The log archive ZIP has no documented internal structure. Empirically it contains:
+- One text file per job, named `<job_name>/<step_number>_<step_name>.txt`
+- Each line prefixed with a timestamp: `2026-06-05T08:00:45.3456789Z <message>`
+- Timestamp uses **nanosecond precision** (9 decimal places)
+- Group markers: `##[group]<name>` and `##[endgroup]`
+- Command markers: `##[error]`, `##[warning]`, `##[debug]`
+
+**Consequences**:
+- No guaranteed stability — format may change without notice
+- Step names in filenames may contain characters that need escaping
+- Long step names may be truncated in filenames
+- Parsing requires regex-based extraction of the timestamp prefix
+- No machine-readable metadata file in the ZIP (no manifest)
+
+---
+
+### 24. No Native Test Result Extraction
+
+**Source**: GitHub Community feature requests
+
+GitHub Actions has **no built-in API for test results**. Unlike Azure DevOps (which has a Test Results API), GitHub requires:
+- Uploading JUnit XML as artifacts, then downloading and parsing them
+- Using the Checks API to post annotations (limited to 50 per step)
+- Third-party actions (dorny/test-reporter, EnricoMi/publish-unit-test-result-action)
+
+**Consequences**:
+- No standard location or format for test results
+- Each repository uses different actions/patterns for test reporting
+- Analytics tools must support multiple test result storage patterns
+- The 50-annotation limit per check run step truncates results for large test suites
+
+---
+
+### 25. Workflow Re-run Attempt Tracking Gap in Analytics Tools
+
+**Source**: [CIAnalyzer#966](https://github.com/Kesin11/CIAnalyzer/issues/966)
+
+Most CI analytics tools only capture the **final attempt** of a workflow run, missing the history of prior failures.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Flaky test detection is impaired (cannot see fail→pass patterns within a single run)
+- MTTR metrics are skewed (successful re-run looks like a fast fix)
+- Failure categorization loses context (was it infrastructure vs code?)
+- To get all attempts: must query `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/{run_id}/attempts/{attempt_number}` for each attempt
+
+---
+
+### 26. 500 Internal Server Error on Log Deletion
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation
+
+The `DELETE /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/{run_id}/logs` endpoint can return **500 Internal Server Error**, potentially leaving orphaned log data.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Cleanup automation cannot rely on deletion succeeding
+- Orphaned logs may continue consuming storage quota
+- Retry logic must distinguish between transient 500s and permanent failures
+
+---
+
+### 27. Secondary Rate Limit: CPU Time Constraint
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API rate limits documentation
+
+Beyond request-count limits, GitHub enforces a **90 seconds of CPU time per 60 seconds of real time** constraint.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Complex search queries or large response payloads consume more "CPU time"
+- Extraction tools making many filter-heavy queries may hit this before hitting request count limits
+- No clear visibility into how much CPU budget remains
+- Manifests as 403 with a `retry-after` header, not 429
+
+---
+
+### 28. `exclude_pull_requests` Creates Empty Array Instead of Omitting Field
+
+**Source**: GitHub REST API documentation observation
+
+When `exclude_pull_requests=true` is set, the response still includes `pull_requests: []` rather than omitting the field entirely.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Consumers using presence-checking (`if "pull_requests" in response`) get false positives
+- Must check both field existence AND array length
+- Minor but causes bugs in naive extraction code
+
+---
+
+### 29. Maximum 256 Jobs Per Workflow Run (Matrix Limit)
+
+**Source**: GitHub Actions limits documentation
+
+Matrix strategies generate at most **256 jobs per workflow run**.
+
+**Consequences for extraction**:
+- Each job requires a separate API call for step-level data
+- A single workflow run can require 256+ API calls to fully extract (jobs + steps + logs)
+- Combined with the 1,000 req/hr/repo limit for GITHUB_TOKEN, a single large workflow extraction can take significant time
+- Log ZIP for a 256-job workflow can be very large
+
+---
+
+### 30. Workflow Run Execution Time: 35-Day Maximum
+
+**Source**: GitHub Actions limits documentation
+
+A workflow run (including wait/approval time) can last up to **35 days**. Individual jobs are capped at **6 hours** (hosted) or **5 days** (self-hosted).
+
+**Consequences for extraction**:
+- A "running" workflow may be in that state for weeks (waiting for environment approval)
+- Extraction tools must handle extremely long-lived runs gracefully
+- Polling for completion at fixed intervals is wasteful for approval-gated workflows
+- Must distinguish "actively running" from "waiting for approval" — both show as `in_progress`
+
+---
+
+### 31. No Step-Level Artifacts Association
+
+**Source**: Design analysis
+
+While GitHub's API associates artifacts with workflow runs and (since v4) with specific jobs, there is **no step-level artifact association** in the API.
+
+**Consequences**:
+- Cannot determine which step within a job produced an artifact purely from the API
+- Must infer from step names, artifact names, or log parsing
+- Multiple steps in the same job uploading artifacts creates ambiguity
+- The `upload-artifact` action outputs help but are only available within the same workflow run
+
+---
+
+## Summary of Severity
+
+| Issue | Severity | Workaround Available? |
+|-------|----------|----------------------|
+| Artifact URL timing | High | Yes (v4 outputs) |
+| Check suite randomness | High | Partial (separate apps) |
+| 1,000 result cap | High | Yes (date windowing) |
+| Step timing nullability | Medium | Yes (null filtering) |
+| Log ZIP undocumented format | Medium | No (reverse-engineer) |
+| Run ID int64 overflow | Medium | Yes (manual type fix) |
+| Timing endpoint deprecation | High | Yes (compute from jobs) |
+| Re-run log incompleteness | Medium | Yes (per-attempt download) |
+| GITHUB_TOKEN rate limit | Medium | Yes (use PAT) |
+| No test result API | High | Partial (third-party actions) |
+| Artifact URL 1-min expiry | Medium | Yes (immediate download) |
+| Secondary CPU rate limit | Low | Yes (slower requests) |
+| 256 job extraction cost | Medium | Yes (batch with backoff) |
+
+---
+
+## Recommendations for Implementation
+
+1. **Use date-windowed incremental collection** — never attempt to list >1,000 runs in one query
+2. **Track `run_attempt` and download all attempts** — critical for flaky test detection
+3. **Download artifacts immediately after URL retrieval** — 1-minute expiry is unforgiving
+4. **Store raw log ZIP and parse offline** — don't rely on undocumented format stability
+5. **Use PATs over GITHUB_TOKEN** for extraction tools — 5× rate limit headroom
+6. **Implement exponential backoff with jitter** — both primary (429) and secondary (403 + retry-after) limits
+7. **Validate step timing before aggregation** — null `started_at`/`completed_at` is expected
+8. **Capture workflow YAML at extraction time** — workflow definition drift is real
+9. **Handle check suite randomness** — don't assume annotation → workflow run mapping is correct
+10. **Prepare for timing endpoint removal** — compute from job-level timestamps now

+

+
+### Observations on metadata.json
+
+- `ci_system` is NOT an explicit field — inferred from presence of `jenkins_url`
+- Timestamps use `+0000` offset (happens to be UTC here but Jenkins can return local time offsets)
+- `commit_url` is null (Jenkins Blue Ocean frequently doesn't populate this)
+- 5 `git_repos` — Jenkins logs every shared library checkout as a separate BuildData action
+- `changesets` is empty — PR builds don't have SCM changeSets (the PR itself IS the change)
+- `parameters` is empty — this PR build was triggered by a webhook, not manually with params
+- `extracted_at` uses a different format (Python ISO with `+00:00`) vs Jenkins's `+0000`

+### Node Schema (All Fields Present in Every Node)
+
+```json
+{
+    "_class": "io.jenkins.blueocean.rest.impl.pipeline.PipelineNodeImpl",
+    "_links": {
+        "self": {"_class": "io.jenkins.blueocean.rest.hal.Link", "href": "..."},
+        "actions": {"_class": "io.jenkins.blueocean.rest.hal.Link", "href": "..."},
+        "steps": {"_class": "io.jenkins.blueocean.rest.hal.Link", "href": "..."}
+    },
+    "actions": [],
+    "displayDescription": null,
+    "displayName": "Stage Name Here",
+    "durationInMillis": 84698,
+    "id": "15",
+    "input": null,
+    "result": "SUCCESS",
+    "startTime": "2026-06-05T08:00:45.363+0000",
+    "state": "FINISHED",
+    "type": "STAGE",
+    "causeOfBlockage": null,
+    "edges": [
+        {"_class": "io.jenkins.blueocean.rest.impl.pipeline.PipelineNodeImpl$EdgeImpl", "id": "436", "type": "STAGE"}
+    ],
+    "firstParent": null,
+    "restartable": false
+}
+```
+
+### Key Structural Rules
+
+1. **`firstParent` semantics differ by type:**
+   - STAGE nodes: `firstParent` = sequential predecessor (the stage that ran before this one)
+   - PARALLEL nodes: `firstParent` = tree parent (the container STAGE that spawned the parallel branches)
+   - First STAGE node: `firstParent` = null
+
+2. **`edges` are forward-pointing:**
+   - STAGE → next STAGE: single edge
+   - STAGE → PARALLEL branches: multiple edges (one per branch)
+   - PARALLEL → convergence STAGE: edge to the stage after all branches complete
+
+3. **Result/State combinations observed:**
+   - `SUCCESS` / `FINISHED` — ran and passed
+   - `UNSTABLE` / `FINISHED` — ran but had warnings (e.g., CheckmarxOne non-compliance)
+   - `FAILURE` / `FINISHED` — ran and failed
+   - `NOT_BUILT` / `SKIPPED` — skipped due to upstream failure or conditional
+   - `NOT_BUILT` / `NOT_BUILT` — never started (e.g., HaaS Cluster conditional was false)
+
+4. **Convergence points have `firstParent: null`:**
+   Stages 1122, 2161, 2435, 2675, 3186 all have `firstParent: null`. These are stages immediately after parallel branches converge — Blue Ocean doesn't assign them a sequential parent.
+

+
+### Key Differences: WFAPI vs Blue Ocean pipeline_tree.json
+
+| Aspect | Blue Ocean (pipeline_tree.json) | WFAPI (wfapi_describe.json) |
+|--------|-------------------------------|----------------------------|
+| Stage count | 37 nodes | 30 stages |
+| Includes Declarative stages | No | Yes (Checkout SCM, Post Actions) |
+| Parallel representation | PARALLEL as separate nodes with parent | Flattened (Create HaaS Cluster is id=3059, not 3056) |
+| Status vocabulary | `SUCCESS`, `FAILURE`, `UNSTABLE`, `NOT_BUILT` | `SUCCESS`, `FAILED`, `UNSTABLE`, `NOT_EXECUTED` |
+| State vocabulary | `FINISHED`, `SKIPPED`, `NOT_BUILT` | (no state field — status covers both) |
+| Timing | `startTime` (ISO 8601 string), `durationInMillis` | `startTimeMillis` (epoch), `durationMillis`, `pauseDurationMillis` |
+| Hierarchy | `firstParent` + `edges` graph | Flat list (no parent/child info) |
+| Pause time | Not available | Available via `pauseDurationMillis` |
+| Node ID | Numeric string, stable within build | Flow node start ID (matches Blue Ocean for STAGE, differs for PARALLEL) |
+| `execNode` | Not available | Available (Jenkins node label the stage ran on) |

+## 8. The Failed Stage: Start_Barrier_Workload (115 Steps, 4 Failed)
+
+### Failed Steps
+
+**Step 3272 — Shell Script (FAILURE, 190.5s)**
+```
+displayDescription: "#!/bin/bash set -Eeuo pipefail export CCA_STARTER_ENVIRONMENT=barrier
+export HANA_PASSWORD=${HANA_PASSWORD_PSW}
+export DRIVER_CLUSTER_CONFIG=/home/jenkins/agent/workspace/cca-starter_PR-2222/landscape/generated/kubeconfig-ccadb-5c7-orc.yaml
+export HANA_CLUSTER_CONFIG=/home/jenkins/agent/workspace/cca-starter_PR-2222/landscape/generated/kubeconfig-ccahb-5c7-haas.yaml
+export TAG=1.0-5548.0c364e77
+export CCA_TESTS_DIR=cca-tests
+python3 /home/jenkins/agent/workspace/cca-starter_PR-2222/cca-tools/pipeline_utils/starter_barrier/mocking_barrier_call.py"
+```
+
+**Step 3275 — Stash some files to be used later in the build (FAILURE, 37.7s)**
+```
+displayDescription: "container-4e85e168-4224-4140-87e9-7bc305ed2087"
+```
+
+**Step 3290 — Print Message (FAILURE, 13ms)**
+```
+displayDescription: "--- An error occurred in the library step: dockerExecute---
+The error was: hudson.AbortException: script returned exit code 1
+Documentation of library step dockerExecute: https://url.sap/piper/steps/dockerExecute
+Source code: https://github.com/SAP/jenkins-library/blob/master/vars/dockerExecute.groovy
+--- End library step of: dockerExecute ---"
+```
+
+**Step 3336 — Error signal (FAILURE, 33ms)**
+```
+displayDescription: "[testsPublishResults] Some tests failed!"
+```
+
+### Root Cause Chain
+
+1. `mocking_barrier_call.py` script failed with exit code 1 (the actual test failure)
+2. The stash step after it failed (because the container was being torn down)
+3. `dockerExecute` library step caught the error and printed the diagnostic
+4. `testsPublishResults` emitted the final error signal
+
+---
+
+## 9. Step Bubbling Problem — Confirmed
+
+Steps **3335** and **3360** (both `Archive the artifacts`) appear in **every single stage's steps.json** (all 27 stages). These are from the `Declarative: Post Actions` phase that runs at the end of the pipeline.
+
+**How to identify bubbled steps:** Check the `actions[].LogStorageAction._links.self.href` — it contains the true owning node ID:
+```
+/blue/rest/.../nodes/15/steps/3335/log/    → belongs to node 15 (Init)
+/blue/rest/.../nodes/15/steps/3360/log/    → belongs to node 15 (Init)
+```
+
+But these steps appear in `stages/Docker_Build/steps.json`, `stages/Pre-Build_Scans/steps.json`, etc. — they're bubbled up to ALL ancestor stages.
+
+### De-duplication Strategy
+
+For each step in a stage's steps.json:
+1. Extract the node_id from `actions[0]._links.self.href` (parse `/nodes/{node_id}/steps/`)
+2. Compare to the pipeline_tree.json node IDs
+3. The step TRULY belongs to the node in the href, not necessarily the stage directory it appears in
+
+---
+
+## 10. Artifact-to-Stage Mapping Analysis
+
+### The Problem
+
+Jenkins's `/api/json` artifact list provides only `{fileName, relativePath, displayPath}` — **no stage attribution**. The `Archive the artifacts` step has an empty `displayDescription`, so we can't read the include pattern from the step metadata.
+
+### Available Approaches
+
+#### Approach 1: True Node ID from Archive Steps (Stage-Level Attribution)
+
+By filtering `Archive the artifacts` steps and extracting their true node_id from the log href, we get stage-level attribution:
+
+| Step ID | True Node (Stage) | Start Time |
+|---------|-------------------|------------|
+| 1012 | Microdelivery Release Compliance Check (523) | 08:09:50 |
+| 1076, 1079, 1095 | Microdelivery Release Compliance Check (523) | 08:09:59 |
+| 1589 | Pre-Build Scans (1122) | 08:12:27 |
+| 1630, 1732 | Pre-Build Scans (1122) | 08:13:00 - 08:13:28 |
+| 1743-1749 | Pre-Build Scans (1122) | 08:13:31 |
+| 1980, 2039-2091, 2135, 2141 | Pre-Build Scans (1122) | 08:14:08 - 08:14:46 |
+| 2372-2374, 2408, 2412 | DepLayer #1 - push-to-registry-gate (2294) | 08:20:39 - 08:20:48 |
+| 3335, 3360 | Init (15) — Post Actions, bubbled everywhere | 08:53:07 - 08:53:20 |
+
+But: Pre-Build Scans has **18 archive steps** and we don't know which files each one archived.
+
+#### Approach 2: Naming Convention Correlation
+
+Artifact subdirectories embed coordinates that match stage names:
+
+| Artifact Subdirectory | Likely Stage |
+|----------------------|--------------|
+| `checkmarx_one_scan_Main_CCA_Starter_1_0_5548_0c364e77/` | `<sast-gate> [Main:CCA Starter:1.0-5548.0c364e77]` |
+| `hadolint_Main_CCA_Starter_1_0_5548_0c364e77/` | `<hadolint-gate> [Main:CCA Starter:1.0-5548.0c364e77]` |
+| `sonar_scan_Main_CCA_Starter_1_0_5548_0c364e77/` | `<sonar-gate> [Main:CCA Starter:1.0-5548.0c364e77]` |
+| `sbom/dbaas_dbaas_cca_starter_*` | DepLayer / push-to-registry-gate |
+| `checkmarxOne/` | `<sast-gate>` (duplicate of above, different path) |
+| `toolruns/` | `<sast-gate>` (duplicate of above) |
+
+Root-level artifacts (15 files) cannot be attributed this way:
+- `build-type.json`, `changelog.*`, `compliance.html` → likely Microdelivery Release Compliance Check
+- `cobertura-coverage.xml` → likely Unit Tests
+- `influx_data*.json`, `jenkins_data*.json` → likely Post Actions (telemetry upload)
+- `sonarscan.json`, `hadolint.xml` → likely Pre-Build Scans (flattened copies)
+- `xmake_stage.json` → likely DepLayer/push-to-registry-gate
+- `style.css` → likely compliance report companion
+
+#### Approach 3: Per-Step Log Fetch (Not Currently Captured)
+
+Each archive step's log would contain the actual `archiveArtifacts` call pattern (e.g., `includes: "checkmarxOne/**"`). This would give exact step→file mapping but requires N additional API calls during capture (27 archive steps for this build, excluding the 2 bubbled post-action ones).
+

+### Per-Stage Files Captured (from manifest)
+
+Each stage gets up to 3 files:
+1. `steps.json` — Blue Ocean step listing (always captured, may be empty `[]`)

+3. `console.log` — stage log (captured only for failed/unstable stages OR all stages depending on capture config)
+
+**Note:** The locally-downloaded inspection does NOT have the `wfapi_describe.json` files in the stage subdirectories (they exist in HDLF but weren't downloaded to /tmp). The manifest confirms they were captured.

+## 13. Console Log Format
+
+### Structure
+```
+[2026-06-05T08:00:17.820Z] Pull request #2222 opened
+[2026-06-05T08:00:17.982Z] Connecting to https://github.wdf.sap.corp/api/v3 using Technical User for GitHub
+[2026-06-05T08:00:18.832Z] Obtained Jenkinsfile from 0c364e77fb6f681ec135ef06ae17220d7bfccb83
+...
+[2026-06-05T09:03:26.121Z] ERROR: script returned exit code 1
+[2026-06-05T09:03:26.607Z] GitHub has been notified of this commit's build result
+[2026-06-05T09:03:26.858Z] Finished: FAILURE
+```
+
+### Key Observations
+
+- Every line prefixed with `[ISO-8601-timestamp]` (UTC, Z suffix)
+- `[Pipeline]` markers indicate Jenkins pipeline DSL step boundaries
+- Error messages at end: `ERROR: script returned exit code 1`
+- Final line: `Finished: FAILURE`
+- 20,368 lines total (2.87 MB)
+- Contains ALL stage logs concatenated — this is the full build output

+## 14. Issues and Observations for Unified Schema
+
+### Timestamp Inconsistency
+
+| Source | Format | Example |
+|--------|--------|---------|
+| `metadata.json` timestamps | ISO 8601 + offset | `2026-06-05T08:00:17.820+0000` |
+| `metadata.json` extracted_at | Python ISO + offset | `2026-06-05T11:51:15.558265+00:00` |
+| Blue Ocean node `startTime` | ISO 8601 + offset | `2026-06-05T08:00:45.363+0000` |
+| WFAPI `startTimeMillis` | Epoch milliseconds | `1780646444802` |
+| Classic API `timestamp` | Epoch milliseconds | `1780646417812` |
+| Console log timestamps | ISO 8601 UTC (Z) | `[2026-06-05T08:00:17.820Z]` |
+
+All happen to be UTC in this build, but Jenkins instances configured with local time will produce different offsets.
+
+### Step Bubbling (Blue Ocean Behavior)
+
+Steps from post-actions and certain late-pipeline steps bubble up to ALL ancestor nodes. In this inspection:
+- Steps 3335 and 3360 appear in all 27 stage directories
+- The log href reveals their true owner (node 15 / Init — which hosts post-actions)
+- Any consumer must de-duplicate by checking the href's node_id against the stage's node_id
+
+### WFAPI vs Blue Ocean ID Mismatch for PARALLEL Nodes
+
+| Concept | Blue Ocean ID | WFAPI ID |
+|---------|--------------|----------|
+| Create CCA Cluster | 3057 | 3061 |
+| Create HaaS Cluster | 3056 | 3059 |
+
+The unified schema cannot blindly assume IDs are consistent between the two sources.
+
+### Empty Captures
+
+Stages that never ran still produce files in HDLF:
+- `console.log` → 0 bytes
+- `steps.json` → 0 bytes (empty array `[]`)
+- `wfapi_describe.json` → non-empty (contains the stage metadata even if NOT_EXECUTED)
+
+### Duplicate Artifacts
+
+Several artifacts exist at both root level AND in subdirectories:
+- `Cx1_SASTReport_*.json` at root AND in `checkmarx_one_scan_*/`
+- `sonarscan.json` at root AND in `sonar_scan_*/`
+- `piper_checkmarxone_report.*` in both `checkmarxOne/` and `checkmarx_one_scan_*/`
+
+This is a Piper library behavior — it archives files both to a tool-specific directory and to the build root for backward compatibility.
+
+### build_causes.json and build_params.json Are Nearly Empty
+
+The `?tree=actions[causes[*]]` and `?tree=actions[parameters[*]]` queries return all 68 actions but only show the `_class` field for most (the tree filter only expands matching fields). The actual useful data is:
+- Causes: one entry `"Pull request #2222 opened"`
+- Parameters: zero (PR build, no manual params)
+
+These could be inlined into `pipeline_run.json` in the unified schema.

index 00000000..3a59e6da
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/azure-devops-extractor/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,994 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps API client
+
+The system SHALL provide an `AzureDevOpsClient` async context manager (parallel to
+`JenkinsAPIClient`) that authenticates via PAT token (Basic auth with empty username)
+and exposes the following methods:
+
+- `get_build(build_id)` → `RawAdoBuild`
+- `get_timeline(build_id)` → `RawAdoTimeline`
+- `get_log(build_id, log_id)` → `bytes`
+- `list_test_runs(build_id)` → `list[RawAdoTestRun]`
+- `get_test_results(run_id)` → `list[RawAdoTestResult]`
+- `list_artifacts(build_id)` → `list[RawAdoArtifact]`
+
+All HTTP methods SHALL use retry with exponential backoff on 408, 429, and 5xx.
+
+#### Scenario: Client lifecycle
+
+- **GIVEN** valid Azure DevOps credentials in the vault
+- **WHEN** the client is used as `async with AzureDevOpsClient.from_vault(pipeline_url) as client:`
+- **THEN** it SHALL create an `aiohttp.ClientSession` with auth headers on enter and close it on exit
+
+#### Scenario: URL parsing
+
+- **GIVEN** a pipeline URL like `https://dev.azure.com/{org}/{project}/_build/results?buildId={id}`
+- **WHEN** the client is constructed
+- **THEN** it SHALL extract `organization`, `project`, and `build_id` from the URL and use them to build API endpoint URLs
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps URL validator
+
