+The system SHALL add an `AzureDevOpsUrlValidator` to the `VALIDATORS` list in
+`pipeline_url_validators.py` that recognizes Azure DevOps pipeline URLs.
+
+#### Scenario: Modern URL format
+
+- **WHEN** a URL contains `dev.azure.com` and `/_build/results`
+- **THEN** `matches()` SHALL return `True`
+- **AND** `normalize()` SHALL extract org/project/buildId into a canonical form
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy URL format
+
+- **WHEN** a URL matches `{org}.visualstudio.com/{project}/_build/results?buildId={id}`
+- **THEN** `matches()` SHALL return `True`
+- **AND** `normalize()` SHALL convert it to the equivalent `dev.azure.com` canonical form
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Timeline-based stage discovery
+
+The system SHALL use the Azure DevOps Timeline API as the primary discovery mechanism
+for stages, jobs, and tasks. The timeline returns the full hierarchy in a single call
+(unlike Jenkins which requires per-node step fetches).
+
+#### Scenario: Timeline record hierarchy
+
+- **GIVEN** a timeline response with records of types `Stage`, `Phase`, `Job`, and `Task`
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the system SHALL reconstruct the hierarchy using `parentId` references:
+  - Stage → contains Jobs (via parentId)
+  - Job → contains Tasks (via parentId)
+  - Phase is treated as an intermediate grouping layer between Stage and Job
+
+#### Scenario: Mapping timeline types to unified schema
+
+- **WHEN** producing `stages.json` from Azure DevOps timeline records
+- **THEN** the system SHALL map as follows:
+  - Timeline record `type: "Stage"` → unified Stage with `type="stage"`
+  - Timeline record `type: "Phase"` → unified Stage with `type="phase"`
+  - Timeline record `type: "Job"` → unified Stage with `type="job"` and `parent_stage_id` referencing the containing Stage
+  - Timeline record `type: "Task"` → unified Step (written to `steps/<stage_id>.json`)
+
+#### Scenario: Required execution timeline aggregate
+
+- **WHEN** producing unified files from Azure DevOps timeline records
+- **THEN** the system SHALL also write `execution_timeline.json` at the inspection root
+- **AND** the aggregate SHALL include the same normalized `PipelineRun`, `Stage`, `Step`, `JobDetail`, `ParallelGroup`, and `Artifact` objects written to the separate unified files
+- **AND** `steps_by_stage` SHALL include Task-derived Step entries keyed by the same stage or job ids used in `steps/<stage_id>.json`
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Failure detection independent of stage result
+
+The system SHALL NOT rely solely on a stage's `result` field to identify failures.
+Azure DevOps does not propagate task-level failures to the parent stage in several
+well-known configurations. The capture plan SHALL use a multi-signal approach.
+
+#### Background: Why stage.result is unreliable
+
+Azure DevOps timeline records exhibit non-intuitive result propagation:
+
+| Configuration | Task `result` | Job `result` | Stage `result` | Actual state |
+|---------------|---------------|--------------|----------------|--------------|
+| Normal failure | `failed` | `failed` | `failed` | Genuine failure |
+| `continueOnError: true` | `succeededWithIssues` | `succeededWithIssues` | `succeededWithIssues` | **Task failed but was swallowed** |
+| Task with `condition: always()` fails | `failed` | `succeeded` | `succeeded` | **Failure hidden from stage** |
+| Task with `condition: succeededOrFailed()` fails | `failed` | `succeeded` | `succeeded` | **Failure hidden from stage** |
+| Post-checkout/cleanup task fails | `failed` | `succeeded` | `succeeded` | **System task failure hidden** |
+| `finally` block task fails | `failed` | `succeeded` (if main tasks passed) | `succeeded` | **Failure in cleanup hidden** |
+
+The root cause: Azure DevOps only propagates failures upward from tasks that the
+pipeline *depends on for its logical outcome*. Conditional tasks, decorating tasks,
+and `continueOnError` tasks are considered non-blocking — their failures do not flip
+the parent result.
+
+#### Scenario: Detect failures via child task scan
+
+- **GIVEN** a Stage timeline record with `result: "succeeded"`
+- **WHEN** any Task record under that stage (direct or transitive child via parentId chain) has `result: "failed"`
+- **THEN** the capture plan SHALL treat that stage as having failures
+- **AND** SHALL mark those task logs as priority items
+
+#### Scenario: Detect failures via issues array
+
+- **GIVEN** a Task timeline record with `result: "succeededWithIssues"` (continueOnError case)
+- **WHEN** the record's `issues` array contains any entry with `"type": "error"`
+- **THEN** the system SHALL treat that task as failed for capture prioritization
+- **AND** SHALL persist the issues into `issues.json` for the containing stage
+
+#### Scenario: Detect failures via errorCount
+
+- **GIVEN** a timeline record with `errorCount > 0`
+- **WHEN** the record's `result` is NOT `"failed"` (e.g., `"succeededWithIssues"` or `"succeeded"`)
+- **THEN** the system SHALL still mark the stage as having failures and prioritize its logs
+
+#### Scenario: succeededWithIssues maps to UNSTABLE
+
+- **GIVEN** a stage or task with `result: "succeededWithIssues"`
+- **WHEN** producing the unified status
+- **THEN** it SHALL map to unified status `UNSTABLE`
+- **AND** the stage SHALL be treated as a failure candidate for capture prioritization (same priority as FAILURE)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Structured issues extraction
+
+The system SHALL extract the `issues[]` array from timeline records and persist it
+as structured data in the HDLF inspection folder. This is a key advantage over
+Jenkins where equivalent information requires log parsing.
+
+#### Scenario: Issues persisted per stage
+
+- **WHEN** a stage (or any task within it) has a non-empty `issues` array
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write `provider_raw/azure_devops/issues/<stage_id>.json`
+  containing a plain JSON array of provider-neutral issue items aggregated from all tasks in that stage
+- **AND** each issue item SHALL include `type`, `message`, `category`, `source_record`, and `source_line`
+- **AND** `source_record` SHALL identify the timeline record that carried the issue
+- **AND** `source_line` SHALL be `null` because Azure DevOps timeline issues do not carry log line numbers
+- **AND** the unified `stages.json` entry SHALL have its `failure_messages: list[str]` field populated from issues where `type == "error"` (see unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md — "Stage failure_messages field" requirement)
+
+#### Scenario: Issues used for early failure classification
+
+- **WHEN** a timeline record contains `issues` with `type: "error"` and a `message` field
+- **THEN** those messages SHALL be persisted to the database (via `persist_failed_stages()`)
+  without waiting for log download
+- **AND** handler orchestration MAY begin based on structured issues alone (before logs are fully captured)
+
+#### Scenario: Issue fields preserved in provider issue files
+
+- **GIVEN** an issue object with fields `type` (error/warning), `message`, and `category`
+- **WHEN** writing the issues file
+- **THEN** all three fields SHALL be preserved verbatim (not just the message)
+- **AND** `category` values (e.g., "General", "Code", "Test") SHALL be available for downstream classification
+- **AND** the derived provider issue item SHALL include `source_record` with the source timeline record name
+- **AND** the derived provider issue item SHALL include `source_line: null`
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Multi-signal priority function
+
+The system SHALL implement a `has_failures(stage, all_records)` function that
+determines whether a stage contains failures for capture prioritization purposes.
+
+```python
+def has_failures(stage: TimelineRecord, all_records: list[TimelineRecord]) -> bool:
+    """Determine if a stage contains failures, regardless of its own result.
+
+    Azure DevOps does not propagate task failures to the stage when:
+    - continueOnError is set (task shows succeededWithIssues, not failed)
+    - The task runs under condition: always() or succeededOrFailed()
+    - The task is a post-checkout or cleanup task injected by the system
+
+    This function checks all signals, not just stage.result.
+    """
+```
+
+#### Scenario: Priority assignment uses multi-signal detection
+
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the priority of stage logs SHALL be determined by:
+  1. `stage.result in ("failed", "canceled", "abandoned")` → priority
+  2. `stage.result == "succeededWithIssues"` → priority
+  3. Any child task has `result == "failed"` → priority
+  4. Any child task has `errorCount > 0` → priority
+  5. Any child task has `issues` containing `type: "error"` → priority
+  6. None of the above → non-priority (only captured if cap permits)
+
+#### Scenario: Transitive child lookup
+
+- **WHEN** checking child tasks for a Stage
+- **THEN** the lookup SHALL follow the full parentId chain (Stage → Job → Task),
+  not just direct children
+- **AND** intermediate nodes (Phase, Job) that themselves have `result: "failed"`
+  SHALL also trigger priority
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps capture plan
+
+The system SHALL produce a `list[CaptureItem]` from Azure DevOps timeline data using
+the same `CaptureItem` model as Jenkins extraction. The capture plan SHALL be
+compatible with the existing CI-agnostic downloader.
+
+#### Scenario: Priority items captured first
+
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the following items SHALL be marked `is_priority=True`:
+  1. `build.json` — full build API response
+  2. `timeline.json` — full timeline response
+  3. Per-stage issues (already extracted during plan construction)
+  4. Log entries for all tasks within stages identified by `has_failures()`
+  5. Test result files for all test runs associated with the build
+
+#### Scenario: Non-priority items
+
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the following items SHALL be marked `is_priority=False`:
+  1. Logs from stages where `has_failures()` returns False
+  2. Artifact downloads
+  3. Test run metadata (when test results themselves are priority)
+
+#### Scenario: Log URLs derived from timeline
+
+- **GIVEN** a Task timeline record with `log: {"id": 5, "url": "https://..."}`
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the system SHALL use the `log.url` directly as the `source_url` for the CaptureItem
+- **AND** SHALL set `is_console_log=True` (content may be large)
+
+#### Scenario: Stages without log references
+
+- **GIVEN** a Task timeline record with `log: null`
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** no CaptureItem SHALL be created for that task's log (it has no downloadable content)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Test results extraction
+
+The system SHALL extract test results from the Azure DevOps Test Results API, which
+is a separate endpoint from the build/timeline APIs.
+
+#### Scenario: Test run discovery
+
+- **GIVEN** a completed build
+- **WHEN** building the capture plan
+- **THEN** the system SHALL query `GET /_apis/test/runs?buildId={buildId}` to discover associated test runs
+- **AND** for each test run, create a CaptureItem for the results endpoint
+
+#### Scenario: Test result fields mapped to unified format
+
+- **WHEN** producing test result data
+- **THEN** the following fields SHALL be extracted:
+  - `outcome` → mapped to unified Status (Failed → FAILURE, Passed → SUCCESS, etc.)
+  - `errorMessage` — failure description
+  - `stackTrace` — stack trace if available
+  - `automatedTestName` — fully qualified test identifier
+  - `durationInMs` — test execution time
+  - `failureType` — ADO's classification (Known Issue, New Issue, Regression)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps metadata extraction
+
+The system SHALL extract pipeline metadata from the build endpoint for `metadata.json`.
+
+#### Scenario: Build metadata mapped
+
+- **WHEN** the metadata extractor processes an Azure DevOps URL
+- **THEN** it SHALL populate `metadata.json` with:
+  - `ci_system`: `"azure_devops"`
+  - `commit_id`: from `build.sourceVersion`
+  - `branch`: from `build.sourceBranch` with `refs/heads/` prefix stripped
+  - `is_pr`: `True` when `build.reason == "pullRequest"`
+  - `pr_number`: extracted from `refs/pull/{N}/merge` in sourceBranch
+  - `pr_url`: constructed from org/project/pullRequestId
+  - `trigger_reason`: from `build.reason` (manual, individualCI, pullRequest, schedule, etc.)
+  - `queue_time`, `start_time`, `finish_time`: from build timestamps, normalized to ISO 8601 UTC
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Extractor dispatch by CI system
+
+The system SHALL dispatch to the appropriate extraction logic based on the detected
+CI system.
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps pipeline detected
+
+- **GIVEN** a pipeline URL that matches the AzureDevOpsUrlValidator
+- **WHEN** `extract_data_activity()` dispatches an Azure DevOps inspection
+- **THEN** it SHALL use `AzureDevOpsClient` and Azure DevOps capture-plan builders instead of the Jenkins equivalents
+- **AND** the downstream download + manifest logic SHALL remain unchanged (it is CI-agnostic)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Pydantic models for Azure DevOps API responses
+
+The system SHALL define Raw Pydantic models for Azure DevOps API responses following
+the project's two-layer pattern (Raw models → Output models).
+
+#### Scenario: Timeline record model
+
+- **GIVEN** a timeline API response
+- **WHEN** parsing records
+- **THEN** the `RawTimelineRecord` model SHALL include:
+  - `id: str`
+  - `parent_id: str | None` (aliased from `parentId`)
+  - `type: str` (Stage, Phase, Job, Task)
+  - `name: str`
+  - `state: str` (pending, inProgress, completed)
+  - `result: str | None` (succeeded, succeededWithIssues, failed, canceled, skipped, abandoned)
+  - `start_time: str | None`
+  - `finish_time: str | None`
+  - `issues: list[RawTimelineIssue]`
+  - `error_count: int` (aliased from `errorCount`)
+  - `warning_count: int` (aliased from `warningCount`)
+  - `log: RawLogReference | None` (with `id: int` and `url: str`)
+  - `task: RawTaskReference | None` (with `id: str`, `name: str`, `version: str`)
+  - `attempt: int`
+  - `identifier: str | None` (consistent across retries)
+  - `worker_name: str | None`
+  - `order: int | None`
+
+#### Scenario: Timeline issue model
+
+- **GIVEN** an issue entry in a timeline record
+- **WHEN** parsing
+- **THEN** the `RawTimelineIssue` model SHALL include:
+  - `type: Literal["error", "warning"]`
+  - `message: str`
+  - `category: str | None`
+
+#### Scenario: Build response model
+
+- **GIVEN** a build API response
+- **WHEN** parsing
+- **THEN** the `RawAdoBuild` model SHALL include:
+  - `id: int`
+  - `build_number: str` (aliased from `buildNumber`)
+  - `status: str`
+  - `result: str | None`
+  - `queue_time: str` (aliased from `queueTime`)
+  - `start_time: str` (aliased from `startTime`)
+  - `finish_time: str | None` (aliased from `finishTime`)
+  - `reason: str`
+  - `source_branch: str` (aliased from `sourceBranch`)
+  - `source_version: str` (aliased from `sourceVersion`)
+  - `definition: RawBuildDefinition` (with `id`, `name`)
+  - `project: RawProjectReference` (with `name`, `id`)
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact response model
+
+- **GIVEN** an artifacts API response from `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/artifacts`
+- **WHEN** parsing
+- **THEN** the `RawAdoArtifact` model SHALL include:
+  - `id: int` — artifact ID
+  - `name: str` — artifact container name (e.g., "drop", "TestResults", "CodeCoverageReport")
+  - `source: str | None` — ⚠️ the ID of the **job timeline record** that produced this artifact. This is the direct link from artifact to producing code. If an artifact is associated with multiple sources, this points to the first source.
+  - `resource: RawArtifactResource`
+
+- **AND** the `RawArtifactResource` model SHALL include:
+  - `type: str` — resource type ("Container", "FilePath", "VersionControl", "UNCPath")
+  - `data: str` — type-specific identifier (e.g., container path, drop location)
+  - `download_url: str` (aliased from `downloadUrl`) — direct download link
+  - `url: str` — API metadata URL
+  - `properties: dict[str, Any]` — type-specific properties (may include `artifactsize`, `HashType`, etc.)
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact source field used for stage attribution
+
+- **GIVEN** an artifact with `source: "job-record-id-abc"`
+- **WHEN** building the unified `artifacts.json`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL:
+  1. Look up `source` in the timeline records to find the Job-type record
+  2. Set `producing_stage_id` to the corresponding Stage entry in `stages.json` (the job's stage ID)
+  3. Scan `steps/<job_stage_id>.json` for a step whose task name matches `PublishBuildArtifacts` or `PublishPipelineArtifact` — set `producing_step_id` to that step's ID
+  4. Set attribution confidence to `"high"` (direct API link, not heuristic)
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact with null source
+
+- **GIVEN** an artifact where `source` is `null` or empty
+- **WHEN** building the unified `artifacts.json`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL set `producing_stage_id = None` and `candidate_stage_ids = None`
+- **AND** log a warning that attribution is unavailable for this artifact
+
+---
+
+## Design Notes
+
+### Why this multi-signal approach is necessary
+
+Jenkins Blue Ocean has simpler failure semantics: if a step fails, the stage is
+marked FAILURE (even with `catchError`, the *stage* still shows red in Blue Ocean —
+only the *build* result changes). There is no Jenkins equivalent of "stage succeeded
+but a child task actually failed."
+
+Azure DevOps intentionally decouples task outcomes from stage outcomes for
+flexibility (conditional cleanup, optional quality gates, etc.). This is a feature,
+not a bug — but it means automated analysis tools cannot trust the stage-level result
+as the sole failure signal.
+
+### Performance consideration
+
+The multi-signal approach does NOT require additional API calls. The timeline
+endpoint returns all records (stages, jobs, tasks) with their `issues`, `errorCount`,
+and `result` in a single response. The failure detection is pure in-memory traversal
+of the already-fetched timeline data.
+
+### Compatibility with existing downloader
+
+The `CaptureItem` model and `download_items()` function are already CI-agnostic — they
+take a URL and an auth header. The Azure DevOps capture plan produces the same
+`list[CaptureItem]` output, so no changes are needed to the downloader, size cap logic,
+or streaming infrastructure.
+
+---
+
+## Additional Bottlenecks and Edge Cases
+
+The following documents non-obvious behaviors discovered through the official Azure
+DevOps documentation and known practitioner pitfalls. Each requires explicit handling
+in the extractor to avoid silent data loss or misclassification.
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 1: Cancellation produces incomplete timeline records
+
+**Source:** [Pipeline runs — Timeouts and disconnects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/runs?view=azure-devops)
+
+When a build is canceled (manually or via timeout):
+- The server signals the agent to stop
+- The agent has a `cancelTimeoutInMinutes` grace period (default 5 min) to finish
+  "always-run" tasks and upload remaining results
+- After the grace period, if the agent hasn't responded, the job is force-marked as
+  `failed` — **but the timeline records may be incomplete**
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Timeline records for in-progress tasks may have `state: "inProgress"` with NO
+  `finishTime` and NO `result` (null)
+- The `log` reference may point to a partially-written log (truncated mid-line)
+- Some tasks may never appear in the timeline at all if cancellation happened before
+  the agent reported them
+
+**Required handling:**
+
+```python
+def _normalize_canceled_record(record: RawTimelineRecord) -> RawTimelineRecord:
+    """Fill in missing fields for records orphaned by cancellation.
+
+    When a build is canceled, in-progress tasks may never receive a final
+    state update. The timeline will show state=inProgress, result=None,
+    finishTime=None. We normalize these to result="canceled" so downstream
+    consumers have a definitive terminal state (mapped to unified ABORTED).
+    """
+    if record.state == "inProgress" and record.result is None:
+        # Build is completed but record never got a final update
+        record = record.model_copy(update={"result": "canceled"})
+    return record
+```
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 2: Agent disconnect leaves phantom records
+
+**Source:** [Pipeline runs — Timeouts and disconnects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/runs?view=azure-devops)
+
+If an agent loses connectivity (power loss, network failure):
+- The server waits for 5 consecutive missed heartbeats (5 minutes)
+- Then marks the job as `failed`
+- **But:** task-level timeline records from before the disconnect are preserved
+  with their last-known state — they are NOT retroactively updated to `failed`
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- You may see a Job record with `result: "failed"` containing Task records where
+  ALL tasks show `result: "succeeded"` — because the failure was infrastructure
+  (disconnect), not any individual task
+- The `log` for the currently-running task at disconnect time may be incomplete
+  or empty (never uploaded by the agent)
+- No `issues[]` will be present — the failure mode has no structured error
+
+**Required handling:**
+- When a Job has `result: "failed"` but ALL child tasks show `result: "succeeded"`,
+  treat this as an infrastructure failure (agent disconnect / timeout)
+- Persist a synthetic issue: `{"type": "error", "message": "Job failed with no
+  task-level failure — probable agent disconnect or timeout", "category": "Infrastructure"}`
+- Do NOT prioritize individual task logs (they completed successfully) — instead
+  prioritize the job-level metadata
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 3: Stage retries produce multiple timelines with `attempt` numbers
+
+**Source:** [Timeline API — TimelineAttempt](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/build/timeline/get?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1) and [Stage manual reruns](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/stages?view=azure-devops)
+
+When a stage is manually rerun or uses `retryCountOnTaskFailure`:
+- Each attempt gets its own `timelineId` (a separate sub-timeline)
+- The main timeline contains the record for the LATEST attempt only
+- `previousAttempts` array on the record lists earlier attempt timelines
+- The `attempt` field on each record indicates which attempt number it is
+- The `identifier` field is consistent ACROSS attempts (same logical step)
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- If you only fetch the main timeline, you see the latest attempt's result —
+  which may be `succeeded` after a retry, hiding the original failure
+- The original failure's logs and issues are in a DIFFERENT timeline
+  (referenced via `previousAttempts[].timelineId`)
+- For failure analysis, we typically want the FIRST failure, not the successful retry
+
+**Required handling:**
+
+```python
+async def _fetch_all_attempts(
+    client: AzureDevOpsClient,
+    build_id: int,
+    record: RawTimelineRecord,
+) -> list[RawTimeline]:
+    """Fetch timeline data for all attempts of a retried stage.
+
+    The main timeline only shows the latest attempt. For failure analysis,
+    we need the original failure — its logs and issues live in a separate
+    timeline referenced by previousAttempts[].timelineId.
+    """
+    timelines = []
+    for prev in record.previous_attempts or []:
+        timeline = await client.get_timeline(build_id, timeline_id=prev.timeline_id)
+        timelines.append(timeline)
+    return timelines
+```
+
+- Write all attempt timelines to `provider_raw/azure_devops/attempts/`
+- In the unified `stages.json`, include `attempt: int` and `total_attempts: int`
+- Mark stages where `total_attempts > 1` for special handling by fault handlers
+  (the retry itself is signal — it means the first attempt failed)
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 4: `condition: always()` steps run AND FAIL without affecting parent result
+
+**Source:** [Pipeline conditions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/conditions?view=azure-devops)
+
+The conditions documentation explicitly confirms:
+- A step with `condition: always()` runs even if the build is canceled
+- A step with `condition: failed()` runs only when a previous dependency fails
+- **Critical:** "A successful cleanup step doesn't save the job from failing.
+  Jobs can't return to success after failing."
+- **BUT the inverse is also true:** a FAILING cleanup step doesn't make a
+  succeeded job fail
+
+This means `condition: always()` steps are effectively "fire and forget" from the
+perspective of the parent job's result. A deployment notification step, a Slack
+message, a metrics push — all of these can fail silently.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- These failures are genuinely uninteresting for most fault analysis (they're
+  infrastructure/notification noise, not build failures)
+- BUT some teams put important validation in `always()` steps (e.g., deployment
+  verification, smoke tests after deploy) where failures ARE meaningful
+- We cannot distinguish the two cases from the API alone
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Capture all task-level failures regardless of condition semantics
+- In the unified model, add a `is_conditional_failure: bool` flag to Steps where:
+  - The parent Job/Stage result is `succeeded`
+  - The Step itself has `result: "failed"`
+- Let fault handlers decide whether to analyze conditional failures (configurable
+  per handler via handler applicability rules)
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 5: Log line-range API returns 0-indexed but timeline shows 1-indexed references
+
+**Source:** [Builds — Get Build Log API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/build/builds/get-build-log?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)
+
+The log API accepts `startLine` and `endLine` parameters for partial fetching.
+However:
+- The API uses **0-based** line numbering for `startLine`/`endLine`
+- The ADO web UI and some `issues[].data` references use **1-based** line numbers
+- There is NO documented `Content-Range` or total line count header — you must
+  fetch the full log to know its size, or use the log list endpoint which gives
+  byte size but NOT line count
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Cannot do Range-based head+tail truncation like we do for Jenkins console logs
+  (Jenkins supports HTTP Range headers on `/consoleText`)
+- Must fetch full logs and truncate locally
+- OR use `startLine=0&endLine=N` for head and a second request for tail (but we
+  don't know the total lines without fetching first)
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Use the log list endpoint (`GET /builds/{buildId}/logs`) to get byte sizes
+- Apply head_cap based on byte size (same as Jenkins stage log streaming) via
+  standard HTTP streaming with early abort
+- Do NOT rely on `startLine`/`endLine` for truncation — it adds latency
+  (requires knowing total line count) and has off-by-one risk
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 6: Test results pagination cap (max 1000 results per request)
+
+**Source:** [Test Results — List API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/test/results/list?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)
+
+The test results API returns a maximum of:
+- **1000 results** when `detailsToInclude=None`
+- **200 results** when `detailsToInclude=Iterations` or `WorkItems`
+
+There is NO continuation token — pagination uses `$skip` and `$top`.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- A test run with >1000 test cases requires multiple paginated requests
+- Large test suites (common in enterprise Java/C# projects) routinely exceed
+  this limit
+- If you don't paginate, you silently lose test results from the response
+
+**Required handling:**
+
+```python
+async def _fetch_all_test_results(
+    client: AzureDevOpsClient,
+    run_id: int,
+    include_details: bool = False,
+) -> list[RawAdoTestResult]:
+    """Paginate through all test results for a test run.
+
+    The API caps at 1000 results per request without details, or 200 when
+    detailsToInclude is set (e.g., Iterations, WorkItems). Missing pagination
+    silently truncates — no error is returned, just fewer results than exist.
+    """
+    all_results = []
+    skip = 0
+    top = 200 if include_details else 1000
+    while True:
+        batch = await client.get_test_results(
+            run_id, skip=skip, top=top, include_details=include_details
+        )
+        all_results.extend(batch)
+        if len(batch) < top:
+            break  # Last page (includes empty response case)
+        skip += top
+    return all_results
+```
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 7: `build.result` is `None` while build is in-progress
+
+**Source:** [Builds — Get API (BuildResult enum)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/build/builds/get?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)
+
+The `result` field on a Build response is `None`/absent when `status != "completed"`.
+The `BuildResult` enum has a value `none` that means "no result yet" — but the field
+itself may be null (not the string "none") in the JSON response.
+
+Additionally, `partiallySucceeded` (maps to our `UNSTABLE`) is only set at the BUILD
+level, not at individual stage/job levels. A build is `partiallySucceeded` when some
+stages succeeded and some failed — it's a rollup, not a per-stage status.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- We MUST check `build.status == "completed"` before trusting `build.result`
+- If extraction runs on an in-progress build (e.g., triggered too early by a
+  webhook), `result` will be `None` and the timeline will have incomplete records
+- `partiallySucceeded` at the build level does NOT mean any individual stage has
+  that result — stages are either `succeeded` or `failed`
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Validate `build.status == "completed"` at extraction start
+- If not completed, fail with a retryable error (the Temporal activity will retry)
+- Do NOT map build-level `partiallySucceeded` to individual stage results — it's
+  a composite rollup only
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 8: `continueOnError` at JOB level vs STEP level have different semantics
+
+**Source:** [Jobs in Azure Pipelines — YAML schema](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/phases?view=azure-devops)
+
+The `continueOnError` property exists at TWO levels with DIFFERENT effects:
+
+**Job level (`continueOnError: true` on a job):**
+- If THIS job fails, downstream dependent jobs still run
+- The job itself still shows `result: "failed"` in the timeline
+- It affects the DEPENDENCY GRAPH, not the job's own result
+
+**Step level (`continueOnError: true` on a step/task):**
+- If THIS step fails, subsequent steps in the same job still run
+- The step shows `result: "succeededWithIssues"` (NOT `failed`)
+- The job's result becomes `succeededWithIssues` (NOT `failed`)
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Job-level `continueOnError` is invisible in the timeline API — you cannot tell
+  from the timeline alone whether a failed job had `continueOnError` set
+- Step-level `continueOnError` IS visible via the `succeededWithIssues` result
+- The distinction matters for fault classification: a job that failed WITH
+  `continueOnError` might be a known-flaky test suite that the team tolerates
+
+**Required handling:**
+- For step-level: already handled via `succeededWithIssues` → `UNSTABLE` mapping
+- For job-level: when a Job has `result: "failed"` but downstream jobs still ran,
+  infer that `continueOnError` was likely set. Add `inferred_continue_on_error: bool`
+  to the provider_extensions for the stage, so fault handlers can deprioritize
+  these known-tolerated failures
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 9: The `details` field — sub-timelines for complex tasks
+
+**Source:** [Timeline API — TimelineRecord.details](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/build/timeline/get?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)
+
+Some tasks (e.g., deployment jobs, multi-config matrix expansions) create their own
+sub-timeline referenced by the `details` field on their TimelineRecord:
+
+```json
+{
+  "id": "task-123",
+  "type": "Job",
+  "details": {
+    "id": "sub-timeline-uuid",
+    "changeId": 5,
+    "url": "https://dev.azure.com/.../_apis/build/builds/123/timeline/sub-timeline-uuid"
+  }
+}
+```
+
+This is a SEPARATE timeline that must be fetched with its own API call.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- The main timeline will show the Job-level summary (e.g., `result: "failed"`)
+  but the actual task-level detail is in the sub-timeline
+- Without fetching sub-timelines, you lose step-level granularity for deployment
+  jobs and matrix expansions
+- Matrix expansions can produce MANY sub-timelines (one per leg)
+
+**Required handling:**
+- During capture plan construction, check for `details` on Job-type records
+- Fetch sub-timelines for failed jobs only (avoid explosion for large matrices)
+- Store sub-timelines at `provider_raw/azure_devops/sub_timelines/{record_id}.json`
+- For unified `steps/<stage_id>.json`, merge sub-timeline tasks into the step list
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 10: Build-level vs Run-level result mismatch with `partiallySucceeded`
+
+**Source:** [Builds API — BuildResult enum](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/build/builds/get?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)
+
+The `BuildResult` enum includes `partiallySucceeded` but the `TaskResult` enum for
+timeline records does NOT include this value (it has `succeededWithIssues` instead):
+
+| Level | Enum | Values |
+|-------|------|--------|
+| Build | `BuildResult` | `succeeded`, `partiallySucceeded`, `failed`, `canceled` |
+| Timeline record | `TaskResult` | `succeeded`, `succeededWithIssues`, `failed`, `canceled`, `skipped`, `abandoned` |
+
+This means:
+- A build can be `partiallySucceeded` while containing stages that are all either
+  `succeeded` or `failed` — no stage has `partiallySucceeded`
+- `succeededWithIssues` on a timeline record (task/job/stage level) is NOT the same
+  concept as `partiallySucceeded` on the build level
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Map `build.result == "partiallySucceeded"` → unified `UNSTABLE` (build level only)
+- Map `timeline_record.result == "succeededWithIssues"` → unified `UNSTABLE` (task level)
+- These are different enums expressing different concepts but both map to `UNSTABLE`
+  in our unified model — document this explicitly to prevent confusion
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 11: Condition evaluation prevents stages from appearing in timeline
+
+**Source:** [Pipeline conditions — Condition outcomes when a build is canceled](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/pipelines/process/conditions?view=azure-devops)
+
+When a stage's condition evaluates to `false` (e.g., `condition: eq(variables['Build.SourceBranch'], 'refs/heads/main')`
+and the branch is not main):
+- The stage MAY appear in the timeline with `result: "skipped"`
+- OR the stage may NOT appear at all (if it was never evaluated due to parent skip)
+
+Additionally, when a build is canceled:
+- If a stage's condition doesn't include a status check (no `succeeded()`/`failed()`),
+  it may still run after cancellation
+- If a stage IS skipped due to parent cancellation, child jobs/tasks won't appear
+  in the timeline at all
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Cannot rely on timeline stage count matching the pipeline definition's stage count
+- Missing stages are ambiguous: skipped-by-condition vs never-evaluated vs canceled
+- The `ran` field in our unified model needs to account for all three cases
+
+**Required handling:**
+- If a stage appears with `result: "skipped"` → set `ran=false`, `result="SKIPPED"`
+- If a stage from the pipeline definition is ABSENT from the timeline entirely →
+  set `ran=false`, `result="SKIPPED"` with a note in `provider_extensions`
+- To detect absent stages, compare timeline stages against the build definition's
+  stages (requires an additional API call to get the pipeline YAML or definition)
+- For MVP: only analyze stages that ARE present in the timeline; log a warning
+  if fewer stages appear than expected
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 12: Log IDs lack identifying metadata — finding the right log requires content inspection
+
+**Source:** [microsoft/azure-devops-python-api#486](https://github.com/microsoft/azure-devops-python-api/issues/486)
+
+The build logs list endpoint (`GET /builds/{buildId}/logs`) returns log references with
+`id`, `type`, and `url` — but NO task name, stage name, or step identifier. The only way
+to associate a log with a specific task is via the timeline record's `log.id` field.
+
+However, if you have a log ID but no timeline context (e.g., navigating from a UI link),
+you cannot determine what the log belongs to without:
+1. Fetching the full timeline
+2. Searching all records for one whose `log.id` matches
+
+Additionally, log IDs are NOT stable across retries — a retried task gets a new log ID
+in its new attempt's timeline.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- ALWAYS resolve logs via timeline records (record → record.log.id → fetch log)
+- NEVER enumerate the logs list and try to identify logs by content inspection
+  (this is O(n) API calls and extremely slow — reported as 20+ minutes for 16 builds)
+- Log IDs are scoped to the build but NOT to the attempt — must use the correct
+  timeline for the correct attempt
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Build the log fetch plan exclusively from timeline records (already our approach)
+- Cache the timeline in memory during capture plan construction to avoid re-fetching
+- For retried stages, use the attempt-specific timeline to find the correct log IDs
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 13: Rate limiting uses TSTUs with unpredictable cost per request
+
+**Source:** [Rate and usage limits](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/integrate/concepts/rate-limits?view=azure-devops)
+
+Azure DevOps rate limiting is NOT based on simple request-count-per-second. It uses
+**TSTUs (Throughput Units)** — an abstract blend of CPU, memory, database DTUs, and
+storage bandwidth. Key characteristics:
+
+- Each pipeline has a 200 TSTU limit per sliding 5-minute window
+- Different API calls consume different (undocumented) TSTU amounts
+- The `X-RateLimit-Cost` header (when present) reports TSTUs consumed per request
+- When throttled: HTTP 429 with `Retry-After` header (seconds to wait)
+- Throttling manifests as progressive DELAYS before outright blocking — your requests
+  may silently slow down before you get a 429
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Cannot predict throughput capacity from request count alone
+- Log downloads (large responses) likely consume more TSTUs than metadata fetches
+- Parallel log fetching (our `max_concurrency=10` pattern) may trigger throttling
+  faster than expected
+- The `Retry-After` header may appear on HTTP 200 responses (advisory delay) — NOT
+  just on 429s
+
+**Required handling:**
+
+```python
+def _apply_rate_limit_headers(response: aiohttp.ClientResponse) -> float | None:
+    """Check rate limit headers and return advisory delay if throttling is imminent.
+
+    Azure DevOps may include Retry-After on 200 responses as an advisory.
+    X-RateLimit-Remaining approaching 0 means delays are about to start.
+    """
+    retry_after = response.headers.get("Retry-After")
+    if retry_after:
+        return float(retry_after)
+
+    remaining = response.headers.get("X-RateLimit-Remaining")
+    if remaining and float(remaining) < 10:
+        # Proactively slow down before hard throttle kicks in
+        return 1.0  # 1 second backpressure
+
+    return None
+```
+
+- Honor `Retry-After` on ALL responses (not just 429)
+- Monitor `X-RateLimit-Remaining` and reduce concurrency proactively
+- Consider adaptive concurrency: start at 10, reduce to 3-5 if rate limit headers
+  indicate pressure
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 14: Artifact download requires different auth scope than metadata read
+
+**Source:** [microsoft/azure-devops-python-api#508](https://github.com/microsoft/azure-devops-python-api/issues/508)
+
+A PAT with `Build: Read` scope can successfully call `get_artifact()` (metadata) but
+FAILS with `TF400813: not authorized` when calling the artifact content download URL.
+This is a known inconsistency: the metadata endpoint and the download endpoint require
+different permission levels.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- The PAT used for extraction must have BOTH `Build: Read` (for metadata, timeline,
+  logs) AND artifact download permissions
+- The download URL returned by the artifacts API may point to a different service
+  (e.g., Azure Blob Storage with a SAS token, or a different auth domain)
+- Cannot assume that auth working for one endpoint means it works for all
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Validate artifact download permissions during client initialization (test download
+  of a small artifact)
+- If artifact download fails with 401/403, emit `SKIPPED_UNREACHABLE` status for
+  artifacts (same as Jenkins pattern) — don't fail the entire extraction
+- Document required PAT scopes: `Build: Read`, `Test Results: Read`,
+  `Project and Team: Read` (minimum)
+
+---
+
+### Bottleneck 15: `get_test_results_for_build` removed in API v7.1
+
+**Source:** [microsoft/azure-devops-python-api#513](https://github.com/microsoft/azure-devops-python-api/issues/513)
+
+The convenience method `get_test_result_details_for_build` that existed in API v6.0
+was removed in v7.1. To get test results for a build, you must now:
+1. List test runs for the build (`GET /_apis/test/runs?buildUri={buildUri}`)
+2. For each test run, fetch results with pagination (`GET /_apis/test/runs/{runId}/results`)
+
+This is a two-step process where it used to be one.
+
+**Impact on extractor:**
+- Cannot use a single "get all test results for build" call
+- Must discover test runs first, then paginate results per run
+- A single build can have MULTIPLE test runs (one per test task in the pipeline)
+- Test runs may be associated via `buildUri` OR `buildId` — use both query params
+  for reliability
+
+**Required handling:**
+- Implement the two-step discovery: list runs → fetch results per run
+- Combine with Bottleneck 6 (pagination cap at 1000) for each run
+- Store results per test run: `tests/run_{id}_results.json` (preserves run boundary)
+- Emit a combined `tests/all_failures.json` with just failed results for quick access
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps credential retrieval
+
+The system SHALL provide a `get_azure_devops_credentials(pipeline_url: str) -> str` function that retrieves a PAT (Personal Access Token) from HashiCorp Vault for the organization extracted from the pipeline URL.
+
+#### Scenario: Vault path convention
+
+- **GIVEN** a pipeline URL `https://dev.azure.com/myorg/myproject/_build/results?buildId=123`
+- **WHEN** the credential function extracts the organization (`myorg`)
+- **THEN** it SHALL read the PAT from Vault path `<mount>/azure_devops/myorg` with key `pat`
+- **AND** return the PAT string for use in Basic auth headers
+
+#### Scenario: PAT used for Basic auth
+
+- **GIVEN** a valid PAT retrieved from Vault
+- **WHEN** constructing the auth header for Azure DevOps API calls
+- **THEN** the header SHALL be `Authorization: Basic base64(":" + pat)` (empty username, PAT as password)
+
+#### Scenario: Required PAT scopes
+
+- **WHEN** configuring a PAT for the extraction pipeline
+- **THEN** the minimum required scopes SHALL be:
+  - `Build: Read` — for build details, timeline, and log endpoints
+  - `Test Results: Read` — for test run discovery and result pagination
+  - `Project and Team: Read` — for project metadata resolution
+
+#### Scenario: Missing credentials fail-fast
+
+- **WHEN** no credentials exist in Vault for the extracted organization
+- **THEN** the function SHALL raise a `CredentialsNotFoundError` (non-retryable)
+- **AND** the Temporal activity SHALL NOT retry (this requires manual PAT provisioning)
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: FailedStage DB persistence for ADO
+
+The Azure DevOps capture plan construction SHALL persist `FailedStage` rows to the database (same pattern as Jenkins extraction) so that handler orchestration can match handlers without waiting for full data download.
+
+#### Scenario: FailedStage rows populated from timeline issues
+
+- **WHEN** the ADO capture plan builder identifies stages with failures via `has_failures()`
+- **THEN** it SHALL call `persist_failed_stages()` with:
+  - `stage_name`: the Stage timeline record's `name` field
+  - `error_message`: the first `issues[]` entry with `type: "error"` from any child task, or `"Job failed with no task-level failure — probable agent disconnect or timeout"` for infrastructure failures (Bottleneck 2)
+
+#### Scenario: FailedStage populated before log download
+
+- **GIVEN** the ADO timeline provides structured issues without requiring log download
+- **WHEN** the capture plan is being constructed
+- **THEN** `persist_failed_stages()` SHALL be called during capture plan construction (same as Jenkins)
+- **AND** handler orchestration MAY begin applicability matching before logs are fully downloaded
+
+#### Scenario: Multiple error messages consolidated
+
+- **WHEN** a stage has multiple tasks with `type: "error"` issues
+- **THEN** `error_message` SHALL contain the first error message from the highest-priority task (failed tasks before succeededWithIssues tasks)
+- **AND** the complete set of error messages SHALL be available via `failure_messages` in the unified Stage model (after normalization)
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/github-actions-extractor/spec.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/github-actions-extractor/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..e6c59613
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/github-actions-extractor/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,558 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions API client
+
+The system SHALL provide a `GitHubActionsClient` async context manager, parallel
+to `JenkinsAPIClient` and `AzureDevOpsClient`, that authenticates with a GitHub
+App installation token or PAT and supports both github.com and GitHub Enterprise
+hosts.
+
+The client SHALL expose methods for:
+- `get_workflow_run(owner, repo, run_id)` -> `RawGhaWorkflowRun`
+- `list_workflow_jobs(owner, repo, run_id, attempt=None)` -> `list[RawGhaWorkflowJob]`
+- `download_workflow_logs(owner, repo, run_id, attempt=None)` -> `bytes`
+- `list_workflow_run_artifacts(owner, repo, run_id)` -> `list[RawGhaArtifact]`
+- `download_artifact(owner, repo, artifact_id)` -> `bytes`
+- `get_workflow_file(owner, repo, path, ref)` -> `bytes | None`
+
+All HTTP methods SHALL retry on 408, 429, 5xx, network errors, and GitHub
+secondary-rate-limit responses (`403` with `retry-after` or rate-limit headers)
+using exponential backoff with jitter. The client SHALL honor `Retry-After`.
+
+#### Scenario: Client lifecycle
+
+- **GIVEN** valid GitHub credentials for a repository host
+- **WHEN** the client is used as `async with GitHubActionsClient.from_vault(pipeline_url) as client:`
+- **THEN** it SHALL create an `aiohttp.ClientSession` with authorization headers on enter
+- **AND** it SHALL close the session on exit
+
+#### Scenario: Enterprise host support
+
+- **GIVEN** a run URL under a GitHub Enterprise host, such as `https://github.wdf.sap.corp/org/repo/actions/runs/123`
+- **WHEN** the client builds API URLs
+- **THEN** it SHALL use the enterprise REST base URL for that host, not hardcoded `api.github.com`
+
+#### Scenario: Rate-limit handling
+
+- **WHEN** GitHub returns `429` or a secondary-rate-limit `403`
+- **THEN** the client SHALL delay according to `Retry-After` when present
+- **AND** SHALL retry within the configured retry budget
+- **AND** SHALL surface a retryable extraction failure only after the retry budget is exhausted
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions URL validator enablement
+
+The system SHALL add a `GitHubActionsUrlValidator` only in the same change that
+implements the GHA metadata and data extractors. Until then, the existing
+ingestion-api spec remains authoritative: GHA URLs are rejected with HTTP 422.
+
+#### Scenario: Canonical run URL accepted after extractor implementation
+
+- **WHEN** GHA support is enabled and a URL matches `https://<host>/<owner>/<repo>/actions/runs/<run_id>`
+- **THEN** `matches()` SHALL return `True`
+- **AND** `normalize()` SHALL return the canonical run URL without fragment or non-essential query parameters
+- **AND** `detect_source_system()` SHALL return `github_actions`
+
+#### Scenario: Attempt query preserved when present
+
+- **WHEN** a URL contains `?attempt=<n>`
+- **THEN** normalization SHALL preserve the attempt number in the internal request metadata
+- **AND** the canonical stored `pipeline_url` MAY omit the query string while `provider_extensions.run_attempt` records the chosen attempt
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions metadata extraction
+
+The system SHALL extract pipeline metadata from the workflow run endpoint and
+write the shared `metadata.json` shape before pipeline analysis, matching the
+Jenkins and Azure DevOps metadata flow.
+
+#### Scenario: Workflow run metadata mapped
+
+- **WHEN** the metadata extractor processes a GHA run URL
+- **THEN** it SHALL write `metadata.json` with:
+  - `ci_system`: `"github_actions"`
+  - `pipeline_url`: canonical workflow run URL
+  - `run_identifier`: `"{run_number}/{run_attempt}"`
+  - `jenkins_url`: `null`
+  - `canonical_url`: workflow run `html_url`
+  - `commit_id`: `head_sha`
+  - `commit_url`: repository commit URL for `head_sha`
+  - `github_repo_url`: repository HTML URL
+  - `pr_url`, `pr_number`, `pr_title`, `pr_author`: populated from `pull_requests[0]` when present
+  - `build_number`: `null`
+  - `full_display_name`: workflow name plus run number or display title
+  - `result`: mapped from `conclusion` or `status`
+  - `state`: mapped from `status`
+  - `building`: `true` when the run is not terminal
+  - `duration_ms`: computed from `run_started_at` and `updated_at` when terminal
+  - `start_time`, `end_time`: normalized ISO 8601 UTC strings
+  - `git_repos`: one entry for the repository and `head_sha`
+  - `parameters`: workflow inputs when available
+  - `provider_extensions.workflow_id`, `run_id`, `run_number`, `run_attempt`, `event`, `actor`, `triggering_actor`, `check_suite_id`
+
+#### Scenario: Pull request metadata absent
+
+- **WHEN** the workflow run has no `pull_requests` entries
+- **THEN** PR-specific fields SHALL be `null`
+- **AND** extraction SHALL still succeed for push, schedule, workflow_dispatch, and other non-PR events
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Extractor dispatch by CI system
+
+The existing Temporal activity names SHALL remain unchanged. The metadata and
+data extraction activities SHALL dispatch internally by `request.source_system`.
+
+#### Scenario: GitHub Actions pipeline detected
+
+- **GIVEN** a pipeline URL accepted by `GitHubActionsUrlValidator`
+- **WHEN** `extract_metadata_activity()` processes the request
+- **THEN** it SHALL call the GHA metadata extractor
+
+- **WHEN** `extract_data_activity()` processes the request
+- **THEN** it SHALL call the GHA data extractor
+- **AND** the downstream downloader, resume, unified validation, and manifest logic SHALL remain CI-agnostic
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Pydantic models for GitHub Actions API responses
+
+The system SHALL define Raw Pydantic models for GHA API responses following the
+project's two-layer pattern: Raw models mirror API payloads and convert to clean
+internal output models.
+
+#### Scenario: Workflow run model
+
+- **GIVEN** a workflow run API response
+- **WHEN** parsing it
+- **THEN** `RawGhaWorkflowRun` SHALL include:
+  - `id: int`
+  - `run_number: int`
+  - `run_attempt: int`
+  - `workflow_id: int`
+  - `name: str | None`
+  - `display_title: str | None`
+  - `event: str`
+  - `status: str`
+  - `conclusion: str | None`
+  - `html_url: str`
+  - `run_started_at: str | None`
+  - `created_at: str`
+  - `updated_at: str`
+  - `head_branch: str | None`
+  - `head_sha: str`
+  - `repository: RawGhaRepository`
+  - `head_repository: RawGhaRepository | None`
+  - `pull_requests: list[RawGhaPullRequest]`
+  - `actor: RawGhaUser | None`
+  - `triggering_actor: RawGhaUser | None`
+  - `check_suite_id: int | None`
+  - `path: str | None` for the workflow file path
+
+#### Scenario: Workflow job and step model
+
+- **GIVEN** a workflow jobs API response
+- **WHEN** parsing it
+- **THEN** `RawGhaWorkflowJob` SHALL include:
+  - `id: int`
+  - `run_id: int`
+  - `run_attempt: int | None`
+  - `name: str`
+  - `status: str`
+  - `conclusion: str | None`
+  - `started_at: str | None`
+  - `completed_at: str | None`
+  - `html_url: str | None`
+  - `runner_name: str | None`
+  - `runner_group_name: str | None`
+  - `labels: list[str]`
+  - `steps: list[RawGhaStep]`
+
+- **AND** `RawGhaStep` SHALL include:
+  - `name: str`
+  - `status: str`
+  - `conclusion: str | None`
+  - `number: int`
+  - `started_at: str | None`
+  - `completed_at: str | None`
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact model
+
+- **GIVEN** a workflow run artifact API response
+- **WHEN** parsing it
+- **THEN** `RawGhaArtifact` SHALL include:
+  - `id: int`
+  - `name: str`
+  - `size_in_bytes: int`
+  - `archive_download_url: str`
+  - `expired: bool`
+  - `created_at: str`
+  - `updated_at: str`
+  - `expires_at: str | None`
+  - `workflow_run: dict | None`
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions capture lifecycle
+
+The GHA extractor SHALL use the same extraction lifecycle as Jenkins and Azure
+DevOps: manifest check, metadata read, resume scan, provider capture,
+normalization, unified validation, and manifest write-last.
+
+#### Scenario: Completion and resume markers
+
+- **WHEN** `<inspection_id>/manifest.json` already exists
+- **THEN** the GHA extractor SHALL return success without re-fetching
+
+- **WHEN** the folder exists without `manifest.json`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL delete stale `.tmp` files, skip already captured files, and capture only missing items
+
+#### Scenario: Required unified outputs
+
+- **WHEN** GHA extraction completes
+- **THEN** the inspection folder SHALL contain the same required unified files as Jenkins and ADO:
+  - `execution_timeline.json`
+  - `pipeline_run.json`
+  - `stages.json`
+  - `artifacts.json`
+  - `jobs.json`
+  - `parallel_groups.json`
+  - `tests.json`
+- **AND** `manifest.json` SHALL be written last
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions capture plan
+
+The system SHALL build an explicit GHA capture plan before downloading large
+payloads. The plan SHALL separate small generated JSON items from HTTP download
+items, like the ADO extractor, while preserving the same manifest and downloader
+contract used by Jenkins and ADO.
+
+#### Scenario: Priority items captured first
+
+- **WHEN** building the GHA capture plan
+- **THEN** the following items SHALL be treated as priority:
+  1. `provider_raw/github_actions/run.json` — workflow run response
+  2. `provider_raw/github_actions/jobs/attempt_<n>.json` — current-attempt jobs and steps
+  3. `provider_raw/github_actions/logs/attempt_<n>.zip` — current-attempt log archive
+  4. Derived issue files for failed jobs or parsing failures
+  5. Test-report artifacts when their names match configured priority patterns
+
+#### Scenario: Non-priority items
+
+- **WHEN** building the GHA capture plan
+- **THEN** the following items SHALL be non-priority unless explicitly promoted by failure or test-report rules:
+  1. General workflow artifacts
+  2. Prior-attempt logs and job lists
+  3. Raw workflow YAML when it is not needed to reconstruct dependencies
+  4. Successful-job normalized logs when cap pressure requires skipping low-value data
+
+#### Scenario: Capture plan survives artifact expiry
+
+- **WHEN** artifact metadata exists but the artifact content cannot be downloaded
+- **THEN** the plan SHALL still include a manifest entry for that artifact
+- **AND** the item SHALL be recorded as `skipped_unreachable` with the provider error
+
+#### Scenario: Unified files are generated from captured provider data
+
+- **WHEN** raw run, jobs, logs, and artifact metadata have been captured or skipped
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL generate unified files from the available data
+- **AND** missing optional provider data SHALL degrade individual fields to `null` or empty lists rather than failing the full extraction
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Pagination and request-budget control
+
+The GHA client and extractor SHALL handle paginated endpoints and API request
+budgets explicitly. Large workflows can contain up to 256 matrix jobs and can
+exhaust low-scope token budgets if each job is handled naively.
+
+#### Scenario: Workflow jobs pagination
+
+- **WHEN** listing workflow jobs for a run or attempt
+- **THEN** the client SHALL follow GitHub pagination until all pages are read
+- **AND** SHALL preserve page-level failures as retryable extraction failures
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact pagination
+
+- **WHEN** listing workflow run artifacts
+- **THEN** the client SHALL follow pagination until all artifact metadata is collected
+- **AND** extraction SHALL continue with an empty artifact list only when GitHub authoritatively reports no artifacts
+
+#### Scenario: Request budget avoids secondary limits
+
+- **WHEN** extracting a run with many jobs or artifacts
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL bound concurrent GitHub API requests below GitHub's secondary-rate-limit threshold
+- **AND** SHALL prefer bulk endpoints (`list_workflow_jobs`, log ZIP download) over one-request-per-step designs
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub credential and scope validation
+
+The system SHALL validate GitHub credential usability before starting expensive
+capture. A wrong token often returns `403`, and GitHub Enterprise hosts may use
+different credential sources.
+
+#### Scenario: Required read permissions validated
+
+- **WHEN** the GHA extractor starts
+- **THEN** it SHALL verify that the token can read the workflow run and workflow jobs endpoints
+- **AND** SHALL fail with non-retryable `InvalidInput` when credentials are valid but insufficiently scoped
+
+#### Scenario: Enterprise credential lookup
+
+- **WHEN** the pipeline URL uses a GitHub Enterprise host
+- **THEN** credential lookup SHALL be keyed by host and repository owner
+- **AND** SHALL NOT reuse github.com credentials for enterprise hosts
+
+#### Scenario: Token type captured for diagnostics
+
+- **WHEN** metadata extraction succeeds
+- **THEN** provider extensions SHOULD record a non-secret credential type such as `github_app`, `pat`, or `enterprise_app`
+- **AND** SHALL NOT store token values or authorization headers in HDLF
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Workflow jobs mapped to unified stages and jobs
+
+The extractor SHALL use workflow jobs as the primary execution units. Each GHA
+workflow job SHALL be represented as a unified Stage with `type="job"` and as a
+`JobDetail` entry.
+
+#### Scenario: Job mapped to stage and job detail
+
+- **WHEN** a workflow run contains a job named `build (ubuntu-latest, py3.12)`
+- **THEN** `stages.json` SHALL contain a Stage with `type="job"`, ID equal to the job ID string, and status mapped from job conclusion
+- **AND** `jobs.json` SHALL contain a `JobDetail` with runner metadata, labels, and matrix values when detectable
+
+#### Scenario: Step files produced per job
+
+- **WHEN** a job contains steps from the workflow jobs API
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write `steps/<job_id>.json`
+- **AND** every step SHALL include a stable string ID, name, status, start/end timestamps when present, and `log_ref` when a normalized step log was found
+
+#### Scenario: DAG dependencies from workflow YAML
+
+- **WHEN** the workflow YAML can be fetched at the run's commit SHA
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL parse `jobs.<job_id>.needs`
+- **AND** map those dependencies into Stage.`depends_on`
+- **AND** preserve the raw workflow YAML under `provider_raw/github_actions/workflow.yml`
+
+#### Scenario: Dependencies unavailable
+
+- **WHEN** the workflow YAML cannot be fetched or parsed
+- **THEN** Stage.`depends_on` SHALL be an empty list
+- **AND** the extractor SHALL write a provider issue noting that dependency reconstruction is incomplete
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Attempt and rerun handling
+
+The extractor SHALL preserve GHA rerun context. GHA retries create new
+`run_attempt` values under the same `run_id`, and partially rerun log archives
+may contain only rerun jobs.
+
+#### Scenario: Current attempt captured
+
+- **WHEN** extracting a run with `run_attempt=3`
+- **THEN** `pipeline_run.json.run_identifier` SHALL be `"{run_number}/3"`
+- **AND** every Stage and JobDetail produced from that attempt SHALL have `attempt=3`, `total_attempts=3`, and `has_prior_failure=true`
+
+#### Scenario: Prior attempts preserved as provider raw
+
+- **WHEN** `run_attempt > 1`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHOULD fetch prior attempts when credentials and rate budget allow
+- **AND** write attempt job lists and log archives under `provider_raw/github_actions/attempts/<attempt_number>/`
+- **AND** record skipped prior attempts in the manifest as `skipped_unreachable` when rate limits, permissions, or retention prevent capture
+
+#### Scenario: Partially rerun logs do not replace previous logs
+
+- **WHEN** the current attempt log archive contains only rerun jobs
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL NOT infer that missing jobs had no logs
+- **AND** SHALL preserve current-attempt logs separately from prior-attempt logs
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: GitHub Actions log extraction and normalization
+
+The extractor SHALL download the workflow run log ZIP, store it as provider raw,
+and derive normalized job and step logs from it when possible.
+
+#### Scenario: Raw log archive stored
+
+- **WHEN** downloading logs for a workflow run attempt
+- **THEN** the ZIP payload SHALL be written under `provider_raw/github_actions/logs/attempt_<n>.zip`
+- **AND** the manifest entry SHALL record the captured or truncated size
+
+#### Scenario: Timestamp prefixes stripped
+
+- **WHEN** writing normalized log files from GHA log ZIP entries
+- **THEN** timestamp prefixes such as `2026-07-06T14:30:00.000Z ` SHALL be stripped from each line
+- **AND** normalized line numbering SHALL be 1-indexed
+
+#### Scenario: Step log mapping best effort
+
+- **WHEN** ZIP entries can be matched to a workflow job and step number/name
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write `logs/<job_id>/<step_id>.log` and set the Step.`log_ref`
+- **WHEN** a ZIP entry can only be matched to the job
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write or append to `logs/<job_id>.log`
+- **AND** leave Step.`log_ref` as `null` for unmatched steps
+
+#### Scenario: Undocumented ZIP structure changes
+
+- **WHEN** the log ZIP structure cannot be parsed
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL keep the raw ZIP under `provider_raw/github_actions/logs/`
+- **AND** write a provider issue describing the parse failure
+- **AND** still produce valid unified files with missing `log_ref` values
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Failure detection and issue extraction
+
+The GHA extractor SHALL use multi-signal failure detection. It SHALL NOT rely
+only on workflow-run conclusion, because individual jobs and steps may expose
+more useful failure context.
+
+#### Scenario: Failed jobs persisted as failed stages
+
+- **WHEN** a workflow job has `conclusion` in `failure`, `cancelled`, `timed_out`, or `action_required`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL persist a `FailedStage` row for the job
+- **AND** the error message SHOULD come from parsed log command markers or check annotations when available
+
+#### Scenario: Step-level failures drive priority
+
+- **WHEN** any step in a job has `conclusion` in `failure`, `cancelled`, or `timed_out`
+- **THEN** that job's logs SHALL be priority capture items
+- **AND** the corresponding Stage.`failure_messages` SHALL include extracted messages when available
+
+#### Scenario: Log command markers become provider issues
+
+- **WHEN** normalized logs contain command markers such as `##[error]message` or `::error file=...,line=...::message`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write `provider_raw/github_actions/issues/<job_id>.json`
+- **AND** each issue SHALL include `type`, `message`, `category`, `source_record`, and `source_line` when parseable
+
+#### Scenario: UNSTABLE inferred from tests
+
+- **WHEN** the workflow conclusion is `failure` but normalized test results show mixed passing and failing tests
+- **THEN** `pipeline_run.json.result` MAY be `UNSTABLE`
+- **AND** `result_confidence` SHALL be `inferred`
+- **AND** provider extensions SHALL preserve the raw GitHub conclusion
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Test result extraction from artifacts
+
+GitHub Actions has no native test results API. The extractor SHALL derive test
+reports only from captured artifacts and recognized report files.
+
+#### Scenario: JUnit artifacts parsed
+
+- **WHEN** an artifact contains JUnit XML files matching configured patterns
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL parse them into `tests.json`
+- **AND** preserve the original artifact under `artifacts/`
+- **AND** record the artifact path in test report provenance when the model supports it
+
+#### Scenario: No test artifacts
+
+- **WHEN** no recognized test result files are found
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write an empty `tests.json`
+- **AND** SHALL NOT fail extraction
+
+#### Scenario: Malformed test report
+
+- **WHEN** a recognized test report file is malformed
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write a provider issue
+- **AND** continue processing other reports
+- **AND** only fail extraction if all unified output validation becomes impossible
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Artifact capture and attribution
+
+The extractor SHALL capture GHA artifacts during the extraction window and SHALL
+not persist GitHub's short-lived redirect URLs as durable download sources.
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact download redirects are handled immediately
+
+- **WHEN** downloading an artifact through `archive_download_url`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL follow the 302 redirect and download immediately
+- **AND** retries SHALL re-request `archive_download_url` rather than retrying an expired redirected URL
+
+#### Scenario: Expired artifact recorded
+
+- **WHEN** an artifact has `expired=true` or download returns 404 due to retention expiry
+- **THEN** the manifest SHALL include the artifact as `skipped_unreachable`
+- **AND** `artifacts.json` SHALL include metadata with unavailable content when enough metadata exists
+
+#### Scenario: Job-level artifact attribution
+
+- **WHEN** GitHub provides workflow/job association for an artifact
+- **THEN** `artifacts.json.producing_stage_id` SHALL be set to the matching job stage ID
+- **AND** `attribution_confidence` SHALL be `medium` unless a step-level source is inferred from upload-artifact outputs or logs
+
+#### Scenario: No step-level artifact association
+
+- **WHEN** no step-level association is available
+- **THEN** `producing_step_id` SHALL be `null`
+- **AND** `candidate_stage_ids` MAY include likely jobs inferred from artifact name or log markers
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Provider raw storage layout
+
+The extractor SHALL use a provider-specific raw layout under
+`provider_raw/github_actions/` while writing normalized files at the inspection
+root like Jenkins and ADO.
+
+#### Scenario: Required provider raw files
+
+- **WHEN** GHA extraction runs
+- **THEN** it SHALL write available raw data under:
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/run.json`
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/jobs/attempt_<n>.json`
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/logs/attempt_<n>.zip`
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/artifacts/list.json`
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/artifacts/<artifact_id>.zip` when captured
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/workflow.yml` when available
+  - `provider_raw/github_actions/issues/<job_id>.json` when issues are derived
+
+---
+
+### Requirement: Unified retrieval compatibility
+
+GHA inspections SHALL be consumed through the same `UnifiedInspectionReader` and
+HDLF server routes as Jenkins and ADO inspections.
+
+#### Scenario: Reader factory selects unified reader
+
+- **WHEN** a GHA inspection has `pipeline_run.json`
+- **THEN** `create_reader()` SHALL return `UnifiedInspectionReader`
+- **AND** no GHA-specific reader branch SHALL be required for standard pipeline data
+
+#### Scenario: Handler orchestration context available
+
+- **WHEN** GHA data extraction discovers failed jobs
+- **THEN** it SHALL persist `FailedStage` rows before handler orchestration
+- **AND** handler orchestration SHALL use the same DB-driven path as Jenkins and ADO
+
+---
+
+## Design Notes
+
+### Why this mirrors Jenkins and Azure DevOps
+
+The Temporal workflow does not need new activity names for GHA. GHA should fit
+behind the existing `extract_metadata_activity` and `extract_data_activity`
+dispatch branches, then produce the same normalized files and DB side effects:
+`metadata.json`, `manifest.json`, unified root files, provider raw files,
+`FailedStage` rows, and `Inspection` repo/commit fields.
+
+### Why GHA differs from ADO and Jenkins
+
+GHA has no native test results API, exposes logs as an undocumented ZIP archive,
+uses rerun attempts under one `run_id`, and uses short-lived artifact redirect
+URLs. Those differences are provider-specific capture concerns. They SHALL NOT
+leak into downstream retrieval APIs.
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..34c76f90
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/ingestion-api/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@
+## MODIFIED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Unsupported CI system rejected
+The Ingestion API SHALL validate `pipeline_url` against URL patterns for supported CI systems: Jenkins job-run URLs (`https://<host>/job/<project>/job/<branch>/<build_number>/`) and Azure DevOps pipeline run URLs (`https://dev.azure.com/<org>/<project>/_build/results?buildId=<id>` and legacy `https://<org>.visualstudio.com/<project>/_build/results?buildId=<id>`). URLs that do not match any supported pattern SHALL be rejected with HTTP 422 naming all supported systems.
+
+GitHub Actions URL validation (`https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/actions/runs/<run_id>`) is deferred until the GHA extractor is implemented (separate change). GHA URLs SHALL receive HTTP 422 until then.
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins URL accepted
+- **WHEN** a POST request contains a `pipeline_url` matching the Jenkins pattern `https://<host>/job/<project>/job/<branch>/<build_number>/`
+- **THEN** the API responds with HTTP 202 and processes the inspection with `source_system="jenkins"`
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps URL accepted (modern format)
+- **WHEN** a POST request contains a `pipeline_url` matching `https://dev.azure.com/<org>/<project>/_build/results?buildId=<id>`
+- **THEN** the API responds with HTTP 202 and processes the inspection with `source_system="azure_devops"`
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps URL accepted (legacy format)
+- **WHEN** a POST request contains a `pipeline_url` matching `https://<org>.visualstudio.com/<project>/_build/results?buildId=<id>`
+- **THEN** the API responds with HTTP 202 and processes the inspection with `source_system="azure_devops"`
+- **AND** the URL SHALL be normalized to the modern `dev.azure.com` format for internal storage
+
+#### Scenario: GitHub Actions URL rejected (extractor not yet implemented)
+- **WHEN** a POST request contains a `pipeline_url` matching `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/actions/runs/<run_id>`
+- **THEN** the API responds with HTTP 422 with an error message: "GitHub Actions is not yet supported. Supported CI systems: Jenkins, Azure DevOps."
+
+#### Scenario: Unsupported URL rejected with descriptive error
+- **WHEN** a POST request contains a syntactically valid URL that does not match Jenkins or Azure DevOps patterns
+- **THEN** the API responds with HTTP 422 with an error message listing supported CI systems
+
+### Requirement: source_system derived from URL validator result
+
+The `source_system` field on the `IngestionRequest` (passed to Temporal workflow) SHALL be derived from the URL validator that matched the pipeline URL — NOT from naive string matching on the URL.
+
+#### Scenario: Validator result propagated to workflow
+- **WHEN** a pipeline URL passes validation via `validate_pipeline_url()`
+- **THEN** the matching validator's `.name` property SHALL be used as `source_system`
+- **AND** the ingestion router SHALL NOT use independent string matching (e.g., checking for `/job/` substring)
+
+#### Scenario: source_system stored in Inspection DB row
+- **WHEN** the ingestion API creates an Inspection record
+- **THEN** the `ci_system` column SHALL be populated with the validator's `.name` (same value as `source_system`)
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..f1703b07
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/provider-raw-storage/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,162 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Provider-specific raw data stored in dedicated subdirectory
+The Data Extractor SHALL write provider-native API responses into `provider_raw/<ci_system>/` within the inspection folder. The canonical provider-raw roots for this change are `provider_raw/jenkins/` and `provider_raw/azure_devops/`. Consumer-facing normalized files SHALL be written outside `provider_raw/` at the top level (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, etc.) or under normalized `logs/...`.
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins raw data stored in provider_raw/jenkins/
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins Data Extractor captures a build
+- **THEN** raw Jenkins API response files SHALL be written under `<uuid>/provider_raw/jenkins/`
+- **AND** `artifacts_list.json` SHALL contain the `artifacts[]` array from the classic `/api/json` response, persisted separately for artifact attribution tracing
+- **AND** the unified schema files (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, etc.) SHALL be written at the top level
+- **AND** normalized stage and step logs SHALL be written under `<uuid>/logs/...`, not consumed from provider-raw paths
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps raw data stored in provider_raw/azure_devops/
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps Data Extractor captures a pipeline run
+- **THEN** the following raw API responses SHALL be stored in `<uuid>/provider_raw/azure_devops/`:
+
+| File | API source | Description |
+|------|-----------|-------------|
+| `build.json` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}` | Full build details: status, result, timestamps, branch, commit, trigger reason, parameters, definition, project |
+| `timeline.json` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/timeline` | Complete stage→job→task hierarchy with state, result, timing, issues, errorCount, and log references |
+| `issues/<stage_id>.json` | Extracted from timeline records | Plain JSON array of provider-neutral issue items for all tasks within each stage that has errors or warnings |
+| `attempts/<attempt>/timeline.json` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/timeline/{timelineId}` | Per-attempt timelines for retried stages (referenced via `previousAttempts[].timelineId` on timeline records) |
+| `sub_timelines/<record_id>.json` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/timeline/{details.id}` | Sub-timelines for deployment jobs and matrix expansions (referenced via `details.id` on Job-type records) |
+| `tests/run_<id>_results.json` | `GET /_apis/test/runs/{runId}/results` (paginated) | Test results per test run, discovered via `GET /_apis/test/runs?buildId={buildId}` |
+| `tests/all_failures.json` | Derived from test result files | Combined failed test results across all runs for quick access |
+| `logs/<log_id>.txt` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/logs/{logId}` | Raw task log content, fetched via `log.id` from timeline records (priority logs from failed stages captured first) |
+| `artifacts_list.json` | `GET /_apis/build/builds/{buildId}/artifacts` | Full artifact metadata array including `source` field (producing job record ID) for each artifact — the primary input for artifact-to-stage attribution |
+
+- **AND** the unified schema files (`pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, etc.) SHALL be written at the top level
+- **AND** normalized job and task logs SHALL be written under `<uuid>/logs/...`, using raw `provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/<log_id>.txt` only as normalization input
+
+### Requirement: Provider issue files use one item schema
+
+The Data Extractor SHALL write provider issue files as plain JSON arrays under
+`provider_raw/<ci_system>/issues/<stage_or_record_id>.json`. Each array item
+SHALL use the same provider-neutral issue item schema for Azure DevOps and
+Jenkins.
+
+#### Scenario: Issues schema is CI-agnostic
+
+- **GIVEN** `issues/<stage_or_record_id>.json` files in either `provider_raw/jenkins/` or `provider_raw/azure_devops/`
+- **THEN** both SHALL contain a plain JSON array
+- **AND** each array item SHALL include:
+  - `type: Literal["error", "warning"]`
+  - `message: str`
+  - `category: str | None`
+  - `source_record: str | None`
+  - `source_line: int | None`
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps issue file shape
+
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes `provider_raw/azure_devops/issues/<record_id>.json`
+- **THEN** each issue item SHALL preserve `type`, `message`, and `category` from the native timeline issue
+- **AND** when the native timeline issue has no category, `category` SHALL be `null`
+- **AND** `source_record` SHALL identify the timeline record that carried the issue
+- **AND** `source_line` SHALL be `null` because Azure DevOps timeline issues do not carry log line numbers
+
+```json
+[
+  {
+    "type": "error",
+    "message": "Build failed",
+    "category": "General",
+    "source_record": "Compile",
+    "source_line": null
+  }
+]
+```
+
+### Requirement: Derived issues files for Jenkins (log-parsed equivalent of ADO structured issues)
+
+Azure DevOps provides structured `issues[]` arrays natively on timeline records, giving pre-parsed error/warning objects without log download. Jenkins has no equivalent — errors are embedded in console log text. To provide a uniform interface for downstream consumers, the Jenkins extractor SHALL derive equivalent issues files from log content after download.
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins issues files written after log capture
+
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins Data Extractor has captured stage console logs
+- **THEN** it SHALL parse each failed stage's log for error patterns and write `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/<stage_id>.json`
+- **AND** the file SHALL contain a plain JSON array using the same issue item schema as the Azure DevOps issues files
+- **AND** `source_record` SHALL identify the Jenkins stage or step segment where the issue was found when available
+- **AND** `source_line` SHALL contain the 1-based line number in the parsed stage log when available
+
+```json
+[
+  {
+    "type": "error",
+    "message": "FATAL: remote file operation failed: /path/to/file",
+    "category": null,
+    "source_record": "Build",
+    "source_line": 17
+  },
+  {
+    "type": "error",
+    "message": "hudson.AbortException: script returned exit code 1",
+    "category": null,
+    "source_record": "Build",
+    "source_line": 42
+  }
+]
+```
+
+#### Scenario: Derivation differences documented
+
+- **GIVEN** the Jenkins issues are derived (heuristic) rather than native (authoritative)
