+- **THEN** the following differences SHALL be understood by consumers:
+
+| Aspect | ADO `issues/` | Jenkins `issues/` |
+|--------|---------------|-------------------|
+| Source | Native `issues[]` on timeline records | Extracted from console log text post-download |
+| Timing | Available immediately with timeline fetch | Available only after log download + parsing |
+| `category` | Populated by ADO agent (General, Code, Test) | `null` (no native categorization in Jenkins) |
+| `source_record` | Timeline record name | Stage or step segment name when available |
+| `source_line` | `null` | 1-based parsed stage-log line when available |
+| Completeness | Authoritative — agent reports all issues | Best-effort — heuristic extraction from log patterns |
+
+#### Scenario: Issues files only written for stages with detected errors
+
+- **WHEN** a stage log contains no recognized error patterns
+- **THEN** no `issues/<stage_id>.json` file SHALL be written for that stage (absence means no errors detected)
+
+---
+
+#### Scenario: Raw files excluded from main reader path
+- **WHEN** the `UnifiedInspectionReader` reads inspection data
+- **THEN** it SHALL NOT access any files under `provider_raw/` — those are only available through the dedicated provider-raw endpoint
+
+### Requirement: Dedicated endpoint for provider-specific data
+The HDLF Server SHALL expose a `GET /api/v1/inspection/{inspection_id}/provider-raw/{path:path}` endpoint that streams raw provider files from the `provider_raw/` subdirectory. This endpoint SHALL require the same authentication as other inspection endpoints. The `{path}` parameter SHALL be validated against path traversal attacks using the same `_validate_subpath` logic as existing raw file access.
+
+#### Scenario: Access Jenkins raw data via endpoint
+- **WHEN** a client GETs `/api/v1/inspection/{id}/provider-raw/jenkins/blue_ocean_run.json`
+- **THEN** the server SHALL stream the raw Blue Ocean JSON response as stored
+
+#### Scenario: Path traversal rejected
+- **WHEN** a client GETs `/api/v1/inspection/{id}/provider-raw/../../metadata.json`
+- **THEN** the server SHALL respond with HTTP 400 and not serve the file
+
+#### Scenario: Non-existent raw file returns 404
+- **WHEN** a client requests a path under `provider-raw/` that does not exist
+- **THEN** the server SHALL respond with HTTP 404
+
+### Requirement: Provider-raw directory listing
+The HDLF Server SHALL expose a `GET /api/v1/inspection/{inspection_id}/provider-raw/` endpoint (no path suffix) that returns a listing of available raw files for the inspection, grouped by CI system. The response SHALL include file paths, sizes, and modification times.
+
+#### Scenario: List all raw files for a Jenkins inspection
+- **WHEN** a client GETs `/api/v1/inspection/{id}/provider-raw/`
+- **THEN** the response SHALL include a JSON array of file entries under `jenkins/` with path, size_bytes, and modification_time fields
+
+#### Scenario: Empty listing for inspection without raw data
+- **WHEN** a client GETs `/api/v1/inspection/{id}/provider-raw/` for an old inspection captured before provider_raw/ existed
+- **THEN** the response SHALL be an empty array (not an error)
+
+### Requirement: CI-agnostic consumers SHALL NOT depend on provider_raw
+All CI-agnostic consumers (Handler Orchestrator, Execution Engine, MCP handler tools) SHALL operate exclusively on the unified schema files and SHALL NOT import or read from `provider_raw/`. The `provider_raw/` data is reserved for: (a) debugging/forensics tools, (b) CI-system-specific enrichment pipelines, and (c) future backfill operations.
+
+#### Scenario: Handler executes without provider-raw access
+- **WHEN** a handler in the Handler Orchestrator processes a pipeline failure
+- **THEN** it SHALL use only `PipelineRun`, `Stage`, `Step`, and `Artifact` models from the unified reader
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT read from `provider_raw/` or branch on `ci_system` for its core logic
+
+#### Scenario: Provider-raw unavailable does not break consumers
+- **WHEN** the `provider_raw/` directory is missing from an inspection folder (e.g., due to storage cleanup)
+- **THEN** all unified reader methods SHALL continue to function normally — `provider_raw/` absence SHALL NOT be an error condition for the main read path
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/shared-inspection-reader/spec.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/shared-inspection-reader/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..8742c1af
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/shared-inspection-reader/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,162 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Shared InspectionReader protocol
+The system SHALL define an `InspectionReaderProtocol` (Python Protocol class) in `fl_shared/` that specifies the read interface for inspection data. The protocol SHALL declare methods: `get_execution_timeline(inspection_uuid) -> ExecutionTimeline`, `get_pipeline_run(inspection_uuid) -> PipelineRun`, `list_stages(inspection_uuid) -> list[Stage]`, `get_stage_steps(inspection_uuid, stage_id) -> list[Step]`, `get_jobs(inspection_uuid) -> list[JobDetail]`, `get_parallel_groups(inspection_uuid) -> list[ParallelGroup]`, `list_artifacts(inspection_uuid) -> list[Artifact]`, `get_artifact_provenance(inspection_uuid, artifact_path) -> ArtifactProvenance | None`, `stream_log(inspection_uuid, stage_id=None, step_id=None) -> AsyncGenerator[bytes, None]`, and `get_metadata(inspection_uuid) -> InspectionMetadata`. All consumers (HDLF Server, Handler Orchestrator, MCP tools) SHALL use this protocol — not concrete implementations.
+
+#### Scenario: Consumer uses protocol without knowing CI system
+- **WHEN** a handler in the Handler Orchestrator reads pipeline data via `InspectionReaderProtocol`
+- **THEN** the handler code SHALL contain no `if ci_system == ...` branching — it operates on the unified models exclusively
+
+#### Scenario: Protocol is satisfied by unified reader
+- **WHEN** an inspection folder contains `pipeline_run.json` (indicating unified format)
+- **THEN** the `UnifiedInspectionReader` class SHALL satisfy the `InspectionReaderProtocol` by reading from the unified schema files
+
+### Requirement: UnifiedInspectionReader implementation
+The system SHALL provide a `UnifiedInspectionReader` class that implements `InspectionReaderProtocol` by reading the unified JSON files (`execution_timeline.json`, `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `steps/<stage_id>.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, `artifacts.json`, `logs/`). This reader SHALL NOT contain any CI-system-specific parsing logic. It SHALL raise `InspectionNotFoundError` when the inspection folder does not exist, and `MalformedInspectionDataError` when a required file contains invalid JSON or fails Pydantic validation.
+
+#### Scenario: Read execution timeline from unified format
+- **WHEN** `get_execution_timeline("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/execution_timeline.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a validated `ExecutionTimeline` model parsed from that file
+
+#### Scenario: Assemble execution timeline for older unified inspections
+- **WHEN** `get_execution_timeline("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/execution_timeline.json` does not exist, but the separate unified files exist
+- **THEN** the method MAY assemble the `ExecutionTimeline` from `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `steps/<stage_id>.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, and `artifacts.json`
+- **AND** this fallback SHALL be treated as backward compatibility for inspections written before `execution_timeline.json` became required
+
+#### Scenario: Read pipeline run from unified format
+- **WHEN** `get_pipeline_run("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/pipeline_run.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a validated `PipelineRun` model parsed from that file
+
+#### Scenario: Read stages from unified format
+- **WHEN** `list_stages("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/stages.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a list of `Stage` models, one per entry in the JSON array
+
+#### Scenario: Read steps for a stage
+- **WHEN** `get_stage_steps("abc-123", "42")` is called and `abc-123/steps/42.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a list of `Step` models from that file
+- **AND** if the file does not exist, return an empty list (not an error)
+
+#### Scenario: Read job details
+- **WHEN** `get_jobs("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/jobs.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a list of `JobDetail` models from that file
+- **AND** if the file does not exist (e.g., Jenkins inspection), return an empty list (not an error)
+
+#### Scenario: Read parallel groups
+- **WHEN** `get_parallel_groups("abc-123")` is called and `abc-123/parallel_groups.json` exists
+- **THEN** the method SHALL return a list of `ParallelGroup` models from that file
+- **AND** if the file does not exist (pipeline has no parallel execution), return an empty list (not an error)
+
+#### Scenario: Get artifact provenance
+- **WHEN** `get_artifact_provenance("abc-123", "checkmarxOne/result.sarif")` is called
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL look up the artifact in `abc-123/artifacts.json` by `relative_path`
+- **AND** return an `ArtifactProvenance` model with fields:
+  - `artifact_path: str` — the requested artifact path
+  - `producing_stage_id: str | None` — stage/job that produced it
+  - `producing_step_id: str | None` — specific publish/archive step
+  - `candidate_stage_ids: list[str] | None` — ambiguous candidates when confident match impossible
+  - `attribution_method: Literal["api_source", "archive_step_node", "naming_heuristic", "mtime_correlation"] | None`
+  - `attribution_confidence: Literal["high", "medium", "low"] | None`
+- **AND** if the artifact is not found in `artifacts.json`, return `None`
+
+#### Scenario: Artifact provenance provides full trace chain
+- **GIVEN** an `ArtifactProvenance` with `producing_stage_id` and `producing_step_id` set
+- **WHEN** a consumer wants the full provenance chain
+- **THEN** it can resolve: artifact → step (via `producing_step_id`) → job/stage (via `producing_stage_id`) → parent stage (via `parent_stage_id` in `stages.json`)
+- **AND** this chain works identically for both Jenkins and Azure DevOps inspections
+
+#### Scenario: Stream log content
+- **WHEN** `stream_log("abc-123", stage_id="42")` is called
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL stream bytes from `abc-123/logs/42.log`
+- **AND** if `stage_id` and `step_id` are both None, stream from `abc-123/logs/console.log`
+
+### Requirement: Execution timeline exposed to consumers
+
+The system SHALL expose the required `execution_timeline.json` aggregate through the same harmonized reader path used by other unified inspection data. Consumers SHALL NOT read provider-specific raw timeline files to obtain the full-run view.
+
+#### Scenario: HDLF Server exposes execution timeline
+- **WHEN** a client requests `GET /api/v1/inspection/{inspection_id}/unified/execution-timeline`
+- **THEN** the HDLF Server SHALL create an `InspectionReaderProtocol` via `create_reader()`
+- **AND** return `reader.get_execution_timeline(inspection_id)` as an `ExecutionTimeline` response
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT read from `provider_raw/`
+
+#### Scenario: MCP exposes execution timeline
+- **WHEN** an MCP consumer calls `get_execution_timeline`
+- **THEN** the MCP server SHALL return the `ExecutionTimeline` for the current inspection by using the harmonized reader
+- **AND** the tool SHALL be additive; existing stage, step, log, and artifact tools remain available during transition
+
+### Requirement: Legacy Jenkins reader fallback
+The system SHALL provide a `LegacyJenkinsReader` class that implements `InspectionReaderProtocol` for inspection folders that were captured before the unified format was introduced (i.e., folders lacking `pipeline_run.json`). This reader SHALL parse `pipeline_tree.json`, `wfapi_describe.json`, and per-stage `steps.json` files, converting them into the unified models at read-time.
+
+#### Scenario: Automatic reader selection
+- **WHEN** a consumer requests an `InspectionReaderProtocol` instance for a given inspection UUID
+- **THEN** the factory SHALL check for `pipeline_run.json` existence: if present, return `UnifiedInspectionReader`; if absent, return `LegacyJenkinsReader`
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy reader produces same models as unified
+- **WHEN** `LegacyJenkinsReader.list_stages("old-uuid")` is called on a pre-migration inspection
+- **THEN** it SHALL return `Stage` models by parsing `pipeline_tree.json` (Blue Ocean nodes) or `wfapi_describe.json` (WFAPI stages), mapping Jenkins-native statuses to the unified enum
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy reader assembles execution timeline
+- **WHEN** `LegacyJenkinsReader.get_execution_timeline("old-uuid")` is called on a pre-migration inspection
+- **THEN** it SHALL return an `ExecutionTimeline` assembled from the same legacy-to-unified conversions used by `get_pipeline_run()`, `list_stages()`, `get_stage_steps()`, `get_jobs()`, `get_parallel_groups()`, and `list_artifacts()`
+- **AND** `jobs` SHALL be an empty list for legacy Jenkins inspections
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy reader gracefully handles missing files
+- **WHEN** both `pipeline_tree.json` and `wfapi_describe.json` are absent in a legacy inspection
+- **THEN** `list_stages()` SHALL raise `InspectionFileNotFoundError` (not crash on NoneType)
+
+### Requirement: Reader factory function
+The system SHALL provide a factory function `create_reader(hdlf_client, inspection_uuid) -> InspectionReaderProtocol` that probes the inspection folder and returns the appropriate reader implementation. This factory SHALL be the single entry point for obtaining a reader — consumers SHALL NOT instantiate reader classes directly.
+
+#### Scenario: Factory returns unified reader for new inspections
+- **WHEN** `create_reader(client, "new-uuid")` is called and `new-uuid/pipeline_run.json` exists in HDLF
+- **THEN** it SHALL return a `UnifiedInspectionReader` instance
+
+#### Scenario: Factory returns legacy reader for old inspections
+- **WHEN** `create_reader(client, "old-uuid")` is called and `old-uuid/pipeline_run.json` does NOT exist but `old-uuid/metadata.json` does
+- **THEN** it SHALL return a `LegacyJenkinsReader` instance
+
+#### Scenario: Factory raises for non-existent inspection
+- **WHEN** `create_reader(client, "missing-uuid")` is called and neither `metadata.json` nor `pipeline_run.json` exist
+- **THEN** it SHALL raise `InspectionNotFoundError`
+
+### Requirement: Dependency-aware stage traversal
+
+The `depends_on` field on Stage enables consumers to reconstruct the execution dependency graph without CI-specific branching. The reader SHALL provide helper methods for common dependency queries.
+
+#### Scenario: Get blocking dependencies for a stage
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `reader.get_stage_dependencies(inspection_uuid, stage_id)`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL return `list[Stage]` — all stages referenced in the target stage's `depends_on` list
+- **AND** if `depends_on` is empty, return an empty list
+
+#### Scenario: Get downstream dependents of a stage
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `reader.get_stage_dependents(inspection_uuid, stage_id)`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL return `list[Stage]` — all stages whose `depends_on` includes the given `stage_id`
+- **AND** this answers "what would break if this stage failed?"
+
+#### Scenario: Topological ordering available via list_stages
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `reader.list_stages(inspection_uuid)`
+- **THEN** the returned list SHALL be ordered by `order` field (execution order within parent)
+- **AND** the `depends_on` field on each Stage provides the full dependency graph for consumers that need topological sorting beyond simple ordering
+
+### Requirement: Retry-aware stage reading
+
+The reader contract SHALL expose the `attempt`, `total_attempts`, and `has_prior_failure` fields on Stage so consumers can identify and handle retried stages without CI-specific branching.
+
+#### Scenario: Filter for retried stages
+- **WHEN** a consumer wants to find stages that were retried
+- **THEN** it SHALL filter `list_stages()` for `total_attempts > 1`
+- **AND** `has_prior_failure=true` confirms the retry was due to failure (not manual re-run)
+
+#### Scenario: Prior attempt data accessible via provider_raw
+- **WHEN** a consumer needs logs or details from a prior attempt (not the current one)
+- **THEN** it SHALL access `provider_raw/azure_devops/attempts/<attempt>/timeline.json` via the provider-raw endpoint
+- **AND** the unified reader methods SHALL only return data from the LATEST attempt (the one that determined the final result)
+
+### Requirement: Conditional failure filtering
+
+The reader contract SHALL expose the `is_conditional_failure` field on Step so handlers can separate noise (optional notifications that failed) from real blocking failures without CI-specific branching.
+
+#### Scenario: Get blocking failures for a stage
+- **WHEN** a consumer wants the "real" failures within a stage's steps
+- **THEN** it SHALL filter `get_stage_steps(uuid, stage_id)` for steps where `result="FAILURE" AND is_conditional_failure=false`
+- **AND** steps with `is_conditional_failure=true` represent optional work whose failure the pipeline author intentionally tolerates
diff --git a/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..3375125e
--- /dev/null
+++ b/openspec/changes/unified-ci-agnostic-schema/specs/unified-pipeline-schema/spec.md
@@ -0,0 +1,582 @@
+## ADDED Requirements
+
+### Requirement: Unified PipelineRun model
+The system SHALL define a `PipelineRun` Pydantic model that represents a single CI pipeline execution in a CI-agnostic format. The model SHALL include: `id` (string, provider-assigned), `ci_system` (Literal["jenkins", "azure_devops", "github_actions"]), `url` (canonical web URL of the run), `result` (unified Status enum), `state` (unified State enum), `result_confidence` (Literal["authoritative", "inferred"] | None — whether the result came directly from the CI API or was inferred from heuristics), `duration_ms` (int | None), `start_time` (ISO 8601 UTC string | None), `end_time` (ISO 8601 UTC string | None), `commit_id` (str | None), `branch` (str | None), `pr_number` (int | None), `pr_title` (str | None), `pr_author` (str | None), `pr_url` (str | None), `build_number` (int | None — Jenkins sequential build number; kept for backward compatibility), `run_identifier` (str | None — the CI system's native human-readable run identifier; works across all CI systems without type conversion), `display_name` (str | None), `git_repos` (list of GitRepo), `parameters` (dict[str, Any]), and `provider_extensions` (dict[str, Any] for CI-specific fields).
+
+#### Scenario: PipelineRun produced from Jenkins extraction
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor captures a Jenkins build
+- **THEN** a `pipeline_run.json` file SHALL be written to the inspection folder containing all fields populated from the merged Blue Ocean + /api/json data, with timestamps converted to UTC and statuses mapped to the unified vocabulary
+
+#### Scenario: PipelineRun produced from Azure DevOps extraction
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor captures an Azure DevOps pipeline run
+- **THEN** a `pipeline_run.json` file SHALL be written with `ci_system="azure_devops"`, statuses mapped (e.g., "succeeded" → "SUCCESS", "failed" → "FAILURE"), and `provider_extensions` containing Azure-specific fields (e.g., `pool_name`, `definition_id`)
+
+#### Scenario: PipelineRun produced from GitHub Actions extraction
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor captures a GitHub Actions workflow run
+- **THEN** a `pipeline_run.json` file SHALL be written with `ci_system="github_actions"`, statuses mapped (e.g., "success" → "SUCCESS", "failure" → "FAILURE"), and `provider_extensions` containing GitHub-specific fields (e.g., `workflow_id`, `triggering_actor`)
+
+#### Scenario: run_identifier populated for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor writes `pipeline_run.json`
+- **THEN** `run_identifier` SHALL be the string representation of the build number (e.g., `"5548"`)
+- **AND** `build_number` SHALL also be set to the integer value (e.g., `5548`) for backward compatibility
+
+#### Scenario: run_identifier populated for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes `pipeline_run.json`
+- **THEN** `run_identifier` SHALL be the native `buildNumber` string (e.g., `"20260706.1"`, `"1.0.5548"`)
+- **AND** `build_number` SHALL be `null` (ADO build numbers are not integers)
+
+#### Scenario: run_identifier populated for GitHub Actions
+- **WHEN** the GitHub Actions extractor writes `pipeline_run.json`
+- **THEN** `run_identifier` SHALL be `"{run_number}/{run_attempt}"` (e.g., `"142/3"`)
+- **AND** `build_number` SHALL be `null` (GHA run_number alone is ambiguous without attempt)
+
+#### Scenario: Consumers use run_identifier for display
+- **WHEN** a consumer needs to display a human-readable build identifier
+- **THEN** it SHALL use `run_identifier` (works for all CI systems)
+- **AND** SHALL NOT use `build_number` for new code (it exists only for backward compatibility with Jenkins-only consumers)
+
+### Requirement: Unified Stage model
+The system SHALL define a `Stage` Pydantic model representing one stage/job in a pipeline. Fields SHALL include: `id` (string), `display_name` (string), `type` (Literal["stage", "parallel", "job", "phase"]), `result` (unified Status), `state` (unified State), `result_confidence` (Literal["authoritative", "inferred"] | None — whether the result came directly from the CI API or was inferred from heuristics; see Critical Gap #4 note below), `duration_ms` (int | None), `start_time` (ISO 8601 UTC | None), `end_time` (ISO 8601 UTC | None), `parent_stage_id` (str | None for hierarchy), `depends_on` (list[str] — execution dependency graph; stage IDs that must complete before this stage runs; empty list when no explicit dependencies), `order` (int — execution order within parent), `ran` (bool | None — whether the stage actually executed), `attempt` (int — which attempt this represents, default 1), `total_attempts` (int — how many times this stage was executed including retries, default 1), `has_prior_failure` (bool — whether earlier attempts of this stage failed, default False), `failure_category` (Literal["code", "infrastructure", "configuration", "unknown"] | None — classification of WHY the stage failed; None when not classified or stage succeeded), `failure_messages` (list[str] | None — human-readable error messages from the stage's failure; `null` or an empty array when no messages are available), `has_step_logs` (bool — whether step-level log granularity is available for this stage; False when only full-stage logs exist), and `provider_extensions` (dict[str, Any]).
+
+#### Scenario: Stages written as flat array
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor produces unified files for any CI system
+- **THEN** a `stages.json` file SHALL be written containing a flat JSON array of all Stage objects in the pipeline run, with hierarchy expressed via `parent_stage_id` references
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins parallel branches represented
+- **WHEN** a Jenkins pipeline has a STAGE node whose edges point to `type="PARALLEL"` child nodes (the individual branches)
+- **THEN** each PARALLEL branch SHALL appear as a separate Stage with `type="parallel"` and `parent_stage_id` pointing to the parent STAGE that contains the parallel execution
+
+#### Scenario: Stages that did not execute are marked
+- **WHEN** a stage was skipped due to upstream failure (Jenkins failure propagation, Azure DevOps conditional, GitHub Actions if/needs)
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `ran=false` and `result="SKIPPED"`
+
+#### Scenario: depends_on populated for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor encounters a Job with `dependsOn: ["JobA", "JobB"]` in the timeline
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `depends_on: ["<stage_id_of_JobA>", "<stage_id_of_JobB>"]` referencing stage IDs (not display names)
+
+#### Scenario: depends_on populated for GitHub Actions
+- **WHEN** the GitHub Actions extractor encounters a job with `needs: [build, lint]`
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `depends_on` referencing the stage IDs of the `build` and `lint` jobs
+
+#### Scenario: depends_on for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor encounters a pipeline tree with no explicit dependency declarations
+- **THEN** `depends_on` SHALL be an empty list (Jenkins models dependencies implicitly via tree structure and `parent_stage_id`)
+- **AND** if a Jenkins pipeline uses `waitUntil` or other explicit ordering constructs, those MAY be represented in `depends_on` if detectable from the API
+
+#### Scenario: Retry tracking for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor encounters a stage with `previousAttempts: [{timelineId: "...", attempt: 1}]`
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `attempt=2`, `total_attempts=2`, and `has_prior_failure=true`
+- **AND** the prior attempt's timeline data SHALL be available in `provider_raw/azure_devops/attempts/`
+
+#### Scenario: Retry tracking for GitHub Actions
+- **WHEN** the GitHub Actions extractor captures a workflow run with `run_attempt=3`
+- **THEN** all Stage entries SHALL have `attempt=3` and `total_attempts=3` (GHA retries the entire workflow)
+- **AND** `has_prior_failure` SHALL be `true` for all stages (since a prior attempt of the workflow failed)
+
+#### Scenario: No retry for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor writes stages (Jenkins has no native retry mechanism)
+- **THEN** `attempt` SHALL be `1`, `total_attempts` SHALL be `1`, and `has_prior_failure` SHALL be `false`
+
+#### Scenario: Infrastructure failure classified for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor detects an agent disconnect (job result=failed but all child tasks show succeeded)
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `failure_category="infrastructure"`
+
+#### Scenario: Code failure classified
+- **WHEN** a stage fails due to a test failure, compilation error, or script exit code
+- **THEN** the extractor MAY set `failure_category="code"` when the signal is unambiguous
+- **AND** SHALL set `failure_category=None` (not classified) when uncertain — prefer no label over a wrong label
+
+#### Scenario: has_step_logs for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes a Stage whose timeline tasks each have a `log.id` reference
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `has_step_logs=true`
+
+#### Scenario: has_step_logs for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor has captured per-step log files from Blue Ocean (steps API available)
+- **THEN** the Stage SHALL have `has_step_logs=true`
+- **AND** when only the full stage `wfapi/log` was captured (no step granularity), `has_step_logs` SHALL be `false`
+
+#### Scenario: has_step_logs guides consumer behavior
+- **WHEN** a consumer wants to call `stream_log(uuid, stage_id, step_id=X)`
+- **THEN** it SHALL first check `has_step_logs` on the stage — if `false`, the consumer knows only `stream_log(uuid, stage_id)` (full stage log) is available
+
+### Requirement: Unified parallel execution group model
+The system SHALL define a `ParallelGroup` Pydantic model representing a set of stages/jobs that executed concurrently within a parent stage. This model normalizes Jenkins parallel branches and Azure DevOps matrix expansions into a single structure so consumers can discover and iterate concurrent execution without CI-specific logic. Fields SHALL include: `id` (str — unique identifier for the group; for Jenkins this is the parent STAGE node's id since no separate container exists; for ADO this is a synthetic id like `"{stage_id}__parallel"`), `display_name` (str — human-readable name; for Jenkins this is the parent STAGE node's `displayName`), `parent_stage_id` (str — the stage that contains this parallel group; for Jenkins this equals `id` since the parent stage IS the group container), `strategy` (Literal["parallel_branches", "matrix", "multi_job"] — how concurrency was declared), `legs` (list[ParallelLeg]).
+
+The `ParallelLeg` model SHALL include: `stage_id` (str — references the leg's entry in `stages.json`), `display_name` (str), `result` (unified Status), `matrix_values` (dict[str, str] | None — e.g., `{"os": "linux", "node": "16"}` for matrix legs; `None` for Jenkins branches).
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins parallel branches produce a ParallelGroup
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor encounters a STAGE node whose edges point to nodes with `type="PARALLEL"` (i.e., the stage contains parallel branches)
+- **THEN** it SHALL write a `ParallelGroup` entry with:
+  - `id`: the parent STAGE node's id (the stage that contains the parallel execution — e.g., `"417"` for a "Build and validate" stage; there is no single PARALLEL container node in the Blue Ocean API)
+  - `strategy`: `"parallel_branches"`
+  - `legs`: one `ParallelLeg` per `type="PARALLEL"` child node
+  - `matrix_values`: `None` on all legs (Jenkins branches are named, not matrix-generated)
+
+> **Implementation note:** The Blue Ocean `/nodes/` API uses `FlowNodeWrapper.NodeType` enum `{STAGE, PARALLEL, STEP}`. A `parallel {}` block in a Jenkinsfile does NOT produce a PARALLEL container node. Instead, the enclosing STAGE node has edges pointing to the individual branch nodes, each with `type="PARALLEL"` and `firstParent` referencing the parent STAGE. Detection: find STAGE nodes where any outbound edge targets a `type="PARALLEL"` node. The post-parallel convergent stage may have `firstParent=null` rather than referencing the container — implementers must infer parentage from the parallel branch edges, not solely from `firstParent` links.
+>
+> **Verified against:** haas-jenkins-lib / develop / run #62 — STAGE id=417 "Build and validate" has edges `[{id:"420", type:"PARALLEL"}, {id:"421", type:"PARALLEL"}]`. Nodes 420 and 421 each have `firstParent="417"`. The convergent STAGE id=438 "Build" has `firstParent=null`.
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps matrix expansion produces a ParallelGroup
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor encounters a Stage containing multiple Job records that share no `dependsOn` relationship with each other (i.e., they run concurrently)
+- **THEN** it SHALL write a `ParallelGroup` entry with:
+  - `id`: a synthetic id derived from the parent stage (e.g., `"{stage_id}__parallel"`)
+  - `strategy`: `"matrix"` (when jobs have `matrix_leg` set) or `"multi_job"` (when jobs are explicitly declared, not matrix-generated)
+  - `legs`: one `ParallelLeg` per concurrent Job
+  - `matrix_values`: populated from the job's matrix configuration (e.g., `{"os": "ubuntu-latest"}`) when available
+
+#### Scenario: Sequential ADO jobs do NOT produce a ParallelGroup
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor encounters Jobs within a Stage that have `dependsOn` relationships making them sequential
+- **THEN** no `ParallelGroup` SHALL be written for that stage — sequential jobs are not parallel execution
+
+#### Scenario: ParallelGroups written to dedicated file
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor produces unified files and the pipeline contains any parallel execution
+- **THEN** a `parallel_groups.json` file SHALL be written containing a JSON array of all `ParallelGroup` objects
+- **AND** if no parallel execution exists, the file SHALL contain an empty array
+
+#### Scenario: Reader method for parallel groups
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `reader.get_parallel_groups(inspection_id)`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL return `list[ParallelGroup]` if `parallel_groups.json` exists, or an empty list if absent
+- **AND** consumers SHALL use this method instead of manually scanning `stages.json` for concurrent siblings
+
+### Requirement: Unified Step model
+The system SHALL define a `Step` Pydantic model representing one step/task within a stage. Fields SHALL include: `id` (string), `display_name` (string), `display_description` (str | None), `result` (unified Status), `state` (unified State), `duration_ms` (int | None), `start_time` (ISO 8601 UTC | None), `log_ref` (str | None — relative path to log content in HDLF), `job_id` (str | None — references the parent job's stage id from `stages.json`), `is_conditional_failure` (bool — True when this step failed but did NOT cause its parent job/stage to fail; indicates an optional/notification step whose failure is tolerated; default False), and `provider_extensions` (dict[str, Any]).
+
+#### Scenario: Steps stored per stage
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor produces unified files
+- **THEN** a `steps/<stage_id>.json` file SHALL be written for each stage that has step-level data, containing a JSON array of Step objects
+
+#### Scenario: job_id populated for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes steps for a Job-type stage
+- **THEN** each Step SHALL have `job_id` set to the id of the Job stage entry in `stages.json` that contains it
+- **AND** the referenced Job stage SHALL have `type="job"` in `stages.json`
+
+#### Scenario: job_id is null for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor writes steps
+- **THEN** `job_id` SHALL be `null` (Jenkins stages run steps directly without an intermediate job layer)
+
+#### Scenario: Stages without step-level detail
+- **WHEN** a CI system does not provide step-level granularity for a stage (e.g., some Azure DevOps tasks)
+- **THEN** no `steps/<stage_id>.json` file SHALL be created for that stage, and consumers SHALL treat missing files as "no step data available"
+
+#### Scenario: is_conditional_failure detected for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor encounters a Task that has `result=failed` but its parent Job has `result=succeeded` or `result=succeededWithIssues`
+- **THEN** the Step SHALL have `is_conditional_failure=true`
+- **AND** this indicates the task ran under `continueOnError: true` or a condition like `condition: always()` that allows it to fail without blocking the pipeline
+
+#### Scenario: is_conditional_failure for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor writes steps
+- **THEN** `is_conditional_failure` SHALL default to `false` (Jenkins does not have native conditional failure semantics at the step level; a failed step always fails the stage)
+
+#### Scenario: is_conditional_failure guides handler logic
+- **WHEN** a handler filters steps to find "blocking failures"
+- **THEN** it SHALL exclude steps where `is_conditional_failure=true` — these represent optional/notification tasks (e.g., Slack notification, optional code scan) whose failure does not indicate a pipeline problem worth investigating
+
+### Requirement: Azure DevOps Job detail model
+The system SHALL define a `JobDetail` Pydantic model containing rich job-level metadata specific to Azure DevOps. This model is stored in a dedicated file and is optional — absent for CI systems without a job layer. Fields SHALL include: `id` (str — matches the job's stage id in `stages.json`), `display_name` (str), `agent_name` (str | None — worker that executed the job), `agent_pool` (str | None — pool the agent belongs to), `continue_on_error` (bool — whether job failures are tolerated by downstream dependents), `depends_on` (list[str] — ids of job stages this job depends on), `timeout_minutes` (int | None), `cancel_timeout_minutes` (int | None), and `matrix_leg` (str | None — matrix configuration name if this job is part of a matrix expansion).
+
+#### Scenario: Job details written for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor produces unified files
+- **THEN** a `jobs.json` file SHALL be written to the inspection folder containing a JSON array of `JobDetail` objects, one per Job-type timeline record
+
+#### Scenario: Job details absent for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor produces unified files
+- **THEN** `jobs.json` SHALL be written as an empty JSON array (Jenkins has no job layer)
+
+#### Scenario: Reader method returns job details if present
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `reader.get_jobs(inspection_id)`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL return `list[JobDetail]` if `jobs.json` exists, or an empty list if the file is absent
+- **AND** consumers SHALL NOT treat absence as an error — it means the CI system has no job-level metadata
+
+#### Scenario: jobs.json is a required unified output
+- **WHEN** a unified-format inspection is completed
+- **THEN** `jobs.json` SHALL exist before `manifest.json` is written
+- **AND** the file SHALL contain Azure DevOps JobDetail objects for Azure DevOps inspections, or `[]` for Jenkins inspections
+
+### Requirement: Unified Artifact model
+The system SHALL define an `Artifact` Pydantic model representing one build artifact. Fields SHALL include: `file_name` (string), `relative_path` (string — path within artifacts/), `mime_type` (str | None — inferred from extension), `size_bytes` (int | None), `producing_stage_id` (str | None — confident stage/job attribution), `producing_step_id` (str | None — the specific step/task that published this artifact), `candidate_stage_ids` (list[str] | None — ambiguous attribution when confident match impossible), `attribution_method` (Literal["api_source", "archive_step_node", "naming_heuristic", "mtime_correlation"] | None — how attribution was determined), `attribution_confidence` (Literal["high", "medium", "low"] | None), and `download_url` (str | None — original CI system URL).
+
+#### Scenario: Artifacts listed in unified format
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor captures artifacts from any CI system
+- **THEN** an `artifacts.json` file SHALL be written containing a JSON array of all Artifact objects with normalized paths
+
+#### Scenario: Stage attribution preserved
+- **WHEN** the CI system provides information about which stage produced an artifact
+- **THEN** the Artifact SHALL have `producing_stage_id` set when attribution is confident, or `candidate_stage_ids` when ambiguous
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps artifact attribution (high confidence)
+- **GIVEN** an Azure DevOps artifact with `source` field set (the producing job's timeline record ID)
+- **WHEN** building `artifacts.json`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL:
+  1. Look up the `source` value in the timeline to find the Job record
+  2. Set `producing_stage_id` to that job's entry ID in `stages.json`
+  3. Scan `steps/<job_stage_id>.json` for a step whose task name contains `PublishBuildArtifacts` or `PublishPipelineArtifact` — set `producing_step_id` to that step's ID
+  4. Set `attribution_method = "api_source"` and `attribution_confidence = "high"`
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins artifact attribution via archive step node (medium-high confidence)
+- **GIVEN** a Jenkins inspection with step data available
+- **WHEN** building `artifacts.json`
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL:
+  1. Scan all `steps/<stage_id>.json` files for steps with `displayName == "Archive the artifacts"`
+  2. For each archive step, extract the true owning node ID from the step's log href (`/nodes/{node_id}/steps/{step_id}/log/`)
+  3. The owning node ID identifies the stage that produced those artifacts
+  4. If exactly one archive step's node matches a stage → set `producing_stage_id` to that stage, `producing_step_id` to the archive step ID, `attribution_method = "archive_step_node"`, `attribution_confidence = "high"`
+  5. If multiple archive steps exist in the same stage → set `producing_stage_id` to the stage, `candidate_stage_ids = None`, `attribution_method = "archive_step_node"`, `attribution_confidence = "medium"` (stage known but specific step ambiguous)
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins artifact attribution via naming heuristic (medium confidence)
+- **GIVEN** an artifact whose directory name embeds stage coordinates (e.g., `checkmarx_one_scan_Main_CCA_Starter_1_0_5548_0c364e77/`)
+- **WHEN** the archive-step approach yields multiple candidate stages
+- **THEN** the extractor MAY correlate artifact subdirectory naming patterns with stage display names to narrow attribution
+- **AND** SHALL set `attribution_method = "naming_heuristic"` and `attribution_confidence = "medium"`
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins artifact attribution via mtime correlation (low confidence)
+- **GIVEN** an artifact where archive-step and naming approaches fail
+- **WHEN** artifact file mtime falls within exactly one stage's `[start_time, end_time]` window
+- **THEN** the extractor MAY set `producing_stage_id` to that stage
+- **AND** SHALL set `attribution_method = "mtime_correlation"` and `attribution_confidence = "low"`
+
+#### Scenario: Unattributable artifact
+- **WHEN** no attribution method produces a result
+- **THEN** `producing_stage_id`, `producing_step_id`, and `candidate_stage_ids` SHALL all be `None`
+- **AND** `attribution_method` and `attribution_confidence` SHALL be `None`
+
+### Requirement: Unified Status enum
+The system SHALL define a `PipelineStatus` enum with exactly these values: `SUCCESS`, `FAILURE`, `UNSTABLE`, `ABORTED`, `SKIPPED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `QUEUED`, `UNKNOWN`. Every provider extractor SHALL map its native status vocabulary to this enum at write-time. No other status values SHALL appear in unified schema files.
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins status mapping
+- **WHEN** a Jenkins build reports Blue Ocean result "NOT_BUILT"
+- **THEN** the unified status SHALL be "SKIPPED"
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps status mapping
+- **WHEN** an Azure DevOps pipeline reports result "partiallySucceeded"
+- **THEN** the unified status SHALL be "UNSTABLE"
+
+#### Scenario: Unknown status handling
+- **WHEN** a CI system reports a status value not in the known mapping table
+- **THEN** the unified status SHALL be "UNKNOWN" and the original value SHALL be preserved in `provider_extensions`
+
+### Requirement: Timestamps normalized to UTC
+All timestamp fields in unified schema files SHALL be ISO 8601 format in UTC (suffix `Z`). Provider extractors SHALL convert from provider-local timezones at write-time. Null timestamps SHALL remain null (not empty string).
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins timezone conversion
+- **WHEN** Jenkins Blue Ocean returns a timestamp with offset (e.g., "2026-06-30T16:30:00.000+0200")
+- **THEN** the unified file SHALL contain "2026-06-30T14:30:00Z"
+
+#### Scenario: Missing timestamps preserved as null
+- **WHEN** a CI system does not provide a start_time for a stage
+- **THEN** the `start_time` field SHALL be `null` in the JSON output, not omitted or empty string
+
+### Requirement: Log line numbering normalized to 1-indexed
+All stored log files in the inspection folder (`logs/`) SHALL use 1-indexed line numbering. Provider extractors SHALL normalize at write-time so that consumers never encounter CI-system-specific line numbering conventions.
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps logs normalized from 0-indexed
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes log files from the Logs API (which uses 0-indexed line numbers)
+- **THEN** stored log content SHALL be plain text with lines numbered starting at 1 (matching standard text editor conventions)
+- **AND** any line-number references in issues files or error messages SHALL reference the 1-indexed position in the stored log
+
+#### Scenario: GitHub Actions log timestamps stripped
+- **WHEN** the GitHub Actions extractor writes log files from the log ZIP (where each line is prefixed with a timestamp like `2026-07-06T14:30:00.000Z `)
+- **THEN** the timestamp prefix SHALL be stripped from stored log content
+- **AND** timestamps MAY be preserved in a separate metadata structure if needed for correlation, but SHALL NOT appear inline in the log text that consumers read
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins logs stored as-is
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor writes log content from Blue Ocean step logs or WFAPI stage logs
+- **THEN** the content SHALL be stored as-is (Jenkins logs are already 1-indexed plain text without metadata prefixes)
+
+### Requirement: UNSTABLE status confidence and inference rules
+
+The `UNSTABLE` status SHALL represent "partially succeeded" — typically a build that completed but has test failures or quality gate warnings. Its reliability varies by CI system because not all providers have a native equivalent.
+
+#### Scenario: UNSTABLE is authoritative for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** a Jenkins build reports result "UNSTABLE"
+- **THEN** the unified result SHALL be "UNSTABLE" with `result_confidence="authoritative"`
+- **AND** this is reliable — Jenkins natively distinguishes UNSTABLE from FAILURE at the build and stage level
+
+#### Scenario: UNSTABLE is authoritative for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** an Azure DevOps pipeline reports result "partiallySucceeded"
+- **THEN** the unified result SHALL be "UNSTABLE" with `result_confidence="authoritative"`
+- **AND** this maps directly from the ADO result enum
+
+#### Scenario: UNSTABLE is inferred for GitHub Actions
+- **WHEN** a GitHub Actions workflow has `conclusion=failure` but test result analysis shows mixed pass/fail (some tests passed, some failed)
+- **THEN** the extractor MAY set result to "UNSTABLE" with `result_confidence="inferred"`
+- **AND** consumers SHALL understand that GHA has no native UNSTABLE concept — the classification is heuristic
+- **AND** if the extractor cannot determine test outcomes (no JUnit artifacts found), the result SHALL remain "FAILURE" with `result_confidence="authoritative"`
+
+#### Scenario: result_confidence defaults
+- **WHEN** a CI system's native API directly provides the status value used for mapping
+- **THEN** `result_confidence` SHALL be `"authoritative"` (or `None`, which consumers SHALL treat as authoritative)
+- **AND** `result_confidence="inferred"` SHALL only be set when the status was derived from heuristics or multi-signal analysis rather than a single API field
+
+### Requirement: Known provider_extensions keys
+
+The `provider_extensions` field on PipelineRun, Stage, and Step is a `dict[str, Any]` that holds CI-system-specific metadata not promoted to first-class fields. To prevent it from being an undocumented junk drawer, the following keys SHALL be documented with their types and availability:
+
+#### PipelineRun provider_extensions
+
+| Key | Type | CI System | Description |
+|-----|------|-----------|-------------|
+| `definition_id` | `int` | Azure DevOps | Pipeline definition (template) ID |
+| `pool_name` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Default agent pool for the pipeline |
+| `workflow_id` | `int` | GitHub Actions | Workflow file ID |
+| `triggering_actor` | `str` | GitHub Actions | User/bot that triggered the run |
+| `run_attempt` | `int` | GitHub Actions | Raw attempt number (also reflected in Stage.attempt) |
+| `exec_node` | `str` | Jenkins | Build agent label that executed the pipeline |
+| `pause_duration_ms` | `int` | Jenkins | Total time the pipeline spent in input/approval pauses |
+
+#### Stage provider_extensions
+
+| Key | Type | CI System | Description |
+|-----|------|-----------|-------------|
+| `agent_pool` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Pool this job ran on (when different from pipeline default) |
+| `agent_name` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Specific agent that executed this job |
+| `inferred_continue_on_error` | `bool` | Azure DevOps | Whether this job/stage tolerates downstream failures |
+| `original_status` | `str` | All | The CI system's native status string when mapped to UNKNOWN |
+
+#### Step provider_extensions
+
+| Key | Type | CI System | Description |
+|-----|------|-----------|-------------|
+| `task_reference_name` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Task's `refName` for output variable references |
+| `task_version` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Task version (e.g., "2.198.0") |
+| `condition` | `str` | Azure DevOps | Raw condition expression (e.g., "succeededOrFailed()") |
+
+#### Scenario: Consumers can depend on documented extension keys
+- **WHEN** a consumer needs CI-specific metadata (e.g., which agent ran a job)
+- **THEN** it SHALL access the documented key (e.g., `stage.provider_extensions.get("agent_name")`) knowing the type and which CI system populates it
+- **AND** SHALL handle `None`/absent gracefully for inspections from other CI systems
+
+#### Scenario: Undocumented keys are allowed but not depended upon
+- **WHEN** an extractor stores additional CI-specific fields not listed above
+- **THEN** it MAY add them to `provider_extensions` freely
+- **AND** consumers SHALL NOT depend on undocumented keys without first adding them to this table
+
+### Requirement: Stage type documentation
+
+The `type` field on Stage SHALL use `Literal["stage", "parallel", "job", "phase"]` where each value has CI-system-specific applicability. This is by design — the type reflects real structural differences — but consumers must understand the distribution.
+
+| Type value | Jenkins | Azure DevOps | GitHub Actions | Meaning |
+|------------|---------|--------------|----------------|---------|
+| `"stage"` | ✓ Always | ✓ Always | ✓ Always | Top-level pipeline stage (universal) |
+| `"parallel"` | ✓ (PARALLEL nodes) | ✗ Never | ✗ Never | Individual branch within a parallel execution block (parent STAGE holds the group) |
+| `"job"` | ✗ Never | ✓ (Job timeline records) | ✓ (workflow jobs) | Intermediate execution unit between stage and steps |
+| `"phase"` | ✗ Never | ✓ (Phase/Checkpoint records) | ✗ Never | ADO-specific approval/checkpoint grouping |
+
+#### Scenario: Consumer filtering by type accounts for CI differences
+- **WHEN** a consumer filters `stages.json` entries by type
+- **THEN** it SHALL understand that `type="job"` entries only appear for ADO and GHA inspections
+- **AND** `type="parallel"` only appears for Jenkins inspections
+- **AND** universal logic SHALL filter on `type="stage"` which is present across all CI systems
+
+### Requirement: Stage failure_messages field
+
+The unified `Stage` model SHALL include a `failure_messages: list[str] | None` field that contains human-readable error messages associated with the stage's failure. This field promotes the most commonly-needed failure information to the unified model so consumers don't need to access provider_raw.
+
+#### Scenario: failure_messages populated for Azure DevOps
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes a Stage whose timeline records contain `issues[]` entries with `type: "error"`
+- **THEN** `failure_messages` SHALL contain the `message` string from each error issue, deduplicated
+- **AND** the ordering SHALL preserve the timeline record order (earlier tasks first)
+
+#### Scenario: failure_messages populated for Jenkins
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins normalizer processes a failed stage and derives issues from log parsing
+- **THEN** `failure_messages` SHALL contain the deduplicated `message` strings from error issue items in `provider_raw/jenkins/issues/<stage_id>.json`
+- **AND** Jenkins issue files SHALL use the same plain-array issue item schema as Azure DevOps issue files, with `category=null` unless Jenkins extraction has a defensible category signal
+- **AND** if no issues file exists for the stage (log parsing found nothing), `failure_messages` SHALL be `null` or `[]`
+
+#### Scenario: failure_messages is the consumer-facing issue normalization
+- **WHEN** a CI-agnostic consumer reads stage failure information
+- **THEN** it SHALL use `Stage.failure_messages` from `stages.json`
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT read provider issue files under `provider_raw/` for normal analysis
+- **AND** provider issue files SHALL remain available only for debugging, provenance, CI-specific enrichment, and backfill workflows
+
+#### Scenario: failure_messages is empty for stages without errors
+- **WHEN** a stage has `result` in (SUCCESS, SKIPPED, QUEUED, IN_PROGRESS)
+- **THEN** `failure_messages` SHALL be `null` or `[]`
+- **AND** consumers SHALL treat both values as equivalent: no failure messages are available
+
+#### Scenario: failure_messages used by handler orchestration
+- **WHEN** the handler orchestrator persists FailedStage DB rows
+- **THEN** it MAY use `failure_messages[0]` as the `error_message` column value only when `failure_messages` is non-empty
+- **AND** handlers accessing stage data via MCP tools SHALL use `failure_messages` for programmatic error analysis instead of log parsing
+
+### Requirement: Unified metadata model (metadata.json schema)
+
+The `metadata.json` file SHALL be CI-agnostic and serve as the universal entry point for inspection identification. The schema extends the existing format with additive fields — no existing fields are removed or renamed.
+
+#### Fields
+
+The metadata.json SHALL include at minimum:
+- `ci_system: Literal["jenkins", "azure_devops", "github_actions"]` — canonical CI system identifier (snake_case)
+- `pipeline_url: str` — canonical URL of the pipeline run (works for all CI systems)
+- `run_identifier: str | None` — human-readable run identifier (same value as PipelineRun.run_identifier)
+- `commit_id: str | None`
+- `branch: str | None`
+- `is_pr: bool`
+- `pr_number: int | None`
+- `pr_url: str | None`
+- `pr_title: str | None`
+- `pr_author: str | None`
+- `start_time: str | None` (ISO 8601 UTC)
+- `end_time: str | None` (ISO 8601 UTC)
+- `duration_ms: int | None`
+- `result: str | None` (unified PipelineStatus value)
+- `git_repos: list[GitRepo]`
+- `parameters: dict[str, Any]`
+- `extracted_at: str` (ISO 8601 UTC timestamp of extraction)
+
+**Retained Jenkins-specific fields (backward compat):**
+- `jenkins_url: str | None` — populated for Jenkins inspections (same value as `pipeline_url`); `null` for ADO/GHA
+- `build_number: int | None` — populated for Jenkins inspections; `null` for ADO/GHA
+- `changesets: list[ChangesetItem] | None` — populated for Jenkins inspections; `null` for ADO/GHA
+
+#### Scenario: metadata.json produced for Jenkins inspection
+- **WHEN** the metadata extractor processes a Jenkins pipeline URL
+- **THEN** metadata.json SHALL include both `jenkins_url` (backward compat) and `pipeline_url` (same value), with `ci_system="jenkins"`
+
+#### Scenario: metadata.json produced for Azure DevOps inspection
+- **WHEN** the metadata extractor processes an Azure DevOps pipeline URL
+- **THEN** metadata.json SHALL include `ci_system="azure_devops"`, `pipeline_url` set to the canonical ADO URL, and `jenkins_url=null`
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy metadata.json read by LegacyJenkinsReader
+- **WHEN** the `LegacyJenkinsReader` reads a metadata.json that lacks the `ci_system` field
+- **THEN** it SHALL infer `ci_system="jenkins"` and synthesize `pipeline_url` from `jenkins_url`
+
+#### Scenario: Reader factory uses metadata.json to confirm CI system
+- **WHEN** `create_reader()` selects the `UnifiedInspectionReader`
+- **THEN** it SHALL verify that metadata.json contains a valid `ci_system` field
+- **AND** if the field is missing in a unified-format inspection, it SHALL raise `MalformedInspectionDataError`
+
+### Requirement: Per-step log storage path convention
+
+Step-level log files SHALL be stored in a nested directory structure under `logs/`:
+
+```
+logs/
+├── console.log                    (full build log)
+├── <stage_id>.log                 (full stage log — always present for stages with captured logs)
+└── <stage_id>/
+    ├── <step_id_1>.log            (per-step log file)
+    └── <step_id_2>.log
+```
+
+The Step model's `log_ref` field SHALL contain the relative path from the inspection root (e.g., `logs/42/7.log`).
+
+#### Scenario: stream_log resolves step-level log
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `stream_log(uuid, stage_id="42", step_id="7")`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL stream bytes from `<uuid>/logs/42/7.log`
+- **AND** if the file does not exist, raise `InspectionFileNotFoundError`
+
+#### Scenario: stream_log falls back to stage log
+- **WHEN** a consumer calls `stream_log(uuid, stage_id="42", step_id=None)`
+- **THEN** the reader SHALL stream bytes from `<uuid>/logs/42.log`
+
+#### Scenario: Per-step logs only written when available
+- **WHEN** a CI system provides step-level log content (ADO task logs, Jenkins Blue Ocean step logs)
+- **THEN** the extractor SHALL write per-step log files to `logs/<stage_id>/<step_id>.log`
+- **AND** the stage SHALL have `has_step_logs=true`
+- **AND** if step logs are NOT available (only full stage log captured), the `logs/<stage_id>/` directory SHALL NOT exist
+
+#### Scenario: log_ref populated on Step model
+- **WHEN** the extractor writes a per-step log file
+- **THEN** the corresponding Step entry in `steps/<stage_id>.json` SHALL have `log_ref` set to the relative path (e.g., `logs/42/7.log`)
+- **AND** if no log was captured for the step, `log_ref` SHALL be `null`
+
+### Requirement: Unified ExecutionTimeline model
+
+The system SHALL define an `ExecutionTimeline` Pydantic model representing the complete derived execution view for one inspection. The model SHALL include: `schema_version` (string, default `"1.0"`), `pipeline_run` (`PipelineRun`), `stages` (list of `Stage`), `steps_by_stage` (dict keyed by stage id, values are lists of `Step`), `jobs` (list of `JobDetail`), `parallel_groups` (list of `ParallelGroup`), `artifacts` (list of `Artifact`), and `provider_extensions` (dict[str, Any]).
+
+The `ExecutionTimeline` model SHALL reuse existing unified model shapes directly. It SHALL NOT introduce a separate flattened node schema unless a future spec adds a specific consumer requirement for such a graph.
+
+#### Scenario: Execution timeline written for new unified inspections
+- **WHEN** the Data Extractor produces a new unified-format inspection for Jenkins, Azure DevOps, or GitHub Actions
+- **THEN** it SHALL write `execution_timeline.json` at the inspection root before `manifest.json` is written
+- **AND** the file SHALL validate as an `ExecutionTimeline` object
+- **AND** the file SHALL be listed in `manifest.json` with `source="derived"`
+
+#### Scenario: Execution timeline is derived, not provider raw
+- **WHEN** the extractor writes `execution_timeline.json`
+- **THEN** it SHALL write the file at `<uuid>/execution_timeline.json`
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT write the file under `provider_raw/`
+- **AND** it SHALL NOT replace provider-native files such as `provider_raw/azure_devops/timeline.json`
+
+#### Scenario: Execution timeline aggregates existing unified files
+- **WHEN** a consumer reads `execution_timeline.json`
+- **THEN** the content SHALL be equivalent to reading `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, all present `steps/<stage_id>.json` files, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, and `artifacts.json`
+- **AND** `steps_by_stage` SHALL use the same stage ids used in the `steps/<stage_id>.json` file paths
+
+#### Scenario: Azure DevOps timeline records map into execution timeline
+- **WHEN** producing `execution_timeline.json` for Azure DevOps
+- **THEN** ADO timeline records SHALL follow the unified mapping rules: `Stage` records become `Stage(type="stage")`, `Phase` records become `Stage(type="phase")`, `Job` records become `Stage(type="job")`, and `Task` records become `Step` entries in `steps_by_stage`
+- **AND** ADO task `Step.job_id` SHALL reference the containing job-stage id
+
+#### Scenario: Jenkins pipeline nodes map into execution timeline
+- **WHEN** producing `execution_timeline.json` for Jenkins
+- **THEN** Blue Ocean `STAGE` nodes SHALL appear as `Stage(type="stage")`, Blue Ocean `PARALLEL` nodes SHALL appear as `Stage(type="parallel")`, and Blue Ocean stage steps SHALL appear as `Step` entries in `steps_by_stage`
+- **AND** Jenkins `Step.job_id` SHALL be `null`
+- **AND** `jobs` SHALL be an empty list
+
+### Requirement: Unified output validation before manifest write
+
+The Data Extractor SHALL validate required unified outputs before writing `manifest.json`. A validation failure SHALL abort extraction and SHALL leave no `manifest.json`, because manifest presence is the completion signal consumed by readers and handlers.
+
+Required top-level unified files SHALL be:
+- `execution_timeline.json` — JSON object
+- `pipeline_run.json` — JSON object
+- `stages.json` — JSON array
+- `artifacts.json` — JSON array
+- `jobs.json` — JSON array (`[]` for CI systems without a job layer)
+- `parallel_groups.json` — JSON array (`[]` when no parallel groups exist)
+
+Optional `steps/<stage_id>.json` files, when present, SHALL contain JSON arrays. Every non-null `Step.log_ref` SHALL begin with `logs/` and SHALL reference an existing normalized log object in the inspection folder. `log_ref` SHALL NOT point to `provider_raw/...` files.
+
+#### Scenario: Missing required file blocks completion
+- **WHEN** extraction has not produced `execution_timeline.json`
+- **THEN** unified output validation SHALL fail
+- **AND** `manifest.json` SHALL NOT be written
+
+#### Scenario: Missing normalized step log blocks completion
+- **WHEN** `steps/job-build.json` contains `log_ref="logs/job-build/task-publish.log"`
+- **AND** `logs/job-build/task-publish.log` does not exist
+- **THEN** unified output validation SHALL fail before `manifest.json` is written
+
+#### Scenario: provider_raw log_ref rejected
+- **WHEN** a Step entry uses `log_ref="provider_raw/azure_devops/logs/11.txt"`
+- **THEN** unified output validation SHALL fail because consumer-facing logs must live under normalized `logs/...`
+
+### Requirement: Manifest v2 records captured versus derived outputs
+
+The Data Extractor SHALL write a version 2 manifest at `<uuid>/manifest.json` after successful unified output validation. Each manifest item SHALL include `key`, `status`, and `source`, where `source` is one of:
+- `captured` — bytes downloaded from the CI provider or provider-native raw files copied under `provider_raw/<ci_system>/...`
+- `derived` — files generated by normalization, including top-level unified JSON and normalized `logs/...` files
+
+Every manifest item for a stored file SHALL include `captured_size`, regardless of whether `source` is `captured` or `derived`. Items skipped before storage SHALL NOT include `captured_size`; they MAY include `original_size` and an `error` diagnostic.
+
+#### Scenario: Provider raw entries are captured
+- **WHEN** the Jenkins extractor stores `provider_raw/jenkins/pipeline_tree.json`
+- **THEN** the manifest item for that key SHALL have `source="captured"` and a positive `captured_size`
+
+#### Scenario: Normalized log entries are derived
+- **WHEN** the Azure DevOps extractor writes `logs/job-build/task-publish.log`
+- **THEN** the manifest item for that key SHALL have `source="derived"` and a positive `captured_size`
+
+#### Scenario: Generated unified JSON entries are derived
+- **WHEN** the extractor writes `execution_timeline.json`, `pipeline_run.json`, `stages.json`, `jobs.json`, `parallel_groups.json`, or `artifacts.json`
+- **THEN** each corresponding manifest item SHALL have `source="derived"` and `captured_size`
+
+### Requirement: Canonical ci_system string values
+
+All components in the system SHALL use snake_case for `ci_system` values: `"jenkins"`, `"azure_devops"`, `"github_actions"`. These are the canonical identifiers used in:
+- `metadata.json` `ci_system` field
+- `PipelineRun.ci_system` field
+- `Inspection` DB table `ci_system` column
+- Temporal search attribute `SourceSystem`
+- FaultHandler CRD `applicability.ci_systems` array
+- URL validator `.name` property
+
+#### Scenario: Display-friendly names derived from canonical values
+- **WHEN** a UI or human-facing output needs a display name
+- **THEN** it SHALL use a lookup: `"jenkins"` → `"Jenkins"`, `"azure_devops"` → `"Azure DevOps"`, `"github_actions"` → `"GitHub Actions"`
+- **AND** SHALL NOT store display names in data models or APIs
+
+#### Scenario: Legacy code migration
+- **WHEN** existing code uses display-name style (e.g., `Literal["Jenkins", "Azure DevOps", "GitHub Actions"]`)
+- **THEN** it SHALL be migrated to use canonical snake_case values as part of the HDLF server migration task

+# Implementation Tasks: Unified CI-Agnostic Schema
+

+## 1. Unified Schema Models (`fl_shared/unified_models.py`)

+- [x] 1.5 Define `Stage` model — fields: id, display_name, type (stage/parallel/job/phase), result, state, result_confidence, duration_ms, start_time, end_time, parent_stage_id, depends_on, order, ran, attempt, total_attempts, has_prior_failure, failure_category, failure_messages, has_step_logs, provider_extensions
+- [x] 1.6 Define `Step` model — fields: id, display_name, display_description, result, state, duration_ms, start_time, log_ref, job_id, is_conditional_failure, provider_extensions
+- [x] 1.7 Define `Artifact` model — fields: file_name, relative_path, mime_type, size_bytes, producing_stage_id, producing_step_id, candidate_stage_ids, attribution_method, attribution_confidence, download_url
+- [x] 1.8 Define `JobDetail` model — fields: id, display_name, agent_name, agent_pool, continue_on_error, depends_on, timeout_minutes, cancel_timeout_minutes, matrix_leg
+- [x] 1.9 Define `ParallelGroup` model — fields: id, display_name, parent_stage_id, strategy (parallel_branches/matrix/multi_job), legs
+- [x] 1.10 Define `ParallelLeg` model — fields: stage_id, display_name, result, matrix_values
+- [x] 1.11 Define `ArtifactProvenance` model — fields: artifact_path, producing_stage_id, producing_step_id, candidate_stage_ids, attribution_method, attribution_confidence
+- [x] 1.12 Define status mapping dicts per provider + `map_status(ci_system, raw_value) -> PipelineStatus` helper

+- [x] 1.14 Define `ExecutionTimeline` model — fields: schema_version, pipeline_run, stages, steps_by_stage, jobs, parallel_groups, artifacts, provider_extensions
